 1                                     ORDINANCE NO. 2026-10-01

 2   An ordinance relating to land use; adopting an updated Growth Management Comprehensive
 3   Land Use Plan, zoning maps and zoning ordinances; providing for severability; providing an
 4   effective date; and requiring notice.

 5           WHEREAS, Clark County was required to review and, if needed, revise its comprehensive
 6   plan, in accordance with the goals and requirements of RCW 36.70A (the Growth Management
 7   Act, or GMA) by December 31, 2025; and

 8          WHEREAS, on August 13, 2026, the Growth Management Hearings Board adopted an
 9   amended compliance schedule for the county’s completion of the plan with a compliance
10   deadline of October 29, 2026; and

11          WHEREAS, the county's comprehensive plan is required to include maps and descriptive
12   text covering the objectives, principles and standards addressing the essential elements of the
13   plan required by RCW 36.70A.070 along with any additional elements and/or subarea plans
14   prepared pursuant to RCW 36.70A.080; and

15          WHEREAS, GMA directs counties to adopt urban growth areas (areas within which urban
16   growth is encouraged and outside of which only non-urban growth can occur) (“UGAs”) and to
17   address these areas in the countywide planning policies; and

18          WHEREAS, RCW 36.70A.060 and .170 mandate that the county designate, where
19   appropriate, critical areas and agricultural, forest, and mineral resources lands, and adopt
20   development regulations to assure the conservation of such lands and areas; and

21          WHEREAS, GMA mandates that there be early and continuous public involvement, and
22   the county adopted the Clark County Public Participation Plan by Resolution 2023-03-05; and

23            WHEREAS, the county is required under Chapter 43.21C RCW to evaluate environmental
24   impacts that could result from actions it approves or undertakes. As part of the 2025
25   comprehensive plan update, the county established alternatives for study (Resolutions 2025-
26   01-11 and 2025-04-02), selected a preferred alternative (“Exhibit B 2026-0108 Preferred
27   Alternative Selection Table,” adopted April 28, 2026), and issued both a draft EIS (DEIS) and a
28   final EIS (FEIS) containing a detailed analysis of potential environmental impacts of the
29   proposed comprehensive plan and development revisions; and

30          WHEREAS, the county: (1) pursuant to RCW 36.70A.110, adopted a population
31   projection based on the range of estimates provided by OFM (Resolution 2023-05-03); and (2)
32   examined the boundaries of its UGAs to ensure that a 20-year land supply (out to 2045) to
33   accommodate population and job growth is available (Resolution 2024-05-05); and

34          WHEREAS, the county, pursuant to RCW 36.70A.215, submitted by June 30, 2022, to
35   Washington State Department of Commerce the 2022 Buildable Lands Report (Resolution 2022-
36   06-01), satisfying the GMA requirement to review and evaluate the adequacy of suitable
37   residential, commercial and industrial lands inside the Urban Growth Area for accommodating
38   projected 2045 population and employment growth during the 20-year GMA planning horizon; 1
 1   and

 2          WHEREAS, the county, pursuant to Washington State 2023 House Bill 1181 (HB 1181)
 3   codified in RCW 36.70A.020(14), RCW 36.70A.070(9), RCW 36.70A.095, and RCW 70A.45.120,
 4   adopted a public participation plan for the Climate Change and Resiliency Element (Resolution
 5   2023-11-14); and

 6          WHEREAS, pursuant to RCW 36.70A.096, on June 27, 2025 the county submitted a
 7   notice of intent to apply for Washington Department of Commerce approval of the greenhouse
 8   gas emissions reduction subelement; and

 9          WHEREAS, on August 8, 2025 the county submitted a draft greenhouse gas emissions
10   reduction subelement for Department of Commerce initial review; and

11          WHEREAS, on November 14, 2025 the county received initial feedback from the
12   Department of Commerce which did not include advice of any county actions necessary to
13   incorporate into the subelement to receive greenhouse gas emissions reduction subelement
14   approval; and

15           WHEREAS, the county, on March 17, 2022, adopted the Housing Options Study and
16   Action Plan (Resolution 2022-05-11), with implementation of middle housing and small single-
17   family strategies through housing code modifications adopted by approval of Ordinance 2024-
18   03-02 and Ordinance 2024-06-04; and

19           WHEREAS, the county, at the request of certain fire districts within the county,
20   adopted Ordinance 2024-02-03, providing for county code amendments that authorized fire
21   districts to request review of their capital facilities plans and approval of associated impact
22   fees; and

23           WHEREAS, copies of the county's proposed updated comprehensive plan were
24   submitted to the Washington Department of Commerce on July 24, 2026, more than sixty days
25   (60) prior to final adoption; and

26           WHEREAS, it is expected that the cities will complete the updates to their land use and
27   capital facilities plans to make them consistent with the county plan; and

28           WHEREAS, the county suspended the annual review comprehensive plan
29   amendment cycle from October 1, 2023, through September 30, 2025 (Resolution 2022-09-
30   03), from October 1, 2025, through September 30, 2026, (Resolution 2025-09-01) and from
31   October 1, 2026, through October 30, 2026 (Resolution 2026-09-01) to be consistent with
32   RCW 36.70A.130(2)(a); and

33          WHEREAS, the Clark County Council and the Clark County Planning Commission held
34   a duly advertised joint hearing on January 8, 2026 and continued to January 15, 2026 on
35   selection of a preferred alternative; and

36         WHEREAS, the Planning Commission held a duly-advertised public hearing on
37   August 20 and September 3, 2026, to deliberate and make its recommendation to the                 2
 1   County Council; and

 2           WHEREAS, the County Council held a duly-advertised public hearing on October 1,
 3   October 2, and October 5, 2026 to consider the Planning Commission recommendation and
 4   to deliberate; and

 5         WHEREAS, at the October 1, October 2, and October 5, 2026 public hearing the
 6   County Council directed the county to form a Housing Commission, form a Mineral
 7   Resources Commission, and commence work on a Public Benefit Rating System (PBRS);

 8   NOW THEREFORE BE IT ORDERED AND RESOLVED BY THE COUNTY COUNCIL OF CLARK COUNTY,
 9   STATE OF WASHINGTON, as follows:

10   I. FINDINGS

11   The Clark County Council (the Council) finds that all GMA prerequisites for the revision of the
12   county's comprehensive plan have been met and that the plan adopted herein achieves the
13   goals and satisfies the requirements of the GMA, as follows:

14      1. Compliance with RCW 36.70A.070: Mandatory Elements of the Comprehensive Plan. The
15         20-year Comprehensive Growth Management Plan (“Plan”) adopted herein includes all
16         of the following mandatory elements: Land Use, Housing, Capital Facilities and Utilities,
17         Rural and Natural Resources, Transportation, Economic Development, Parks, Recreation
18         and Open Space, Climate and Resiliency, and Shoreline Policies. In addition, the plan also
19         contains the following optional elements: Environment, Historic Preservation, Schools,
20         Community Design, Annexation, and Procedural Guidelines.

21      2. Compliance with Resource and Critical Areas Designations and Development Regulation
22         Requirements. The Plan includes maps designating agriculture, forest and mineral
23         resource lands in compliance with RCW 36.70A.170, and this ordinance adopts
24         development regulations in Clark County Code (CCC) Chapter 40.210, Resource and
25         Rural Districts, to adequately protect resource lands, and in CCC Chapter 40.4, Critical
26         Areas, in compliance with RCW 36.70A.060. Critical Areas and Shorelines regulations are
27         not being amended or affected in this plan update.

28              a. In early 2025, as part of the 2025 Comprehensive Plan Update, the Clark County
29                 Council directed staff to complete a county-wide agricultural resource lands
30                 study. The county, with help from consultants, completed an evaluation of land
31                 within the unincorporated areas, outside of the current urban growth areas. The
32                 general purpose of the study was to help inform the county’s decisions regarding
33                 the comprehensive plan update and ensure that the county’s agricultural
34                 resource lands designations comply with applicable county code and state
35                 statutes, specifically the Growth Management Act (GMA).
36              b. At the October 1-5, 2026, public hearing, the County Council considered Planning
37                 Commission recommendations for agricultural de-designation of certain
38                 properties in the preferred land use alternative and made findings in the record
39                 per RCW 36.70A.130(5), citing criteria found in WAC 365-190-050 and made the
40                 following decisions:                                                             3
41                      i. No. 5 – Camas UGA: Include de-designation of agricultural resource land
 1                       and UGA expansion to the east for housing (Nevin property). Motion to
 2                       APPROVE the Planning Commission recommendation to approve was
 3                       DENIED (resulting in NO de-designation of agricultural resource lands).
 4                 ii.   No. 6 - La Center UGA north: De-designate agricultural resource lands and
 5                       expand the UGA to the north for additional employment capacity. The
 6                       Council DENIED the Planning Commission recommendation to approve
 7                       the de-designation. In addition to making findings based on the WAC
 8                       criteria, the council also relied upon information in the record regarding
 9                       an interlocal agreement between the City of La Center and the Cowlitz
10                       Tribe involving the affected properties.
11                iii.   No. 7 – La Center UGA: De-designate agricultural resource lands and
12                       expand the UGA to the southwest for additional employment capacity.
13                       The Council APPROVED the Planning Commission recommendation to
14                       approve the de-designation as amended by the Council to exclude the
15                       southerly 20-acre equestrian parcel # 211287000 from de-designation. In
16                       the decision to exclude parcel # 211287000 from de-designation, the
17                       council relied in part on the current equestrian use of the site as a factor.
18                iv.    No. 8 – Ridgefield UGA: De-designate agricultural resource lands and
19                       expand the UGA to the north for additional employment and housing
20                       capacity. The Council APPROVED the Planning Commission
21                       recommendation to approve the de-designation. In addition to making
22                       findings based on the WAC criteria, the council also relied upon
23                       information in the record provided by the City of Ridgefield, including the
24                       Johnson Economics Study, to support the de-designation decision.
25                 v.    No. 9 – Ridgefield UGA: De-designate agricultural resource land and
26                       expand the UGA to the southwest for additional housing capacity (Maul
27                       property). The Council APPROVED the Planning Commission
28                       recommendation to approve the de-designation. In particular, the council
29                       relied upon the unique geographical constraints of that property to
30                       support the de-designation decision, noting that it is less than 20 acres in
31                       size and completely surrounded by other City of Ridgefield lands, making
32                       it wholly non-contiguous with other agricultural lands.

33

34   3. Public Participation. The public participation requirements of the GMA (RCW
35      36.70A.140) have been met through an extensive public involvement process that
36      included but is not limited to the following:
37      a. A Technical Advisory Committee comprised of planners from the cities, who met
38          monthly to discuss planning issues of a technical nature;
39      b. Four work sessions and two public hearings related to the Housing Options Study
40          and Action Plan;
41      c. Three rounds of public meetings (May 2024 (scoping); December 2024 (alternatives);
42          and January 2026 (preferred alternative)) on the environmental impact statement;
43      d. 33 work sessions and 6 public hearings before the Planning Commission throughout
44          the update process;
45      e. 36 work sessions and 17 public hearings before the Council throughout the update
46          process to discuss/adopt population and jobs targets, planning assumptions, the 4
 1           vacant and buildable lands model, EIS alternatives, and various other plan-related
 2           items. This included a joint hearing with the Planning Commission to discuss the
 3           preferred alternative.
 4      f.   Extensive use of the Clark County Community Planning website,
 5           https://www.clark.wa.gov/community-planning/2025-update,
 6           that included information on the GMA; an outline of the comprehensive plan update
 7           process; notices of meetings and hearings; maps of alternatives considered in the
 8           SEPA process; meeting summaries and issue discussions; the DEIS; and the FEIS. In
 9           addition, a dedicated email address was included to comment on the comprehensive
10           plan as well as an electronic comment form;
11      g.   Periodic email announcements sent to comprehensive plan e-mail lists
12           containing over 2,250 addresses;
13      h.   Periodic press releases on the status of the update process;
14      i.   Outreach to neighborhood associations and community groups;
15      j.   Several ‘Clark County Close-up’ segments on the update process on CVTV;
16      k.   Televised public hearings held by the Planning Commission and the County Council
17           on plan adoption.
18
19   4. State Environmental Policy Act (SEPA). For the 2025 comprehensive plan update, the
20      county prepared a full environmental impact statement (EIS) covering a maximum study
21      area. The county has complied with the environmental review process required by SEPA,
22      as follows:
23      a. A determination of significance was issued on May 15, 2024. (WAC 197-11-330 and
24          197-11-360)
25      b. A 21-day scoping period was held from May 15 to June 5, 2024 to invite agency,
26          affected tribes, and public to comment. Public input was provided at three in-person
27          public meetings, an online comment submittal webpage, through a web-accessible
28          open house-style presentation, via email and postal mail. (WAC 197-11-408, 197-11-
29          502, 197-11-792).
30      c. The County Council held a hearing and approved three alternatives to study in a
31          draft environmental impact statement (DEIS) on December 6, 2024. The County
32          Council adopted a resolution documenting the alternatives to study in the DEIS on
33          January 28, 2025 (Resolution 2025-01-11). The County Council amended Resolution
34          2025-01-11 on April 15, 2025 (Resolution 2025-04-02).
35      d. Three in-person open houses and one web-accessible self-paced open-house style
36          presentation was provided to explain the three proposed alternatives in May-June,
37          2025.
38      e. The DEIS analyzing three alternatives was issued on October 1, 2025 and a 60-day
39          comment period was held. (WAC 197-11-405, 197-11-440, 197-11-442, 197-11-455,
40          197-11-502, 197-11-510)
41      f. A joint Planning Commission and County Council hearing was held on January 8,
42          2026 and continued to January 15, 2026 on selection of a preferred alternative.
43      g. The Planning Commission continued deliberations and made a recommendation on a
44          preferred alternative on January 29, 2026.
45      h. The County Council held a public hearing on selection of a preferred alternative April
46          27 to 28, 2026 and decided on a preferred alternative.
47      i. A Final EIS (FEIS) was issued on July 24, 2026. The FEIS focused on a preferred
48          alternative, which subsequently became the proposal that was the subject of public 5
 1         hearings before the Planning Commission and the County Council. The FEIS also
 2         responded to comments received on the DEIS. (WAC 197-11-405, 197-11-440, 197-
 3         11-442, 197-11-460, 197-11-510, 197-11-560)
 4      j. An addendum to the FEIS was issued on August 12, 2026. (WAC 197-11-706)
 5      k. The FEIS was a major decision-support tool in the comprehensive plan update
 6         process. (RCW 43.21C.030(b), 43.21C.060).

 7   5. Amendment. The Comprehensive Plan provides adequate monitoring and amendment
 8      procedures.
 9      a. The Comprehensive Plan provides for a procedure to monitor internal and
10         inter­jurisdictional consistency.
11      b. The Comprehensive Plan contains provisions governing its amendment.
12         Amendments will not be considered more than once each year.
13      c. The Comprehensive Plan provides for an automatic comprehensive review of urban
14         growth areas at least once every ten years.

15   6. Record of Process.
16      a. The Clark County Planning Commission conducted a duly-advertised public meeting
17         concerning recommendations for adoption of the Comprehensive Plan on August 20
18         and September 3, 2026.
19      b. The County Council conducted a duly-advertised public meeting on the Planning
20         Commission recommendations and adoption of the Comprehensive Plan on October
21         1, October 2, and October 5, 2026.
22      c. All public hearings before the Planning Commission and the County Council included
23         opportunities for public comment.
24      d. All public hearings before the Planning Commission and the County Council were
25         transcribed, digitally-recorded, and televised locally on cable television. Written
26         transcripts, and video and audio recordings are on file.
27      e. Copies of all news releases, and other selected publications are on file.
28      f. Copies of all written correspondence received by the county are on file.

29   7. Internal Consistency. The Comprehensive Plan is internally consistent.
30      a. The policies within and among elements are complementary, not contradictory. Both
31          separately and together, they further the goals of the GMA.
32      b. The land use map represents a detailed analysis carried out in cooperation with
33          individual cities and the business and environmental communities, including
34          overburdened and vulnerable population groups. The analysis and draft land use
35          maps were subject to public scrutiny for several months as the analysis progressed.
36      c. The Comprehensive Plan contains policies, implementation measures, and
37          procedures which provide for its review and adjustment if internal conflicts are
38          discovered.

39   8. Individual Site-Specific Requests. Clark County accepted site-specific comprehensive
40      plan and zoning amendment requests through March 15, 2024. The county
41      considered individual site-specific requests for a change in designation and/or
42      zoning as part of the plan update and as part of the public hearing process, and
43      selected requests were included in the preferred alternative.
                                                                                             6
 1   9. Concurrency. The Comprehensive Plan meets the concurrency requirement of the GMA.
 2      The plan requires direct concurrency for transportation (implemented by the
 3      concurrency ordinance which specifies levels of service for identified corridors), for
 4      water and for sewer, and the plan includes six and twenty-year project lists for these.
 5      The plan also includes project lists for indirect concurrency elements (schools, parks, and
 6      stormwater).

 7   10. Inter- Jurisdictional Coordination. The Comprehensive Plan update was accomplished in
 8       coordination with the cities of Battle Ground, Camas, La Center, Ridgefield, Vancouver,
 9       Washougal, and Woodland, and the Town of Yacolt. The county formed a coordinating
10       committee that included each city planning director; that group met monthly
11       throughout the entire update process.

12      The Comprehensive Plan is consistent with adopted county-wide planning policies. The
13      Community Framework Plan introduces the relevant element chapters of the plan.
14      County-wide planning policies are included in each plan element.

15   11. Coordination with other plans.
16       a. The Comprehensive Plan is coordinated with those of neighboring jurisdictions.
17       b. Environmental documents were developed jointly by the county and the cities within
18          it, except that the City of Vancouver issued documents separately from the county.
19       c. Capital facilities plans were produced in cooperation with cities, C-Tran, service
20          providers, public safety agencies, school districts, and metropolitan area planning
21          agencies.
22       d. An updated parks, recreation and open space plan was adopted by the county in
23          2022 for the unincorporated portion of the county, including the Vancouver UGA.
24       e. Preliminary review of the Comprehensive Plan meets the Southwest Washington
25          Regional Transportation Council (RTC) coordination certification process, which
26          requires RTC to indicate that the County’s Transportation Element has demonstrated
27          consistency with the Regional Transportation Plan for Clark County (RTP, February
28          2024), reflects the guidelines and principles under RCW 47.80.026, and satisfy the
29          requirements of RCW 36.70A.070 (6).

30   12. Analysis of Potential Environmental Impacts. The county prepared a Final Environmental
31       Impact Statement (FEIS) for the 2025 Comprehensive Plan Update. The county issued
32       the Draft Environmental Impact Statement (DEIS) on October 1, 2025. The county issued
33       the FEIS on July 24 2026, with an Addendum on August 12, 2026. The DEIS and FEIS were
34       issued jointly for the comprehensive plans of the county and its cities and Town of
35       Yacolt, except for the City of Vancouver, which prepared its own, independent SEPA
36       documents. Affected jurisdictions and the public have had an opportunity to and did
37       comment on this analysis.

38   13. GMA Goals. The Comprehensive Plan addresses the goals of the GMA through its
39       policies and implementation measures. The GMA contains 15 goals listed as follows,
40       with corresponding policies of the plan noted. The plan is also in compliance with more
41       specific citations contained within the GMA. Plan goals and policies which achieve
42       compliance with the GMA in its entirety are also not limited to those cited below in
43       association with the 15 basic goals.                                                    7
 1   a. Urban Growth: Encourage development in urban areas where adequate public
 2      facilities and services exist or can be provided in an efficient manner.

 3      The Community Framework Plan contains policies that direct growth within the
 4      region into existing urban areas. The plan addresses this objective most directly
 5      through the goals and policies in the Land Use Element, as well as the adoption of
 6      new urban growth boundaries surrounding local cities. Supporting policies and
 7      implementation measures are contained throughout the document.

 8   b. Reduce Sprawl: Reduce the inappropriate conversion of undeveloped land into
 9      sprawling, low-density development.

10      The plan addresses this objective directly through the goals and policies in the Land
11      Use Element, as well as the adoption of new urban growth boundaries surrounding
12      local cities.

13   c. Transportation: Encourage efficient multimodal transportation systems that will
14      reduce greenhouse gas emissions and per capita vehicle miles traveled, and are
15      based on regional priorities and coordinated with county and city comprehensive
16      plans.
17
18      The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through the goals and policies
19      in the Transportation Element.
20
21   d. Housing: Plan for and accommodate housing affordable to all economic segments of
22      the population of this state, promote a variety of residential densities and housing
23      types, and encourage preservation of existing housing stock.

24      The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through the goals and policies
25      in the Housing Element, as well as the variety of residential designations contained
26      in the Comprehensive Plan map.

27   e. Economic Development: Encourage economic development throughout the
28      state that is consistent with adopted comprehensive plans, promote economic
29      opportunity for all citizens of this state, especially for unemployed and for
30      disadvantaged persons, promote the retention and expansion of existing
31      businesses and recruitment of new businesses, recognize regional differences
32      impacting economic development opportunities, and encourage growth in areas
33      experiencing insufficient economic growth, all within the capacities of the state's
34      natural resources, public services, and public facilities.

35      The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through the goals and policies
36      in the Economic Development Element, and through the designation of industrial
37      and commercial lands on the Comprehensive Plan map.

38   f. Property Rights: Private property shall not be taken for public use without just
39      compensation having been made. The property rights of landowners shall be
40      protected from arbitrary and discriminatory actions.
                                                                                                8
 1        It is the intent of Clark County in administering the Comprehensive Plan to ensure
 2        that regulatory actions affecting private property are not arbitrary or discriminatory
 3        in any way. The rights of private property owners and the avoidance of any taking of
 4        private property without just compensation have been given due consideration in
 5        the development of the Comprehensive Plan policies and implementation measures.

 6   g. Permits: Applications for both state and local government permits should be
 7      processed in a timely and fair manner to ensure predictability.

 8        The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through the goals and policies
 9        in the Housing Element, Rural and Resource Element and the Economic
10        Development Element, and through ordinance language that outlines specific
11        standardized processes for permit issuance.

12   h. Natural Resource Industries: Maintain and enhance natural resource-based
13      industries, including productive timber, agricultural, and fisheries industries.
14      Encourage the conservation of productive forestlands and productive agricultural
15      lands, and discourage incompatible uses.

16        The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through the goals and policies
17        in the Rural and Resource Element and the Economic Development Element, and
18        through specific identification and designation of agricultural, forest, and mineral
19        resource lands. The county’s surface mining overlay policies and code were updated
20        in 2014, pursuant to RCW 36.70A.170.

21   i.   Open Space and Recreation: Retain open space and green space, enhance
22        recreational opportunities, enhance fish and wildlife habitat, increase access to
23        natural resource lands and water, and develop parks and recreation facilities.

24        The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through the goals and policies
25        in the Environmental Element and the Parks, Recreation, and Open Space Element. A
26        parks, recreation and open space plan was adopted by the county in 2015 for the
27        unincorporated portion of the Vancouver UGA. The plan was updated in 2022.

28   j.   Environment: Protect and enhance the environment and enhance the state's high
29        quality of life, including air and water quality, and the availability of water.

30        The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through the goals and policies
31        in the Environmental Element and the Parks, Recreation, and Open Space Element,
32        the Climate Element, and related ordinances relating to protection of critical areas
33        and shorelines. The count’s critical areas ordinances have been updated based on
34        best available science pursuant RCW 36.70A.172.

35   k. Citizen Participation and Coordination: Encourage the involvement of citizens in
36      the planning process, including the participation of vulnerable populations and
37      overburdened communities, and ensure coordination between communities and
38      jurisdictions to reconcile conflicts.

39        Throughout the plan update process Clark County has provided extensive                 9
 1           opportunities for citizen involvement and coordination, ranging from community­
 2           wide open houses to surveys to formal hearing testimony. The Community
 3           Involvement Process is detailed in the Introduction to the Comprehensive Plan, and
 4           within this ordinance, including Appendix F.

 5      l.   Public Facilities and Services: Ensure that those public facilities and services
 6           necessary to support development shall be adequate to serve the development at
 7           the time the development is available for occupancy and use without decreasing
 8           current service levels below locally established minimum standards.

 9           The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through the goals and policies
10           in the Capital Facilities and Utilities Element and the Schools Element, Appendix E
11           and the county's transportation concurrency ordinance.

12      m. Historic Preservation: Identify and encourage the preservation of lands, sites, and
13         structures, that have historical or archaeological significance.

14           The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through the goals and policies
15           in the Historic, Archaeological and Cultural Preservation Element.

16      n. Climate Change and Resiliency: Ensure that comprehensive plans, development
17         regulations, and regional policies, plans, and strategies under RCW 36.70A.210 and
18         chapter 47.80 RCW adapt to and mitigate the effects of a changing climate; support
19         reductions in greenhouse gas emissions and per capita vehicle miles traveled;
20         prepare for climate impact scenarios; foster resiliency to climate impacts and natural
21         hazards; protect and enhance environmental, economic, and human health and
22         safety; and advance environmental justice.

23           The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through the inclusion of a new
24           Climate Element (Chapter 14 of the plan), and Appendix I (Climate Element Resource
25           Documents), and through integration of and cross-references to other affected
26           Elements and appendices of the plan. The Climate Element includes a resilience sub
27           element and, following review and approval by the Washington Department of
28           Commerce, will also include a greenhouse gas emissions reduction sub element.

29      o. Shorelines of the State: For shorelines of the state, the goals and policies of the
30         shoreline management act as set forth in RCW 90.58.020 shall be considered an
31         element of the county's or city's comprehensive plan.

32           The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through Chapter 13, Shoreline
33           Master Program. In addition, the county’s shoreline policies, are included in the
34           Shoreline Element of the comprehensive plan.

35   14. Compliance with countywide planning policies. As required by the GMA, Clark County
36       participated in discussions with cities within the county to revise urban growth area
37       boundaries, and adopted such boundaries for each city consistent with the countywide
38       planning policies. Further, the county provided notification to surrounding jurisdictions
39       of its comprehensive plan development process. The county has achieved consistency 10
 1         with adopted countywide planning policies.

 2      15. Compliance with submission requirements. The required notice of intent to adopt was
 3          filed with the Washington Department of Commerce within the required time frame.
 4          a. Commerce received notice of the county's intent to adopt a comprehensive plan
 5              under the GMA on July 24, 2026.
 6          b. Comments were received from Commerce on September 22, 2026. Commerce's
 7              comments were considered in the update of the Comprehensive Plan.

 8      16. Implementation measures. The county has adequate development regulations in place
 9          through Clark County Code Title 40 Unified Development Code. Adoption of updates to
10          zoning ordinances and other measures necessary to implement the Comprehensive Plan
11          are being adopted as part of this ordinance.

12      17. Capital Facilities Plans. Capital facilities plans for service providers (including school
13          districts, public safety, parks, water, sewer, and transportation) satisfy GMA
14          requirements are incorporated into the comprehensive plan.

15   II. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN ADOPTION

16      1. Adoption of the updated Clark County Comprehensive Plan. The 2025 Comprehensive
17         Growth Management Plan is hereby adopted as the 20-year land use plan as the GMA
18         Comprehensive Plan for Clark County. The plan consists of the following documents:
19         a. The 20-year Comprehensive Growth Management Plan document and all text and
20             policies contained therein, including all Appendices A – O (Exhibit 1), except for the
21             greenhouse gas subelement which is adopted in Section 2 of this ordinance herein
22             for submission to the Washington Department of Commerce.
23         b. An updated map showing plan designations for unincorporated rural and resource
24             lands as well as lands within urban growth boundaries in Clark County (Exhibit 2).
25         c. An updated map showing the corresponding zoning that implements the plan
26             designations (Exhibit 3).
27         d. The 2025 Comprehensive Plan parcel number detail (Exhibit 3a).
28         e. The Clark County Arterial Atlas (Exhibit 4).
29         f. The following items, incorporated by reference:
30             i. Vacant and Buildable Lands Analyses for urban growth areas;
31            ii. School Districts and Fire Districts capital facilities plans.
32      2. Approval of Greenhouse Gas Emissions Reduction subelement for submission to
33         Washington Department of Commerce. The Greenhouse Gas Emissions Reduction
34         subelement of the Climate Change and Resiliency Element included in the Plan adopted
35         in Section 1 of this ordinance is hereby adopted for submission to and approval by the
36         Washington State Department of Commerce (“Commerce”) pursuant to RCW
37         36.70A.096(3)(a). The Greenhouse Gas Emissions Reduction subelement shall be
38         effective upon the date of issuance of a written decision of approval by Commerce
39         pursuant to RCW 36.70A.096(1) and .096(4)(b) and, upon such approval, shall be
40         deemed incorporated into the Plan adopted in Section 1 of this ordinance without the
41         necessity of further action by the County Council.

 7   V. MISCELLANEOUS

 8     1. Effective Date. This ordinance shall go into effect 10 days after adoption, at 12:01 a.m.
 9        on October 23, 2026, except for:

10        a.   School, fire, and traffic impact fees (Section IV, Parts 1, 2, and 3), which will take
11             effect 30 days after adoption at 12:01 a.m. on November 12, 2026; and
12        b.   The greenhouse gas emissions reduction sub-element of the Climate Change and
13             Resiliency Element becomes effective on the date of issuance of a written decision
14             of approval by the Department of Commerce, pursuant to RCW 36.70A.096(1) and
15             .096(4)(b); and
16        c.   Urban growth area expansions and resource zone de-designations become effective
17             60 days after adoption.

18     2. Severability. If any section, clause, or phrase of this ordinance should be held invalid or
19        unconstitutional by the Growth Management Hearings Board or a court of competent
20        jurisdiction, such invalidity or unconstitutionality shall not affect the validity or
21        constitutionality of any other section, sentence, clause or phrase of this ordinance.

 4                                       40.100 GENERAL PROVISIONS


5    40.100.070 Definitions
6    Unless the context clearly requires otherwise, the definitions in this section shall apply to terms in this
7    title. In addition to definitions provided below, there are chapter-specific or section-specific definitions in
8    the following sections:

 9       •   Section 40.240.040, Columbia River Gorge National Scenic Area Districts;

10       •   Section 40.250.010, Airport Environs Overlay Districts (AE-1, AE-2);

11       •   Section 40.250.030, Historic Preservation;

12       •   Section 40.260.050, Bed and Breakfast Establishments;

13       •   Section 40.260.100, Home Businesses;

14       •   Section 40.260.250, Wireless Communications Facilities;

15       •   Section 40.310.010, Sign Standards;

16       •   Section 40.386.010, Stormwater and Erosion Control;

17       •   Section 40.410.010, Critical Aquifer Recharge Areas (CARAs);

18       •   Section 40.420.010, Flood Hazard Areas;

19       •   Section 40.430.010, Geologic Hazard Areas;

20       •   Chapter 40.460, Shoreline Master Program;

21       •   Section 40.560.030, Amendments Docket;

22       •   Chapter 40.570, State Environmental Policy Act (SEPA); and

23       •   Section 40.610.020, Development Impact Fees.

24       (Amended: Ord. 2009-06-01; Ord. 2012-07-16; Ord. 2015-11-24; Ord. 2017-07-04;
25       Ord. 2018-01-09; Ord. 2018-01-17; Ord. 2019-05-07; Ord. 2019-07-01; Ord. 2023-
26       03-01; Ord. 2024-03-01; Ord. 2025-07-01)

     Aboveground storage tanks “Aboveground storage tank” means any one (1) or combination of tanks
                               (including underground pipes connected thereto) that is used to contain an
                               accumulation of regulated substances, and the volume of which is ninety
                               percent (90%) or more above the surface of the ground.
     Abutting                       “Abutting” means sharing a common boundary line; except that where two
                                    (2) or more lots share a common boundary line only at a corner or corners,
                                    they shall not be considered as abutting unless the common boundary line
                                    between the two (2) parcels measures not less than eight (8) feet in a single
                                    direction.                                                                  17
Access                       “Access” means the place, means, or way by which pedestrians or vehicles
                             shall have safe, adequate and usable ingress and egress to a property or
                             use, as required by this title. Residential lots shall be provided a minimum
                             of twenty (20) feet of access to a public or private street. Nonresidential
                             uses shall be provided access according to Chapters 40.340 and 40.350.
                                 (Amended: Ord. 2018-01-09)

Accessway                    “Accessway” means a public facility shared by pedestrians and bicyclists.
Access roads                 “Access roads” means any of the urban or rural access roads as defined in
                             Tables 40.350.030-2 and 40.350.030-3. This term is used to differentiate
                             access roads from arterials, collectors, and commercial/industrial roads.
                                 (Amended: Ord. 2012-05-14)

Accessory use or structure   “Accessory use” or “accessory structure” means one which is subordinate
                             to the principal use of a building on the lot serving a purpose customarily
                             incidental to the use of the principal building.
Adjacent                     “Adjacent” means near, close; for example, an industrial district across the
                             street or highway from a commercial district shall be considered as
                             “adjacent.”
Adjoining                    “Adjoining” means sharing a common boundary line, including across a
                             public or private right-of-way or easement from the property in question.
Administrative manual        “Administrative manual,” when referring to transportation concurrency,
                             means the written documentation adopted by the Public Works Director
                             pursuant to Section 40.350.020.
ADT                          “ADT” stands for average daily trips.
Adult family home            “Adult family home” means a single-family dwelling or duplex licensed as
                             such by the state of Washington, housing a maximum of six (6) adult
                             residents, except when up to two (2) additional residents are authorized
                             pursuant to RCW 70.128.066, where staff assumes the responsibility for
                             the safety and well-being of the residents due to their age or condition.
                             Care is provided by staff and may include provision of meals, laundry, and
                             assistance with activities of daily living, and may include nursing care.
                             Staff may or may not reside in the same dwelling.
                                 (Amended: Ord. 2010-08-06; Ord. 2012-02-08)

Affected transportation      “Affected transportation corridor” means any transportation corridor which
corridor                     is reasonably projected to be affected by the transportation related impacts
                             of a proposed development.
Affordable housing           “Affordable housing” means residential housing whose monthly costs,
                             including utilities other than telephone, do not exceed thirty percent
                             (30%) of the monthly income of a household whose income is:
                                   (a) For rental housing, sixty percent (60%) of the median
                                   household income adjusted for household size, for Clark County,
                                   as reported by the United States Department of Housing and
                                   Urban Development; or
                                   (b) For owner-occupied housing, eighty percent (80%) of the
                                   median household income adjusted for household size, for Clark
                                   County, as reported by the United States Department of Housing
                                   and Urban Development.
Agricultural market          “Agricultural market” means a permanent building or accessory structure
                                                                                                       18
                             used for the year-round sale of agricultural products grown on site or on
                             other farms in the local agricultural area, and may include incidental retail
                             sale of items or fee-based activities, both as accessory to the primary on-
                             site products, to promote the sale of farm crops. An agricultural market is
                             distinguished from a roadside farm stand by a larger scale of activity and a
                             greater range of products offered. This definition does not include a
                             winery.
                                  (Added: Ord. 2012-06-02)

Agriculture, agriculture     “Agriculture,” “agriculture uses” or “agricultural activities” means the use
uses or agricultural         of the land for agricultural purposes, including, but not limited to, farming,
activities                   dairying, pasturage, agriculture, horticulture, floriculture, viticulture and
                             wineries, apiaries, and animal and poultry husbandry, and the necessary
                             accessory uses and structures; provided, however, that the construction and
                             operation of any such accessory use or structure shall be incidental to that
                             of normal agricultural activities; and provided further, that the above uses
                             shall not include slaughterhouses and meat packing or commercial feeding
                             of animals.
                             Marijuana production and processing do not, for the purposes of this
                             zoning title, meet the definition of agriculture, agricultural uses or
                             agricultural activities, and may only occur in accordance with Chapter 314-
                             55 WAC and Section 40.260.115.
                                  (Amended: Ord. 2006-07-09; Ord. 2006-08-03; Ord.
                                  2014-05-07)

Alley, private               “Private alley” means an easement or tract not over thirty (30) feet wide
                             which affords, generally, a secondary means of vehicular access to
                             abutting lots, and is not intended for general use.
Alley, public                “Public alley” means a right-of-way not over thirty (30) feet wide which
                             affords a secondary means of vehicular access to abutting lots and is not
                             intended for general use.
All-weather driving surface “All-weather driving surface” means any road or driveway surface that
                            when reasonably maintained will provide all-weather driving capabilities
                            for fire and other emergency vehicle apparatus.
Alteration, structural       “Structural alteration” means any change or repair which would tend to
                             prolong the life of the supporting members of a building or structure. Any
                             change in the external dimensions of the building is a structural alteration.
Altered                      “Altered,” when referring to wetlands, means a wetland of which at least
                             fifty percent (50%) has been graded, drained, devegetated, or replanted
                             with nonwetland plants.
Ambulance dispatch facility “Ambulance dispatch facility” means a public or private structure used to
                            house a crew and ambulance to be dispatched from a central ambulance
                            service facility located elsewhere. The structure is selected based on its
                            location to serve a specific area of the county with a quick response time.
Ambulance service facility   “Ambulance service facility” means a public or private structure used to
                             house ambulance service administrative personnel, equipment and supply
                             storage, and repair and radio dispatch equipment. This facility may also
                             include an ambulance dispatch facility.
Amusement center             “Amusement center” means a permanent development containing a single
                             or multiple indoor or outdoor amusements such as batting cages, video
                             arcades, go-cart tracks, bumper cars or amusement rides. Traveling
                             carnivals and circuses shall not be considered amusement centers.     19
Anadromous                   “Anadromous” means fish that migrate up rivers and streams from the
                             ocean to breed in fresh water.
Animal boarding facility     “Animal boarding facility” means any premises used to conduct a business
                             that involves overnight boarding of domestic animals for any purpose, but
                             not to include animal hospitals, veterinary clinics, or kennels.
                                  (Amended: Ord. 2011-03-09; Ord. 2012-02-03)

Animal day use facility      “Animal day use facility” means any premises used to conduct a daytime-
                             only business with hours of operation from 6:00 a.m. to 10:00 p.m. that
                             involves domestic animals as defined in Section 8.01.020, including but
                             not limited to breeding, training, grooming, and day care.
                                 (Amended: Ord. 2010-12-12; Ord. 2011-03-09; Ord.
                                 2012-02-03)

Animal hospital or           “Animal hospital” or “veterinary clinic” means a place where animals or
veterinary clinic            pets are given medical or surgical treatment, and are cared for during the
                             time of such treatment.
Animal husbandry             “Animal husbandry” means the care and raising of domesticated animals,
                             such as cattle, horses, sheep, llamas, and poultry, but not including
                             household pets, such as dogs and cats.
                                 (Amended: Ord. 2006-07-09; Ord. 2006-08-03)

Applicant                    “Applicant” means the person, party, firm, corporation, legal entity, or
                             agent thereof who submits an application for an activity regulated by this
                             title.
Aquifer                      “Aquifer” means a groundwater-bearing geologic formation or formations
                             that contain enough saturated permeable material to yield significant
                             quantities of water to wells or springs (source: Chapter 173-100 WAC).
Archaeological site          “Archaeological site” means a site containing significant physical evidence
                             or ruins of human occupation or activity that are located on or below the
                             surface of the ground and are at least one hundred (100) years old.
                             Archaeological resources on these sites include, but are not limited to, the
                             remains of houses, villages, camp and fishing sites, and cave shelters; rock
                             art such as petroglyphs and pictographs; artifacts such as arrowheads,
                             utensils, tools, fragments of tools and utensils, obsidian flakes or other
                             material byproducts from tool and utensil-making activities; and graves,
                             human remains, and associated artifacts.
Area of special flood        “Area of special flood hazard” or “flood hazard area” means the land in the
hazard, or flood hazard area floodplain subject to the base flood.
Arterial                     “Arterial” means any urban parkway arterial, urban principal arterial, or
                             urban minor arterial, as defined in Tables 40.350.030-2 and 40.350.030-3.
                             This term is used to differentiate arterials from collectors,
                             commercial/industrial, and access roads.
                                 (Amended: Ord. 2012-05-14)

Arterial Atlas               “Arterial Atlas” means the current Clark County Arterial Atlas as adopted
                             by Council, or hereafter amended.
                                 (Amended: Ord. 2019-05-07)

Assisted living facility     “Assisted living facility” means a residential use, licensed by the state of
                             Washington as a boarding home, where staff assumes responsibility for the20
                              safety and well-being of the adult residents. Housing, meals, laundry,
                              supervision and varying levels of assistance with daily activities are
                              provided by staff and may include nursing care. An assisted living facility
                              contains seven (7) or more assisted living units.
                                  (Added: Ord. 2010-08-06)

Athletic, health and racket   “Athletic, health and racket clubs” means a place of business where clients
clubs                         are charged to engage in physical activities including aerobics, racket
                              sports, weightlifting, swimming, etc.
                                  (Amended: Ord. 2007-11-13)

Base zone                     “Base zone” means the primary district applicable to a parcel of property
                              irrespective of any overlay district.
Basin                         “Basin” means a watershed.
Bed and breakfast             “Bed and breakfast establishment” means a residence where an individual
establishment                 or family resides and rents up to six (6) bedrooms to guests and provides
                              breakfast to those guests.
                                  (Added: Ord. 2010-08-06)

Best management practices “Best management practices” or “BMPs” means those physical, structural
(BMPs)                    and managerial practices, and prohibitions of practices, that, when used
                          singly or in combination:
                          • Prevent or reduce erosion, or

                              • Control stormwater runoff peak flow rates and volumes, and

                              • Prevent or reduce pollution of surface water or groundwater.

Bike lane                     “Bike lane” means the bicycle-only portion of a public street or road.
Binding site plan             “Binding site plan” means a site plan which provides an alternative means
                              of dividing land for the sale or lease of commercially or industrially zoned
                              property; and placement of manufactured homes or mobile homes on
                              leased sites.
Bioretention facility         “Bioretention facilities” are shallow landscaped depressions, with a
                              designed soil mix and plants adapted to the local climate and soil moisture
                              conditions, that receive stormwater from a contributing area. (Per
                              Department of Ecology 2012 Stormwater Management Manual for
                              Western Washington, as Amended in December 2014 (The 2014
                              SWMMWW).)
                                  (Added: Ord. 2015-11-24)

Block                         “Block” means an area of land whose boundaries are defined by public or
                              private streets, excluding alleys.
Block length                  “Block length” means the distance between intersections with other public
                              or private roads as measured along the nearside right-of-way line.
Block perimeter               “Block perimeter” means the perimeter of a block as measured along the
                              nearside right-of-way lines of public streets or accessway easements but
                              exclusive of driveways.
Boarding house                “Boarding house” means a building other than a hotel with furnished
                              rooms with no cooking facilities, where, for compensation, meals or
                              lodging and meals are provided for four (4) or more persons.        21
Branch bank         “Branch bank” means a service outlet providing primarily personal
                    banking and related financial services to the general public.
Bridge              “Bridge” means a structure including supports erected over a depression or
                    an obstruction, such as water, highway, or railway, and characterized by
                    the following:
                    • A track or passageway for carrying traffic or other moving loads; and

                    • An opening measured along the center of the roadway of:

                    o Single-span steel or concrete with spans of six (6) feet or greater;

                    o Steel corrugated pipes with spans of eight (8) feet or greater;

                    o Timber with spans of four (4) feet or greater;

                    o Multiple pipes with spans of ten (10) feet or greater with multiple
                    spans of eight (8) feet or greater between undercopings of abutments or
                    spring lines of arches, or extreme ends of openings for multiple boxes; or

                    o Multiple pipes, where the clear distance between openings is less than
                    half of the smaller contiguous opening.

                    Bridges do not include structures with the depth of fill more than D/2,
                    where D is the maximum span.
                        (Added: Ord. 2012-05-30)

Buffer              “Buffer,” when referring to wetlands, means an area that surrounds and
                    protects a wetland from adverse impacts to the functions of a wetland.
Buffer              “Buffer,” when referring to geologic hazard areas, means an area
                    surrounding a geologic hazard consisting of naturally occurring or
                    reestablished vegetation and having a width adequate to protect the
                    geologic hazard area.
Building            “Building” means a combination of materials to form a structure that is
                    adapted to a permanent or continued occupancy. It is a structure having a
                    roof supported by columns or walls for the housing or enclosure of
                    persons, animals, or chattel.
Building front      “Building front” means the street-facing elevation(s) of a building.
Building, main      “Main building” means a building within which is conducted the principal
                    use permitted on the lot, as provided in the UDC.
Building permit     “Building permit” means the permit issued by the county to an applicant
                    for building a structure.
Capacity            “Capacity,” when referring to vehicular traffic, means a measurement
                    expressed as the maximum number of peak hour vehicle trips that an
                    individual development may generate as defined in Section 40.350.020.
Carport             “Carport” means a roof projecting from one (1) side of a building designed
                    to cover, but not enclose, automobile parking spaces.
Church              “Church” means a permanently located building primarily used for
                    religious worship.
Circulation drive   “Circulation drive” means a place, means or way by which pedestrians and

Demolition waste            “Demolition waste” means largely inert waste, resulting from the
                            demolition or razing of buildings, roads, and other manmade structures.
                            Demolition waste consists of, but is not limited to, concrete, brick,
                            bituminous concrete, wood, masonry, composition roofing and roofing
                            paper, steel, and minor amounts of other metals like copper. Plaster (sheet
                            rock or plaster board) or any other material, other than wood, that is likely
                            to produce gases or a leachate during the decomposition process, and
                            asbestos wastes, are not considered to be demolition waste to this
                            regulation (source: Chapter 173-304 WAC).

                            comparing the number of dwelling units with land area, and is expressed as
                            the number of residential dwelling units per acre of land in a residential
                            development, including, but not limited to, one (1) house on one (1) lot.

Facultative plants            “Facultative plants” means plants that are equally likely (thirty-four
                              percent (34%) to sixty-six percent (66%) probability) to occur in wetlands
                              or nonwetlands. Such groupings are more fully defined in the Wetlands
                              Delineation Manual.
Facultative wet plants        “Facultative wet plants” means plants that usually (sixty-seven percent
                              (67%) to ninety-nine percent (99%) probability) occur in wetlands. Such
                              groupings are more fully defined in the Wetlands Delineation Manual.

                              housekeeping unit and using common cooking facilities whether related by
                              genetics, adoption, or marriage, or unrelated.

Finished product retailer     “Finished product retailer” means a retail outlet primarily for the sale to

Floor area, gross             “Gross floor area” means the total enclosed area of all floors of a building
                              measured to the outside face of the structural members in exterior walls
                              and including halls, stairways, and elevator shafts at each floor level.
                              For retrofits of existing buildings to be used for residential use and for
                              new construction or the retrofit of existing buildings meeting passive
                              house requirements, gross floor area must be measured from the
                              interior face of the exterior walls, which includes drywall, as typically
                              depicted on the architectural floor plans.
Floor area ratio              “Floor area ratio (FAR)” means the gross floor area of all buildings on a
                              lot divided by the lot area. For example, a FAR of two to one (2:1) means
                              two (2) square feet of floor area for every one (1) square foot of site area.

Hazardous substances          “Hazardous materials” or “hazardous substances” means such material as
                              flammable solids; corrosive liquids; radioactive material; oxidizing
                              material; highly toxic material; poisonous gases; reactive material;
                              unstable material; hyperbolic material; pyrophoric material as defined in
                              Chapter 2 of the International Fire Code; and substances, or mixture of
                              substances, that are an irritant or strong sensitizer or which generate
                              pressure through exposure to heat, decomposition, or other means.
                              “Hazardous substances” shall also mean hazardous waste as designated in
                              Chapter 173-303 WAC as dangerous or extremely hazardous waste.
                              “Hazardous substances” also means any dangerous waste or extremely
                              hazardous waste as defined in RCW 70.105.010(5) and (6), or any
                              dangerous or extremely dangerous waste as designated by rule under
                              Chapter 70.105 RCW; any hazardous substance as defined in RCW
                              70.105.010(14) or any hazardous substance as defined by rule under
                              Chapter 70.105 RCW; and any substance that, on the effective date of the
                              ordinance codified in this section, is a hazardous substance under Section
                              101(14) of the Federal Cleanup Law, 42 U.S.C., Section 9601(14);
                              petroleum products; and any substance or category of substances including
                              solid waste decomposition products, determined by the Washington
                              Department of Ecology’s director to present a threat to human health or the
                              environment if released into the environment. The term “hazardous
                              substances” does not include any of the following when contained in an

                              any fraction thereof or petroleum, if the tank is in compliance with all
                              applicable federal, state, and local laws.

Hazardous waste storage       “Hazardous waste storage” means the holding of dangerous waste for a
                              temporary period as regulated by the State Dangerous Waste Regulations,
                              Chapter 173-303 WAC.
Hazardous waste treatment “Hazardous waste treatment” means the physical, chemical or biological
                          processing of dangerous waste to make such wastes nondangerous or less
                          dangerous, safer for transport, amenable for energy or material recovery,
                          amenable for storage, or reduced in volume.

and storage facility, off-site that treat and store waste from generators on properties other than those on
                               which the off-site facilities are located.
Hazardous waste treatment “Hazardous waste treatment and storage facility, on-site” means facilities
and storage facility, on-site that treat and store waste generated on the same geographically contiguous
                              or bordering property.

examiner                      examiner as established by Chapter 2.51.                                    32
Height, building          “Building height” means the vertical distance from grade plane to the

High intensity land use   “High intensity land use,” when referring to the regulation of wetlands,
                          means the following uses of land: roadways, commercial, industrial, and
                          multifamily exceeding four (4) units per parcel.
High-impact use           “High-impact use,” when referring to critical aquifer recharge areas, means
                          a business establishment that is regulated due to the probability and/or
                          magnitude of its effects on the environment. For purposes of this chapter,
                          these uses possess certain characteristics posing a substantial potential
                          threat or risk to the quality of groundwater and surface waters within
                          Category I CARAs. High-impact uses shall include, but are not limited to,
                          the following:
                          • Landfills;

                          • Class V injection wells:

                          o Agricultural drainage wells,

                          o Untreated sewage waste disposal wells,

                          o Cesspools,

                          o Industrial process water and disposal wells, and

                          o Radioactive waste disposal;

                          • Radioactive disposal sites; and

                          • Activities in Section 40.410.020(B) that are not connected to public
                          sewer.

Home business             “Home business” means a business in conjunction with a residential use
                          which results in financial remuneration from a product or service and is
                          conducted by at least one (1) resident occupying the dwelling on the
                          subject property.

                                (Amended: Ord. 2005-04-12)

Home occupation             In the Columbia River Gorge National Scenic Area Districts only a “home

Homeowners association      “Homeowners association” means a nonprofit organization operating under
                            recorded land agreements through which the following take place:
                            • Each person owning or purchasing a lot in a planned unit or other
                            described land area is automatically a member by such ownership or
                            purchase.

                            • Each lot is automatically subject to a charge for a proportionate share of

                            property are identified and assigned.

Hospital                    “Hospital” means any institution, place, building or agency which
                            maintains and operates organized facilities for twenty (20) or more persons
                            for the diagnosis, care and treatment of human illness, including
                            convalescence and also including care during and after pregnancy; or
                            which maintains and operates organized facilities for any such purpose,
                            and to which persons may be admitted for overnight stay or for a longer
                            period.
Hotel                       “Hotel” means a place of lodging that provides sleeping accommodations,
                            restaurants, cocktail lounges, meeting and banquet rooms or convention
                            facilities, and other retail and service shops.
Hydric soil                 “Hydric soil” means a soil that is saturated, flooded or ponded long enough
                            during the growing season to develop anaerobic conditions in the upper
                            part. The presence of hydric soil shall be determined following the
                            methods described in the Wetlands Delineation Manual.
Hydroperiod                 “Hydroperiod” means a seasonal occurrence of flooding and/or soil
                            saturation; it encompasses depth, frequency, duration, and seasonal pattern
                            of inundation.
Hydrophytic vegetation      “Hydrophytic vegetation” means macrophytic plant life growing in water
                            or on a substrate that is at least periodically deficient in oxygen as a result
                            of excessive water content. The presence of hydrophytic vegetation shall
                            be determined following the methods described in the Wetlands
                            Delineation Manual.
Intermittent stream         “Intermittent stream” means surface streams with no measurable flow
                            during thirty (30) consecutive days in a normal water year.
International Building Code “International Building Code” means the published International Building
                            Code as adopted by Clark County.
Intersection of regional    “Intersection of regional significance” means an intersection at which at
significance                least three (3) approaches have a comprehensive plan functional
                            classification of collector or higher.
Isolated wetlands           “Isolated wetlands” means those wetlands which are not within or
                            contiguous with a fish and wildlife habitat conservation area as defined in
                            Section 40.445.020(C) or shoreline jurisdiction as defined in Section       34
                           40.460.210(A) and are separated from other wetlands by a distance greater
                           than the largest wetland buffers required under Section 40.445.020(B)(5)
                           for all adjacent wetlands.

Kennel, commercial         “Kennel, commercial” means any of the following:
                           • Any premises used to conduct a commercial business involving the
                           buying, selling, breeding for sale, letting for hire, boarding or training of
                           dogs;

                           • Any premises at which ten (10) or more adult dogs are kept for any
                           purpose, including animal shelters, but excluding animal hospitals or
                           clinics where animals are kept only for treatment by licensed veterinarians;

                           • Any premises where offspring puppies or adult dogs are sold to
                           commercial outlets or are sold for research or experimental purposes;

                           • Any premises where offspring from three (3) or more litters per twelve
                           (12) month period are sold or traded, exchanged or bartered for a valuable
                           consideration or joint ownership purpose; or

                           • Any premises used as the location for the training of dogs for
                           obedience, hunting, protection, etc. (if the address is different from the
                           office address), or the premises are used as a combination office/training
                           location, except if the training site is property belonging to a recognized

Land-disturbing activity   “Land-disturbing activity” means any activity that results in a change in
                           the existing soil cover (both vegetative and nonvegetative) and/or the
                           existing soil topography. Land-disturbing activities include, but are not
                           limited to, clearing, grading, filling, and excavation. Compaction that is
                           associated with stabilization of structures and road construction is also
                           considered a land-disturbing activity. Vegetation maintenance practices,
                           including landscape maintenance and gardening, are not considered land-
                           disturbing activity. Stormwater facility maintenance is not considered land-
                           disturbing activity if conducted according to established standards and
                           procedures.
                               (Amended: Ord. 2012-05-14; Ord. 2015-11-24)

Landfill                   “Landfill” means a disposal facility or part of a facility at which solid and
                           demolition waste is permanently placed in or on the land that is not a land-
                           spreading disposal facility (Chapter 173-304 WAC). In addition, “landfill”35
                             means all continuous land and structures and other improvements on the
                             land used for the disposal of solid waste, pursuant to Chapter 173-351
                             WAC.
Landscaping                  “Landscaping” means not only trees, grass, bushes, shrubs, flowers and
                             garden areas, but also the arrangement of fountains, patios, decks, street
                             furniture, and ornamental concrete or stonework areas and artificial turf or
                             carpeting, if those areas are permeable and allow stormwater infiltration in
                             accordance with Section 40.386.010. Artificial plants, shrubs, bushes,
                             flowers, and materials in movable containers are not considered
                             landscaping.

Landslide                    “Landslide” means down-slope movement of a mass of soil, or rock,
                             including, but not limited to, rock falls, slumps, mud flows, debris flows,
                             torrents, and earth flows.
Landslide protection areas   “Landslide protection areas” means those areas which are to be left

                             clearing, grading, filling, building construction or placement or road
