Lot coverage              “Lot coverage” means that percentage of the total lot area covered by
                          structures, including all projections except eaves.
Lot depth                 “Lot depth” means the horizontal distance between the midpoint of the
                          front and opposite, usually the rear lot line. Average lot depth shall be the
                          average of the side lot lines. In the case of a corner lot, the depth shall be
                          the average length of the longer dimension of the lot. In the case of flag
                          lots, lot depth shall be the average length of the longer dimension of the
                          main body of the lot. See Figure 40.100.070-2.
                               (Amended: Ord. 2007-06-05; Ord. 2018-01-09)

Lot line                  “Lot line” means any line bounding a lot as herein defined. Lot lines for
                          unusual lot configurations may be determined by the responsible official.
Lot line, front           “Front lot line” means that portion of the property line abutting a street
                          right-of-way, street easement, street tract, or private driveway easement.
                          • For corner lots, the front lot line is that which provides vehicular
                          access. In the case where vehicular access is provided on more than one (1)
                          street, one (1) front lot line and one (1) street side lot line shall be
                          designated. Within a street side setback, entrances to garages, carports, or
                          similar vehicular shelters shall maintain a minimum eighteen (18) foot
                          setback from the property line, street easement, street tract or inside edge
                          of any pedestrian easement, when the street side setback for the applicable
                          zoning district is less than eighteen (18) feet. If access is provided to a
                          corner lot by an alley, the front lot line is that which is most opposite the
                          alley.

                          • For a flag lot, the front lot line is the juncture between the pole and the
                          flag. (See Figure 40.100.070-2.)

                          • For through lots, all lot lines abutting a street or approved private road
                          or easement, except for alleys, shall be front lot lines unless vehicular
                          access is prohibited, in which case they shall be considered rear lot lines.
                                                                                                           37
                            (Amended: Ord. 2007-06-05; Ord. 2007-11-13; Ord.
                            2008-06-02; Ord. 2016-09-04)

Lot line, rear          “Rear lot line” means a lot line not abutting a street which is opposite and
                        most distant from the front lot line.
Lot line, side          “Side lot line” means any lot line which is not a front lot line or a rear lot
                        line. In the case of flag lots, all lot lines other than the front lot line are
                        considered side lot lines.
                             (Amended: Ord. 2007-06-05)

Lot line, street side   “Street side lot line” means a side property line abutting a public right-of-

                        originally created or segregated, or which is otherwise determined to be
                        consistent with the criteria of the UDC. Owners of such lots shall be

                        Parcels segregated for tax purposes are not lots of record unless they
                        comply with both platting and zoning laws in existence at the time that an
                        application for segregation is received by the County Assessor, or are
                        otherwise determined to be consistent with the criteria of the UDC.
Lot width               “Lot width” means the horizontal distance measured at the building
                        setback line between the two (2) opposite side lot lines. Average lot width
                        shall be the average of the front and rear lot lines. For a corner lot, the lot
                        width shall be the average distance of the narrower dimension of the lot.
                            (Amended: Ord. 2007-06-05)

                        (130) degrees within the lot lines. For the purposes of this definition,

Mitigation                 “Mitigation,” when referring to transportation facilities, means the

Nonconforming use         “Nonconforming use” means a use of land, building, or structure which use

                          surgical or emergency medical services. This definition excludes hospitals
                          and sanitariums.

Obligate plants           “Obligate plants” mean plants that almost always (ninety-nine percent
                          (99%) probability) occur in wetlands under natural conditions. Such
                          groupings are more fully defined in the Wetlands Delineation Manual.
Official controls         “Official controls” means legislatively defined and enacted policies,
                          standards, precise detailed maps, and other criteria, all of which control the
                          physical development of the county, and are the means of translating into
                          regulations and ordinances all or any part of the general objectives of the
                          comprehensive plan. Such official controls may include, but are not limited
                          to, ordinances establishing zoning, subdivision control, platting, and
                          adoption of detailed maps.
Open space/commons        “Open space/commons,” when referring to density transfer, means an area
                          left open and undeveloped, for use by the public, but is not a formal park 42
                           or recreation area managed or owned by the county. The land shall be
                           identified as a separate tract and shall be managed and maintained in
                           perpetuity for the intended use of the homeowners of the relevant
                           development.
Open space, usable         “Usable open space” means an open area that is not covered in impervious
                           surface and that exceeds four hundred (400) square feet with all
                           dimensions a minimum of twenty (20) feet.
Open water                 “Open water,” when referring to wetlands and when not specifically
                           defined by the wetland rating criteria, means a proportion of open water to
                           vegetative cover equal to twenty-five percent (25%) to seventy-five
                           percent (75%) of the total wetland area during a majority of a normal water
                           year.
Operating level            “Operating level,” when referring to transportation facilities, means the
                           performance of a transportation corridor or intersection of regional
                           significance, pursuant to Section 40.350.020(F).
Ordinance                  “Ordinance” means the ordinance, resolution, or other procedure used by
                           the county to adopt regulatory requirements.
Ordinary high water mark   “Ordinary high water mark” means the point on the sides of streams or
                           lakes which is historically or normally at water’s edge, as identified by a
                           visible change in vegetation and/or soil.
Owner                      “Owner” means any person having property rights as a fee owner or
                           contract purchaser, or one duly authorized by the power of attorney to
                           represent the owner. For the purposes of this title, in terms of violations
                           and binding agreements between the county and the owner, “owner” shall
                           also mean a leaseholder, tenant, or person in possession or control of the
                           premises or property at the time of agreement, violations of agreement, or
                           the provisions of this title. For the purpose of processing an application for
                           a land use approval or permit under this title, where such application or
                           permit must be filed by an owner, the term “owner” also includes a
                           governmental entity contemplating acquisition of a parcel for a use which
                           would require such permit or approval. Where property is proposed to be
                           developed pursuant to an easement right, “owner” means the person who is
                           the holder of the easement.
Park, community            “Community park” means a public park that provides a focal point and
                           gathering place for broad groups of users. Community parks are used by all
                           segments of the population and generally serve residents from a one (1) to
                           three (3) mile service area. Community parks often include recreation
                           facilities for organized activities such as sports fields, skate parks, and play
                           courts.
                               (Added: Ord. 2016-06-12)

Park, neighborhood         “Neighborhood park” means a public park designed to provide
                           nonorganized recreational opportunities for residents living within a one-
                           half (1/2) mile radius, and are located such that they are within walking
                           and bicycling distance of most users. These parks generally contain three
                           (3) to five (5) acres but may vary in size depending upon unique site
                           characteristics, opportunities and land availability.
                                (Added: Ord. 2016-06-12)

Park, regional             “Regional park” means a public recreational area that serves residents
                           throughout Clark County, as well as outside the county. Facilities may        43
                            include sports fields, extensive trail systems, or large picnic areas. Because
                            of their large size and broad service area, regional parks typically require
                            more support facilities, such as parking and restrooms. These parks are
                            designed to accommodate large numbers of people.
                                 (Added: Ord. 2016-06-12)

Park trailer                “Park trailer” or "park model trailer" means a travel trailer designed
                            to be used with temporary connections to utilities necessary for
                            operation of installed fixtures and appliances. The trailer's gross area
                            shall not exceed four hundred (400) square feet when in the setup
                            mode. “Park trailer” excludes a manufactured home or recreational
                            vehicle.
Parking area, public        “Public parking area” means an open area other than a street or other
                            public way used for the parking of automobiles and available to the public
                            whether for a fee, free of charge, or as an accommodation for clients or
                            customers.
Parking space               “Parking space” shall be a permanently surfaced and marked area for the
                            parking of a motor vehicle, excluding paved area necessary for access.
Party of record             “Party of record” means all persons, agencies or organizations who have
                            submitted written comments in response to a notice of application, made
                            oral comments in a formal public hearing conducted on the application, or
                            requested in writing to be a “party of record.” In the case of Type I
                            decisions, the party of record shall include the applicant and any person
                            who files a written request prior to the issuance of the decision. In the case
                            of Type II decisions, the party of record shall include the applicant, and
                            any person submitting to the responsible official written testimony, or a
                            written request to be a “party of record,” that is specific to a particular
                            application prior to the issuance of the decision. In the case of Type III
                            decisions, the party of record shall include the applicant and persons
                            submitting written testimony before, during, or prior to the close of a
                            public hearing; providing oral testimony at a public hearing; signing the
                            sign-in sheet noting the person’s name, address and the subject matter in
                            which they are interested; or by submitting a written request to the
                            responsible official to be a “party of record” that is specific to a particular
                            application prior to the close of the subject public hearing.
                            Notwithstanding any of the foregoing, no person shall be a party of record
                            who has not furnished an accurate post office mailing address.
Passive house               “Passive house requirements” means the criteria for certification as a
requirements                passive house by Phius or the international passive house institute.
Pavement or paved surface “Pavement” or “paved surface” means an uncovered, hard-surfaced area or
                          an area covered with a perforated hard surface (such as “Grasscrete”) that
                          is able to withstand vehicular traffic or other heavy impact uses. Paved
                          areas include both permeable and impermeable hard surfaces. Graveled
                          areas are not paved areas.

                                                                                                          44
                            other area intended primarily for use by pedestrians.
Pedestrian plaza         “Pedestrian plaza” means an area devoted solely to pedestrians that is a
                         minimum of ten (10) feet in depth and width with a minimum size of six
                         hundred fifty (650) square feet that is at least fifty percent (50%) paved
                         with colored, textured concrete, brick pavers or other suitable surface and

Permanent supportive     Permanent supportive housing is subsidized, leased housing with no
housing                  limit on length of stay that prioritizes people who need comprehensive
                         support services to retain tenancy and utilizes admissions practices
                         designed to use lower barriers to entry than would be typical for other
                         subsidized or unsubsidized rental housing, especially related to rental
                         history, criminal history and personal behaviors. Permanent
                         supportive housing is paired with on-site or off-site voluntary services
                         designed to support a person living with a complex and disabling
                         behavioral health or physical health condition who was experiencing
                         homelessness or was at imminent risk of homelessness prior to moving
                         into housing to retain their housing and be a successful tenant in a
                         housing arrangement, improve the resident's health status, and
                         connect the resident of the housing with community-based health care,
                         treatment or employment services. Permanent supportive housing is
                         subject to all of the rights and responsibilities defined in chapter 59.18
                         RCW. (RCW 36.70A.030(31))
Permeable pavement       “Permeable pavement” means pervious concrete, porous asphalt,
                         permeable pavers or other forms of pervious or porous paving material
                         intended to allow passage of water through the pavement section. It often
                         includes an aggregate base that provides structural support and acts as a
                         stormwater reservoir.
                             (Added: Ord. 2015-11-24)

Start of construction      “Start of construction” includes substantial improvement, and means the

                           on a site, such as the pouring of slab or footings, the installation of piles,

                            or the placement of a manufactured home on a foundation. Permanent
                            construction does not include land preparation, such as clearing, grading
                            and filling; nor does it include the installation of streets and/or walkways;
                            nor does it include excavation for a basement, footings, piers, or
                            foundation or other erection of temporary forms; nor does it include the
                            installation on the property of accessory buildings, such as garages or
                            sheds not occupied as dwelling units or not part of the main structure.

                           40.570.

significance                owned and maintained roadway or intersection not designated by the state

Stormwater facility         “Stormwater facility” means the natural or constructed components of a
                            stormwater drainage system, designed and constructed to perform a
                            particular function, or multiple functions. Stormwater facilities include, but
                            are not limited to, pipes, swales, ditches, culverts, street gutters, detention
                            ponds, retention ponds, constructed wetlands, infiltration devices, catch-

Story                       “Story,” when referring to structures, means that portion of a building
                            included between the upper surface of any floor and the upper surface of
                            the floor next above, except that the topmost story shall be that portion of a
                            building included between the upper surface of the topmost floor and the
                            ceiling or roof above. If the finished floor level directly above a basement
                            or unused under-floor space is more than six (6) feet above grade for more
                            than fifty percent (50%) of the total perimeter or is more than twelve (12)
                            feet above grade at any point, such basement or unused under-floor space
                            shall be considered as a story.
Stream or streams           “Stream” or “streams” means those areas where surface waters flow
                            sufficiently to produce a defined channel or bed. A defined channel or bed
                            is indicated by hydraulically sorted sediments or the removal of vegetative
                            litter or loosely rooted vegetation by the action of moving water. The
                            channel or bed need not contain water year round. This definition is not
                            meant to include irrigation ditches, canals, stormwater runoff devices or
                            other entirely artificial watercourses unless they are used to convey
                            streams naturally occurring prior to construction. Those topographic
                            features that resemble streams but have no defined channels (i.e., swales)
                            shall be considered streams when hydrologic and hydraulic analyses done
                            pursuant to a development proposal predict formation of a defined channel
                            after development.
Stream bank stabilization   “Stream bank stabilization” means those approved bioengineered projects.
                            The projects can include both passive and active types of methods for
                            stabilizing the stream bank.
Street                      See “Road or street.”
Street tree                 “Street tree” shall be defined as a single-stem deciduous tree with a central
                            leader that can be used as a landscape element for buffering/screening
                            along front, side, and rear setbacks. Unique site features shall determine
                            the most appropriate street tree to use. A street tree shall be broad branched
                            and characteristically have lower branches that are able to be removed to
                                                                                                         53
                            meet clearance standards.
Structure                   “Structure” means that which is built or constructed, an edifice or building

                            joined together in some definite manner.

                            14.

                            area, five (5) or more lots define a subdivision.

                            combination thereof, on which a development activity is proposed.
Subregional facility        “Subregional facility,” when referring to stormwater, means a facility
                            designed to treat and control stormwater runoff from more than one (1)
                            development in a contributing drainage area of less than forty (40) acres.
Substantial completion      “Substantial completion,” when referring to transportation facilities, means
                            that all public or private facilities are constructed, functional and
                            operational, even though they may not be fully completed nor
                            provisionally accepted, including sewer and water systems, storm drainage
                            facilities and street improvements (including construction of the initial lift
                            of asphalt or other approved surfacing), but not necessarily including
                            sidewalks, or electrical, gas, telephone or cable services; and that the
                            project is in full compliance with the erosion control ordinance.
Surface mining operations   “Surface mining operations” means mining of rock, stone, gravel, sand,
                            earth and minerals.
Temporary worker            “Temporary worker” means a person intermittently employed in
                            agriculture and not residing year round at the same site.
                                (Added: Ord. 2011-12-09)

Traffic calming devices     “Traffic calming devices” means physical devices within the roadway
                            designed to manage traffic speeds or which disperse traffic such as speed
                            bumps/humps and traffic circles.
Traffic calming measures    “Traffic calming measures” means street design features intended to
                            manage traffic speeds or which disperse traffic such as “T” intersection,
                            street trees, curvilinear streets, or entry treatments.
Trail                       “Trail” means any path, route, way, right-of-way, or corridor posted,
                            signed, or designated as open for nonmotorized travel or passage by the
                            general public. Five (5) trail types are identified in the Regional Trails and
                            Bikeways System Plan (2006) and include regional, multiuse trails, local
                            trails, rustic trails, semi-primitive trails, and bike lanes and pedestrian
                            walkways. Trails are defined as recreational facilities.
                                 (Amended: Ord. 2016-06-12; Ord. 2019-02-04)

Transitional housing        Transitional housing means a project that provides housing and
                            supportive services to homeless persons or families and that has as its 54
                            purpose facilitating the movement of homeless persons and families
                            into independent living, generally in less than two years. (RCW
                            84.36.043(3)(c))
Transportation analysis     “Transportation analysis” means a study done by a licensed engineer that
                            compares a build-out scenario under the existing and proposed
                            designations analyzing trip generation, modal split and distribution for a
                            current comprehensive planning twenty (20) year horizon.
                                (Amended: Ord. 2007-09-13; Ord. 2019-05-07)

Transportation corridor     “Transportation corridor” or “corridor” means an identified system of
                            road(s) and street(s), which are consistently utilized by vehicular traffic for
                            travel along an identified circulation pattern.
Transportation impact study “Transportation impact study” means a study done by a licensed engineer
                            in accordance with Section 40.350.020.
Transportation              “Transportation improvement program” means the current six (6) year
improvement program         financing plan for roads adopted by the county pursuant to RCW
                            36.81.121, or similar plan adopted by the Washington Department of
                            Transportation or cities for their highway and street facilities.
Transportation terminal     “Transportation terminal” means a facility for the transfer, pickup or
                            discharge of people or goods without the long-term storage of such items.
Travel trailer              “Travel trailer” means any transportable trailer available for recreational
                            use, forty (40) feet or less in length or eight (8) feet or less in width, built
                            on a chassis and equipped with wheels.
Underground storage tank    “Underground storage tank” or “UST” means:
(UST)                       • An underground storage tank and connected underground piping as
                            defined in the rules adopted under Chapter 90.76 RCW; or means any one
                            (1) or combination of tanks (including underground pipes connected
                            thereto) that are used to contain an accumulation of regulated substances,
                            and the volume of which (including the volume of underground pipes
                            connected thereto) is ten percent (10%) or more beneath the surface of the
                            ground. This term does not include any of the exempt UST systems
                            specified in WAC 173-360-110(2), or any piping connected thereto.

                            • Exemptions. The following UST systems, including any piping
                            connected thereto, are exempt from the requirement of this chapter:

                            o Any UST system holding hazardous wastes subject to Subtitle C of the
                            Federal Solid Waste Disposal Act, or a mixture of such hazardous waste
                            and other regulated substances;

                            o Any wastewater treatment tank system that is part of a wastewater
                            treatment facility regulated under Section 402 or 307(b) of the Clean Water
                            Act;

                            o Equipment or machinery that contains regulated substances for
                            operational purposes such as hydraulic lift tanks and electrical equipment
                            tanks;

                            o Any UST system whose capacity is one hundred ten (110) gallons or
                            less;

                            o Any UST system that contains a de minimis concentration of regulated
                            substances;                                                           55
                          o Any emergency spill or overflow containment UST system that is
                          expeditiously emptied after use;

                          o Farm or residential UST systems of one thousand one hundred (1,100)
                          gallons or less capacity used for storing motor fuel for noncommercial
                          purposes (i.e., not for resale);

                          o UST systems used for storing heating oil for consumptive use on the
                          premises where stored; except that such systems which store in excess of
                          one thousand one hundred (1,100) gallons are subject to the release
                          reporting requirements of WAC 173-360-372;

                          o Septic tanks;

                          o Any pipeline facility (including gathering lines) regulated under:

                          • The Natural Gas Pipeline Safety Act of 1968 (49 U.S.C. App. 1671 et
                          seq.); or

                          • The Hazardous Liquid Pipeline Safety Act of 1979 (49 U.S.C. App.
                          2001 et seq.); or

                          • Which is an intrastate pipeline facility regulated under state laws
                          comparable to the provisions of the law referred to in this definition;

                          o Surface impoundments, pits, ponds, or lagoons;

                          o Stormwater or wastewater collection systems;

                          o Flow-through process tanks;

                          o Liquid traps or associated gathering lines directly related to oil or gas
                          production and gathering operations; or

                          o Storage tanks situated in an underground area (such as a basement,
                          cellar, vault, mineworking drift, shaft, or tunnel) if the storage tank is
                          situated upon or above the surface of the floor.

Urban or urban area       “Urban” or “urban area” means land located within an urban area or
                          growth boundary as designated in the comprehensive plan.
Urban growth area (UGA)   “UGA” means an urban growth area designated in the comprehensive plan.
Use                       “Use” means an activity or purpose for which land or premises, or a

                          occupied or maintained, let or leased.
Use, conditional          “Conditional use” means a use specified by this title, which is permitted

Use, permitted            “Permitted use” means a use allowed as a matter of right within certain
                          land use districts; provided, that such use is in accordance with the

                          elsewhere in this title, and other applicable provisions of the county code.

Utility substation facilities   “Utility substation facilities” means a subsidiary or branch facility utilizing
                                aboveground structures which is necessary to provide or facilitate

Vegetation                      “Vegetation” means any and all plant life.
Vehicle repair                  “Vehicle repair” means upholstering of; replacement of parts for; motor

                                and overall painting or paint shop.
Vehicle towing and storage “Vehicle towing and storage services” means a service outlet providing
services                   vehicle towing and/or storage.
Visual obstruction              “Visual obstruction” means any fence, hedge, tree, shrub, device, wall, or
                                structure exceeding three and one-half (3 1/2) feet in height above the
                                elevation of the top of the curb, as determined by the responsible official;
                                and so located at a street or alley intersection as to dangerously limit the

Walkway                         “Walkway” means a facility dedicated to the county for pedestrian use to
                                or through a parcel for the use of the general public which is not generally
                                adjacent to the roadway.
Water-dependent                 “Water-dependent” means a use or a portion of a use which requires direct
                                contact with the water and cannot exist at a nonwater location due to the
                                intrinsic nature of its operations.
Watershed                       “Watershed” means an area draining to a single surface water system as

Wetland classes and             “Wetland classes and subclasses” means descriptive classes of the
subclasses                      wetlands taxonomic classification system of the United States Fish and
                                Wildlife Service (Cowardin et al., 1978).
Wetlands                        “Wetland” or “wetlands” means areas that are inundated or saturated by
                                surface or groundwater at a frequency and duration sufficient to support,
                                and that under normal circumstances do support, a prevalence of
                                vegetation typically adapted for life in saturated soil conditions. Wetlands
                                generally include swamps, marshes, bogs and similar areas. Wetlands do
                                not include those artificial wetlands intentionally created from nonwetland
                                sites, including, but not limited to, irrigation and drainage ditches, grass- 57
                               lined swales, canals, detention facilities, wastewater treatment facilities,
                               farm ponds, and landscape amenities, or those wetlands created after July
                               1, 1990, that were unintentionally created as a result of the construction of
                               a road, street or highway. Wetlands may include those artificial wetlands
                               intentionally created from nonwetland areas created to mitigate conversion
                               of wetlands.

4   B.     Zoning Maps.

5         1. Original Maps. The designations, locations and boundaries of the districts set forth in Chapter
6         40.200 shall be shown on the zoning maps of Clark County, Washington. Said maps and all            60
 1   notations, references, data and other information shown thereon shall be and are hereby adopted and
 2   made a part of this title. The signed copies of the zoning maps containing the zoning districts
 3   designated at the time of adoption of ordinance codified in this title shall be maintained without
 4   change on file in the office of the County Auditor. Any land or property not specifically identified
 5   with a zone designation shall be considered to be zoned as is the most restrictive zone classification
 6   designated on adjoining and/or abutting properties, until such time as it is determined otherwise by a
 7   rezone action.

 8   2. Revised Maps. Council may, from time to time, direct the County Auditor to replace the official
 9   zoning maps, or portions thereof, with a map or maps, or portions thereof, which include all lawful
10   changes of zone to date. Such maps, or portions thereof, filed as replacements, shall bear dated,
11   authenticating signatures of Council and County Auditor. Any maps or portions thereof thereby
12   replaced shall be retained in a separate file by the County Auditor. Any revisions or replacements of
13   said maps, when duly entered, signed, and filed with the County Auditor as authorized by this
14   chapter, are part of this title.

15   3. Copies of Maps. The responsible official shall maintain up-to-date copies of the zoning maps.
16   The responsible official shall cause the copies of the zoning maps to be revised so that they
17   accurately portray changes of zone boundaries.

 2   40.200.040 Minimum and Maximum Calculations
 3   A. General Rule.

 4        1. When determining maximum standards for planning related calculations (including density,
 5        parking setbacks, etc.), the final number in any calculation shall be rounded down to the nearest
 6        whole unit. However, if two (2) or more amounts must be added to figure a total, applicants shall use
 7        numbers accurate to two (2) decimal places (hundredths) when adding the amounts and round off
 8        only the total. For example, if the density for a project were determined to be fifteen and eighty-nine-
 9        hundredths (15.89) units, only fifteen (15) units would be permitted.

10        2. When determining minimum standards for planning related calculations (including density,
11        parking setbacks, etc.), the final number in any calculation shall be rounded up to the nearest whole
12        unit. However, if two (2) or more amounts must be added to figure a total, applicants shall use
13        numbers accurate to two (2) decimal places (hundredths) when adding the amounts and round off
14        only the total. For example, if the required parking for a project were determined to be fifteen and
15        eighty-nine-hundredths (15.89) spaces, a total of sixteen (16) spaces would be required.

16        3. These standards shall not apply for the purposes of engineering related calculations (surveying
17        information, stormwater calculations, road construction information, etc.).

18   B.   Density Calculations.

19        1. For all urban zoning districts with minimum densities except R1-5, R1-6, R1-7.5, R1-10 and R1-
20        20, the following shall apply:

21             a. Minimum density or floor area ratio will be based on the developable area of the lot that
22             remains after subtracting:

23                  (1) Land devoted to public right-of-way or private street easements including alleys, public
24                  parks and trails, required landscaping including required landscaping within setbacks, and
25                  drainageways;

26                  (2) Land designated by covenant or public dedication to be permanently maintained in an
27                  undeveloped state because the land is identified as sensitive due to the presence of steep
28                  slopes, unstable land, historical or archaeological sites, wetlands and buffers, or other
29                  permanent physical development limitations as may be determined by the responsible
30                  official. All other lands shall be considered in the calculation of minimum density including
31                  required setbacks not counted towards required landscaping private recreation or
32                  common areas or amenity areas under Section 40.260.150.

33             b. Maximum density or floor area ratio shall be calculated based upon the gross area of the site
34             lot, excluding public right-of-way or private street easements including alleys.

35        2. For the R1-5, R1-6, R1-7.5, R1-10 and R1-20 zoning districts, minimum and maximum densities
36        shall be calculated pursuant to the standards in Chapter 40.220.

37   (Amended: Ord. 2009-03-02)

38   C.   Lot Area Calculations.

39        1.   Lot area is the computed area contained within the lot lines.

40             a. In the urban area, except for the UH zones, lot area excludes street and alley rights-of-way,
41             street easements, and street tracts.
                                                                                                                    62
42             b.    In the Urban Reserve (UR-10 and UR-20), Urban Holding (UH-10 and UH-20), Rural (R-5,
 1       R-10 and R-20), Rural Center (RC-1 and RC-2.5), agricultural (AG-20 and AG-WL) and forest
 2       resource (FR-40 and FR-80) districts, lot area includes on-site road easements, and one-half (1/2)
 3       the width, or thirty (30) feet, whichever is less, of abutting public rights-of-way for perimeter
 4       streets, excluding limited access state or interstate highways.

 5       c.   Driveways are included in lot area in all zones.

 6   2. One (1) lot within a proposed subdivision, short plat or exempt division shall be considered in
 7   compliance with the minimum lot area requirements if it is within ten percent (10%) of the required
 8   lot area for the zone. To utilize this provision in the R1-5 and R1-6 zones, one lot may be excluded
 9   from the average minimum lot calculations and the ten percent (10%) lot area reduction may be
10   applied to the excluded lot. The provisions of this section shall not apply to developments utilizing
11   the following:

12       a.   Density transfer (Section 40.220.010(C)(5));

13       b.   Rural cluster (Section 40.210.020).

14   (Amended: Ord. 2005-06-09; Ord. 2006-11-15; Ord. 2007-11-13; Ord. 2009-06-01;
15   Ord. 2009-07-01; Ord. 2016-06-12; Ord. 2017-07-04; Ord. 2023-01-08)

 3   40.200.060 Exceptions to Height Limitations.
 4   A. Agricultural structures, water towers and tanks shall not exceed thirty-five (35) feet
 5   in height in all zones, unless they are located at least fifty (50) feet from all property
 6   lines.

 7   B. No height limit shall apply in any zone to chimneys, church spires, belfries, cupolas,
 8   domes, smoke-stacks, flagpoles, grain elevators, cooling towers, solar energy systems,
 9   monuments, fire house towers, masts, aerials, elevator shafts, and outdoor theater screens.

10   C. Subject to Section 40.310.010, signs are prohibited above thirty-five (35) feet in
11   height; provided, that the name of the theater may be allowed on outdoor theater screens
12   higher than thirty-five (35) feet.

13   D. For retrofits of existing buildings to be used for residential use and for new
14   construction or the retrofit of existing buildings meeting passive house
15   requirements, buildings may exceed the maximum height in all zones by eight (8)
16   inches to accommodate additional insulation.

 2   EXHIBIT 9


 3   40.200.070 Exceptions to Setback Requirements.
 4   A. Projections Into Required Setbacks. (Also see Sections 40.200.070(A)(7), 40.200.070(A)(8), and
 5   40.320.010(C)(9) for limitations.)

 6   Certain architectural features and structures may project into required setbacks, subject to applicable
 7   building and fire codes as follows:

 8       1.   Front Setback.

 9            a. Fire escapes, porches, balconies, decks, landing places, or outside stairways over thirty (30)
10            inches in height may project not more than six (6) feet into the required front setback, provided
11            such features are open-sided and are uncovered.

12            b. Projections including bay windows, overhanging breakfast nooks, cornices, canopies, eaves,
13            belt courses, sills or other similar architectural features and fireplaces may extend up to two (2)
14            feet into the required setback.

15            c. Porches, decks, landings or stairways not more than thirty (30) inches in height above
16            finished grade are allowed to within eighteen (18) inches of the front property line, provided
17            such features are open-sided and uncovered.

18       2.   Rear Setback.

19            a. Projections such as bay windows and overhanging breakfast nooks may extend up to two (2)
20            feet into the required setback, provided such features are limited to ten (10) horizontal feet per
21            projection and limited to thirty percent (30%) of the linear dimension of the rear building
22            elevation.

23            b. Cornices, canopies, eaves, belt courses, sills or other similar architectural features and fire-
24            places may extend up to two (2) feet into the required setback.

25            c. Porches, decks, landings or stairways not more than thirty (30) inches in height above
26            finished grade are allowed to within eighteen (18) inches of the rear property line, provided such
27            features are open-sided and uncovered.

28       3.   Side Setback.

29            a. The following features and structures may project into the required side setback not more
30            than two (2) feet; provided, that no portion of the structure may be less than three (3) feet to the
31            property line:

32                 (1) In the R1-5 and R1-6 zones only, projections such as bay windows and overhanging
33                 breakfast nooks may extend up to two (2) feet into the required setback, provided such
34                 features are limited to ten (10) horizontal feet per projection and limited to thirty percent
35                 (30%) of the linear dimension of the side building elevation;

36                 (2) Cornices, canopies, eaves, belt courses, sills or other similar architectural features;
37                 and

38                 (3)   Fireplaces.


                                                                                                                   65
 1        b. Porches, decks, landings or stairways not more than thirty (30) inches in height above
 2        finished grade are allowed to within eighteen (18) inches of the side property line, provided such
 3        features are open-sided and uncovered.

 4   4. Garden Sheds, Gazebos and Playhouses. One (1) garden shed, or one (1) gazebo or one (1)
 5   playhouse meeting all of the following requirements may be located in either the side or rear setback
 6   of single-family residential districts (R1-5, R1-6, R1-7.5, R1-10 and R1-20):

 7        a. The structure contains no more than two hundred (200) square feet of floor area, with
 8        overhangs that do not exceed sixteen (16) inches;

 9        b.   The structure is set back from property lines a minimum of two (2) feet;

10        c.   The floor elevation is eighteen (18) inches or less in height;

11        d.   The structure is less than twelve (12) feet in height;

12        e.   Roof drainage is contained on site; and

13        f.   No utilities are connected to the structure.

14        g. If the structure is located within a utility easement, the property owner must obtain a waiver
15        letter from all applicable utilities.

16   5. Aboveground rainwater cisterns in all urban residential districts are allowed reduced setbacks
17   subject to the following:

18        a. Cisterns six (6) feet in height or less are allowed a setback of three (3) feet to an interior side
19        or rear property line;

20        b. Other reductions from standard setbacks shall be limited to the height of the cistern. For
21        example, a cistern eight (8) feet high is allowed a setback of no less than eight (8) feet to a rear
22        property line in the R1-20 zone; provided, that the maximum height of any cistern under this
23        provision shall not exceed twelve (12) feet;

24        c. If the cistern is located within a utility easement, the property owner must obtain a waiver
25        letter from all applicable utilities;

26        d. Appropriate design and support to ensure the cistern does not affect on-site or off-site
27        foundations, retaining walls, or other structures are required;

28        e.   All other applicable building and plumbing codes shall apply.

29   6.   Aboveground utilities.

30   7. Retrofits of existing buildings to be used for residential use and for new construction or the
31   retrofit of existing buildings meeting passive house requirements may project into setbacks subject to
32   the following:

33        a. The portion of exterior wall assemblies that include insulation may project up to eight (8)
34        inches into setbacks on all sides to accommodate additional insulation.

35        b. For existing nonconforming buildings already projecting into setbacks, the portion of
36        exterior wall assemblies that include insulation may project up to an additional eight (8) inches
37        into the setbacks on all sides.

38        c.   Setbacks shall be measured to the outside face of the foundation.

39   7 8. The allowed projections noted above may not apply to landscape buffers. (See Section                   66
40   40.320.010(C)(9).)
 1        8 9. The allowed projections noted above apply to building and development envelopes except
 2        when limited by the following:

 3            a. The applicable envelope line reflects a critical area or an easement;

 4            b. The applicable envelope line reflects a landscape buffer, except as allowed by Section
 5            40.320.010(C)(9); and

 6            c.   Information on a site plan or plat specifically prohibits such projections.

 7        9 10. Fences and retaining walls, except as specified in Section 40.320.010(F) may be located in
 8        any setback.

 9   B.   Exceptions to Front Setback Requirements.

10        1. If there are dwellings on both abutting lots with front setbacks less than the required depth for
11        the district, the front setback for the lot need not exceed the average front setback of the abutting
12        dwellings.

13        2. If there is a dwelling on one (1) abutting lot with a front setback less than the required depth for
14        the district, the front setback need not exceed a depth of halfway between the depth of the front
15        setback on the abutting lot and the required front setback depth.

16        3. In residential zoning districts, where a temporary turnaround easement constitutes all or part of
17        the front lot line, front setbacks shall be a minimum of ten (10) feet from the temporary easement for
18        the dwelling and fifteen (15) feet for the associated garage. Front setbacks from access and right-of-
19        way easements intended to be permanent shall continue to be as stipulated by the applicable zoning
20        district standard.

21        4. Setbacks from both streets on through lots shall be considered front setbacks. However, if one
22        (1) of the streets has restricted access, the setback from the restricted access street shall be considered
23        a rear setback. Access is considered restricted to a street if any of the conditions below exist:

24            a.   A plat contains a note prohibiting access to one (1) of the abutting streets;

25            b. The road is clearly determined to be a restricted access road, such as but not limited to I-5, I-
26            205, SR-14, SR-500, and Padden Expressway;

27            c. A covenant permanently restricting access to one (1) of the abutting streets (with the county
28            being a party to the recorded document) is recorded individually for a through lot; or

29            d. Topographic constraints exist that prohibit access to the rear street based upon the
30            responsible official’s judgment.

 1   EXHIBIT 10
 2
 3   40.210.010. FOREST, AGRICULTURE AND AGRICULTURAL-WILDLIFE DISTRICTS (FR-80,
 4   FR-40, AG-20, AG-WL).
 5           A. Purpose.
 6                1. Forest 80 District. The purpose of the Forest 80 district is to maintain and enhance
 7                     resource-based industries, encourage the conservation of productive forest lands and
 8                     discourage incompatible uses consistent with the Forest I policies of the
 9                     comprehensive plan. The Forest 80 district applies to lands which have been
10                     designated as Forest Tier 1 on the comprehensive plan. Nothing in this chapter shall be
11                     construed in a manner inconsistent with the Washington Forest Practices Act.
12                2. Forest 40 District. The purpose of the Forest 40 district is to encourage the
13                     conservation of lands which have the physical characteristics that are capable of
14                     management for the long-term production of commercially significant forest products
15                     and other natural resources, such as minerals.
16                3. Agriculture 20 District. The purpose of the Agriculture 20 district is to encourage the
17                     conservation of lands which have the growing capacity, productivity, soil composition,
18                     and surrounding land use to have long-term commercial significance for agriculture
19                     and associated resource production.
20                4. Agricultural-Wildlife. The purpose of the AG-WL district is to encourage the
21                     preservation of agricultural and wildlife use on land which is suited for agricultural
22                     production, and to protect agricultural areas that are highly valuable seasonal wildlife
23                     habitat from incompatible uses. The district provides for activities which can be
24                     considered accessory only to agricultural, game, or wildlife habitat management, or
25                     recreational uses. Nothing in this chapter shall be construed to restrict normal
26                     agricultural practices.
27           B. Uses.
28                The uses set out in Table 40.210.010-1 are examples of uses allowable in the various
29                resource zone districts. The appropriate review authority is mandatory.
30
31                 •     “P” – Uses allowed subject to approval of applicable permits.
32                 •     “R/A” – Uses permitted upon review and approval as set forth in Section 40.520.020.
33                 •     “C” – Conditional uses which may be permitted subject to the approval of a
34                       conditional use permit as set forth in Section 40.520.030.
35                 •     “X” – Uses specifically prohibited.
36                 Where there are special use standards or restrictions for a listed use, the applicable code
37                 section(s) in Chapter 40.260, Special Uses and Standards, or other applicable chapter is
38                 noted in the “Special Standards” column.

 1   3.    Previous Land Divisions.
 2         a. Within the FR-80, FR-40 and AG-20 districts, until the affected property is included
 3              within an urban growth boundary, no remainder lot of a previously approved
 4              agriculture or forest district “cluster” land division or lot reconfiguration shall be:
 5               (1) Further subdivided or reduced in size below seventy percent (70%) of the total
 6                     developable area of the original parent parcel constituting the cluster
 7                     subdivision; or
 8               (2) Reduced by a total of more than one (1) acre.
 9         b. Applications for reduction in remainder lot size consistent with this provision shall be
10              processed as a plat alteration pursuant to Section 40.540.120.
11         c. Exceptions to subsections (C)(3)(a) and (C)(3)(b) of this section. A remainder lot with
12              an existing residence may be short platted further to contain the residence on its own
13              lot, subject to the following:
14             (1) Process. Creation of the new lot is subject to the requirements of Section
15                    40.540.030.
16             (2) Lot Size. The new lot shall be sized to require the minimum reduction in the
17                    remainder lot, but still meet minimum requirements of this section and for on-site
18                    sewage disposal as required by Clark County Public Health.
19             (3) The new lot may not include critical areas unless no other alternative exists. If no
20                    alternative is available, encroachment into these areas shall be limited to the least
21                    amount possible consistent with applicable critical areas ordinances.
22             (4) A building envelope containing the existing residence and accessory buildings
23                    shall be established within the new lot, subject to the following:
24                    (a) A minimum one hundred (100) foot setback between the envelope and the
25                          remainder parcel is maintained, unless it can be shown that a lesser setback
26                          with existing or proposed landscaping or existing vegetation will provide
27                          the same or greater buffering. In no case shall a setback less than fifty (50)
28                          feet be approved.
29                    (b) A minimum twenty (20) foot setback between the envelope and other
30                          cluster lots is maintained.
31             (5) A note shall be placed on the plat stating the following:
32                    The residential property is adjacent to agricultural or forest lands
33                    on which a variety of resource-related activities may occur that are not
34                    compatible with residential development. Potential discomforts or inconvenience
35                    may include, but are not limited to: noise, odors, fumes, dust, smoke, insects,
36                    operation of machinery (including aircraft) during any twenty-four (24) hour
37                    period, storage and disposal of manure, and the application by spraying or
38                    otherwise of chemical fertilizers, soil amendments, herbicides and pesticides.
39             (6) An open space, farm or forest management plan is required for the remainder
40                    parcel, which shall prohibit additional residential development. The plan shall be
41                    submitted and approved with the preliminary application. The plan shall identify
42                    permitted uses and management of the parcel so that it maintains its open space
43                    or other designated functions and provides for the protection of all critical areas.
44                    The management plan shall identify the responsibility for maintaining the
45                    remainder parcel. The plan shall also include any construction activities (trails,
46                    fencing, agricultural buildings) and vegetation clearing that may occur on site.
47                    All subsequent activities must be conducted in conformance with the approved
48                    management plan. Management plans may be modified through a Type II
49                    process. A note shall be placed on the plat and a restrictive covenant shall be
50                    recorded that clearly states that only the above uses are permitted on the
51                    remainder parcel. The note and covenant shall also incorporate the management
52                    plan, as described above.

      1                   minimize glare visible from streets, nearby dwellings, and above the fixtures.
      2       D. Nonconforming Lots – Lot Reconfiguration Standards.
      3            1. Purpose. It is in the public interest to encourage the protection of sensitive lands, expand
      4                 the amount of commercially viable resource land under single ownership, reduce the
      5                 amount of road and utility construction and, within the FR-80, FR-40 and AG-20 districts,
      6                 to protect and buffer designated resource lands.
      7            2. Lot Reconfiguration. Except for previously approved agricultural or forest zoned clusters or
      8                 rural residential planned unit developments, these substandard lots may be modified where
      9                 consistent with the following criteria. Parcels which meet all of the following criteria are
     10                 eligible for reconfiguration and reduction in size subject to a Type II review:
     11                 a. Existing parcel(s) is:
     12                      (1) Smaller than the minimum lot size established for new lots in the applicable
     13                             zoning district. Parcels which meet the minimum lot size
14    may be adjusted as a part of this process, but may not be decreased below the established minimum lot size.
15                           (2) Determined to be legally created, and be reasonably buildable. Within the FR-80, FR-
16                                 40 and AG-20 districts, this section authorizes lot reconfiguration only where existing
17                                 divisions are determined to have a reasonable probability of developing. For the
18                                 purposes of this section the review authority shall determine whether the existing lots
19                                 are reasonably buildable by considering the following: road access, septic suitability,
20                                 topography, costs of providing infrastructure and the presence of sensitive land.
21                     b. Proposed parcel(s) results in the following:
22                         (1) No additional parcels;
23                         (2) Have septic suitability approval;
24                         (3) Have adequate potable water at the time of occupancy, subject to Section 40.370.020;
25                         (4) Each resulting legal nonconforming parcel shall be at least one (1) acre in size with a
26                               minimum width of at least one hundred forty
27                               (140) feet; and
28                         (5) In addition, within the FR-80, FR-40 and AG-20 districts:
29                                (a) The location of the resulting reconfigured lots shall have the least impact on
30                                      sensitive and resource lands;
31                                (b) Access to reconfigured lots shall meet the minimum standards necessary to obtain
32                                      a building permit;
33                                (c) The remainder lot shall not be further subdivided or reduced in size unless the
34                                      affected property is included within an urban growth boundary;
35                                (d) Reconfigured lots shall not be further adjusted by boundary line adjustment
36                                      without approval under this section.
37                      c. Reconfigured lots shall result in achieving one (1) or more of the identified public interest
38                            issues in Section 40.210.010(D)(1).
39                 3. Lot Requirements. The setback, dimensional, use and height standards for these lots shall be as
40                      established for the Rural-5 (R-5) district except that reductions in side and rear setbacks shall be
41                      granted where necessary to permit construction of a dwelling on the parcel; providing, when the
42                      parcel is abutting, or surrounded by, property zoned for resource uses, the minimum setback
43                      from those property lines shall be fifty (50) feet for all structures.
44                 4. The review authority may impose conditions on the lot reconfiguration tofurther the purposes of
45                      this section.
46                 5. Lot reconfigurations shall be finalized upon the filing of a record of survey or covenant.
47           E. Land Divisions in the AG-20 and FR-40 Zones.
48                 1. Applicability.
49                      a. The provisions of this subsection shall apply to all land divisions in the AG-20 and FR-40
50                            zoning districts after July 1, 2016.
51                      b. Available options for land division are authorized:
52                            (1) Pursuant to Chapter 40.540; or.
53                            (2) Pursuant to Chapter 40.540 and by using the cluster provisions in Section           76
54                                   40.210.010(E)(4).
 1        c.   In the AG-20 zoning district:
 2              (1) Land divisions that result in parcels twenty (20) acres (or lots capable of being
 3                    described as one-thirty-second (¹⁄₃₂) of a section) in size or larger are allowed under
 4                    the exemption provisions of Section 40.540.020(B)(4)(b).
 5              (2) Land divisions that result in parcels less than twenty (20) acres in size must be
 6                    platted and meet the additional requirements of this chapter.
 7        d. In the FR-40 zoning district, land divisions that result in parcels less than forty (40) acres
 8             in size must be platted and meet the additional requirements of this chapter.
 9        e. Previously approved cluster or lot reconfiguration remainder lots are not eligible to use the
10             provisions of this section.
11   2.   Definitions. For the purposes of this subsection, the following definitions shall apply:
12
           Critical lands          “Critical lands” mean those lands classified by Subtitle 40.4.
           Remainder parcel        “Remainder parcel” means the remainder parcel of the cluster
                                   subdivision that contains the majority of the land within the
                                   development and is devoted to resource or open space use.

13
14   3.    Development Standards for Subdivisions or Short Plats. Subdivisions and short plats are allowed
15         pursuant to Chapter 40.540. The density shall be based on one hundred percent (100%) of the
16         gross area of the site.
17   4.    Development Standards for Clustering.
18        (1) Cluster developments are allowed at a maximum density equivalent to that which would be
19             permitted by applying the otherwise applicable minimum lot size requirements of this
20             section. The density shall be based on one hundred percent (100%) of the gross area of the
21             site.
22        (2) Cluster lots shall be created, as follows:
23             (a) To minimize conflicts between housing and agricultural or forest uses;
24             (b) Along parent property boundary lines, adjacent to existing roads, and to minimize the
25                   need for new roads and driveways;
26             (c) To have building envelopes that avoid critical areas;
27             (d) On parcels with an existing house, one (1) of the cluster lots has to include the existing
28                   house;
29             (e) To be adjacent to each other and to any preexisting residence, unless the location of
30                   the existing residence would preclude compliance with the other provisions of this
31                   subsection;
32             (f) If located on agriculturally zoned land, to be limited to lands with poor soils or soils
33                   otherwise unsuitable for agricultural purposes; and
34             (g) Each cluster lot shall contain a buffer from abutting resource uses.
35        (3) Remainder Parcel.
36             (a) The remainder parcel shall be contiguous. Fragmentation of the parcel by public or
37                   private road easements and/or building sites shall not occur unless no other reasonable
38                   alternative exists. Remainder parcels shall also be located adjacent to other bordering
39                   remainder parcels or public parks and open space, if practical.
40             (b) The remainder parcel shall be nonbuildable and used for the agriculture and forestry
41                   uses as listed in Table 40.210.010-1(8)(a), (8)(b) and (8)(d), or as open space.
42             (c) A farm or forest management plan is required for the remainder parcel. The plan shall
43                   be submitted and approved with the preliminary application. The plan shall:
44         •    Identify permitted uses and management of the parcel so that it maintains designated

 8                c.     Design Requirements. The design requirements for cluster developments are listed below. These
 9                       requirements shall be recorded on the plat.
10                       (1) No entryway treatments, monument or other permanent development signs are permitted.
11                             This shall not be construed to prohibit landscaping.
12                       (2) To the maximum practicable extent, existing historic rural features shall be preserved as
13                             part of the cluster development. These features include but are not limited to rock walls,
14                             fences, functional and structurally safe farm buildings, monuments and landscape features.
15                d.     Landscaping Standards. Cluster developments shall be landscaped within the cluster lots to
16                       reduce views of the development from public right(s)-of-way, so that a filtered view is provided
17                       of the cluster and the cluster does not dominate the landscape.
18                       (1) At a minimum, proposed or existing landscaping and vegetation shall be of sufficient size
19                             and type to provide a buffer of vegetation six (6) feet in height and fifty percent (50%)
20                             opaque year-round within three (3) years of planting. New landscaping materials shall
21                             consist of native vegetation as provided on the Clark County plant list (see the Standard
22                             Details Manual). A combination of trees and shrubs must be used.
23                       (2) All landscaping shall be installed prior to final plat unless financial guarantees are made for
24                             its installation prior to any building permit activity. Any required landscaping materials that
25                             fail to survive within the first two (2) years shall be promptly replaced.
26                e.     Notice of Resource Activities. For any areas abutting property zoned for agricultural or forestry
27                       uses, the following notice shall be recorded as part of the developer covenants to Clark County
28                       for each parcel within the cluster:
29
30                       The subject property is adjacent to commercial agricultural or forest lands on which a variety of
31                       commercial activities may occur that are not compatible with residential development. Potential
32                       discomforts or inconvenience may include, but are not limited to: noise, odors, fumes, dust,
33                       smoke, insects, operation of machinery (including aircraft) during any twenty-four (24) hour
34                       period, storage and disposal of manure, and the application by spraying or otherwise of chemical
35                       fertilizers, soil amendments, herbicides and pesticides.

 1   EXHIBIT 11


 2   40.220.010 LOW-DENSITY Residential Districts (R1-20, R1-10, R1-7.5, R1-6 and R1-5)
 3   A. Purpose.

 4        1.   The R1-20, R1-10, R1-7.5, R1-6 and R1-5 districts are intended to:

 5             a. Provide for a mix of single-family and middle housing types in a low-density context at a
 6             neighborhood scale.

 7             b. Establish higher densities where a full range of community services and facilities are present or will
 8             be present at the time of development.

 9             c. Provide for additional related uses such as schools, parks and utility uses necessary to serve immediate
10             residential areas.

11   B.   Uses.

12     The uses set out in Table 40.220.010-1 are examples of uses allowable in single-family residential zone districts.
13   The appropriate review authority is mandatory.

14        •    “P” Uses allowed subject to approval of applicable permits.

15        •    “R/A” Uses permitted upon review and approval as set forth in Section 40.520.020.

16        •    “C” Conditional uses which may be permitted subject to the approval of a conditional use permit as set
17             forth in Section 40.520.030.

18        •    “X” Uses specifically prohibited.

19   Where there are special use standards or restrictions for a listed use, the applicable code section(s) in Chapter
20   40.260, Special Uses and Standards, or other applicable chapter is noted in the “Special Standards” column.

 9   C.   Development Standards.

10        1. New lots and structures and additions to structures subject to this chapter shall comply with the applicable
11        standards for lots, building height and setbacks in Tables 40.220.010-2 and 40.220.010-3, subject to the
12        provisions of Chapter 40.200 and Section 40.550.020, except that single-family attached (townhouse) lots and
13        structures shall comply with the applicable standards in Table 40.220.010-4.

14        2. An exception to the maximum average lot size may be granted for a short plat creating lot(s) for an
15        existing legal residence(s) and one (1) remainder lot subject to the following:

16             a. For a two (2) lot short plat with one (1) existing residence, neither the lot with the residence nor the
17             remainder lot must meet the maximum average lot area.

18             b. When three (3) or more lots are created, only those lots with existing residences are exempted from
19             maximum lot area average calculations.

20             c. The resulting plat shall contain a plat note specifying that this exception may not be used for any
21             further divisions of the subject lots.

22        3. Lots created for drainage facilities, parks, open space, wetlands and buffers or utilities shall not be subject
23        to maximum lot size requirements.

       The maximum and minimum density is for the purpose of calculating densities for planned
 3   unit developments, cottage housing and compact lot developments. Densities shall be
 4   calculated based on the gross area of the site minus any public rights-of-way, private road
 5   easements, or street tracts. The minimum density shall be calculated based on the total
 6   number of dwelling units. For developments that include middle housing, the maximum
 7   density shall be calculated on an equivalent lot area basis as follows: The adjusted gross area,
 8   in acres, times the maximum density times the minimum lot area for single-family detached
 9   dwellings in the zone shall equal the total maximum allowed lot area, which may be divided
10   into any combination of middle housing lots, each meeting the applicable minimum lot size for

         Additional requirements may apply, based on building and fire codes.

 6   4.     Density Transfer for Single-Family Detached Dwellings.

 7          a. Purpose. To achieve the density goals of the comprehensive plan with respect to the urban area, while
 8          preserving environmentally sensitive lands and the livability of the low-density residential neighborhoods,
 9          while also maintaining compatibility with existing residences.

10          b. The density for property developed in low-density zone districts for single-family detached dwellings,
11          if encumbered by land identified as sensitive due to the presence of steep slopes, unstable land, historical
12          or archaeological sites, wetlands and buffers, regional stormwater facilities, or other permanent physical
13          development limitations as may be determined by the responsible official or land voluntarily set aside for
14          open space or commons as approved by the responsible official, from the gross acreage may be transferred
15          to the remaining unencumbered land areas on the same development site, subject to the following
16          limitations:

17             (1) Easements established for utility transmission lines such as Bonneville Power Administration
18             (BPA), PacifiCorp a.k.a. Pacific Power (formally known as PP&L), Clark Public Utilities, and NW
19             Natural can not be utilized for density transfer.

20             (2) Maximum Number. The maximum number of units that can be achieved on the site is based on
21             the density in Table 40.220.010-5, multiplied by the gross acreage of the entire site, both encumbered
22             and unencumbered, without deducting for road easements or right-of-way. The potential maximum
23             number of lots will be dependent upon site characteristics and the lot requirements of Table
24             40.220.010-5.

25             (3) The minimum lot depth of any lot abutting environmentally sensitive lands shall be fifty-five (55)
26             feet.

27             (4)   For parent parcels larger than two and one-half (2.5) acres:

28                   (a) The resulting lots which abut R1-5, R1-6, R1-7.5, R1-10 or R1-20 zoned lots or parcels
29                   shall:

30                        (i) Be at least ninety percent (90%) of the minimum lot area standard for the subject parcel;

31                        (ii) Have a lot depth of not less than eighty percent (80%) of the minimum lot depth of the
32                        subject parcel;

33                        (iii) Have a minimum lot width not less than ten (10) feet from the minimum lot width of
34                        the subject parcel.

35                   (b) The resulting lots which are interior (not a part of the parent parcel abutting an adjacent
36                   property line) to the site shall conform to the lot requirements set out in Table 40.220.010-4.

37             (5) For parent parcels two and one-half (2.5) acres or less, all lots, both exterior and interior, to be
38             created shall conform to the lot requirements in Table 40.220.010-5.




                                                                                                                    90
 1                  (6) This density transfer development provision may not be used in association with the planned unit
 2                  development provisions of Section 40.520.080, the compact lot development provisions of Section
 3                  40.260.072, or the cottage housing provisions of Section 40.260.073.

 4                  (7) A recorded covenant shall be placed on those areas or tracts from which density is transferred
 5                  prohibiting any development of the parcel or tract inconsistent with its intended use.

 6                  (8) Subdivision lots shall be subject to the maximum average lot area provisions of the underlying
 7                  zone.
