           Minimum usable area is that portion of the lot which is unencumbered by the land voluntarily
11        set aside, environmentally sensitive lands to be protected and their respective buffers, and shall
12        exclude setbacks and easements. [Example: A typical five thousand (5,000) square foot lot
13        would have three thousand (3,000) square feet of usable area, even if unencumbered by
14        environmentally sensitive lands.]

           The maximum density rates apply to the overall gross acreage of the site, whether
17        encumbered or not. The actual maximum number of lots that may be achieved is also
18        dependent on the other requirements of this table. The maximum density rates are based upon
19        dividing the gross acres by the minimum lot size in the zone, minus twenty percent (20%)
20        which would normally be devoted to road right-of-way or road easements in a typical
21        subdivision.

26        due to minimum lot size requirements but would not meet maximum lot size requirements for a two (2) lot
27        division.

 1   EXHIBIT 12


 2   40.220.020 Residential and Office Residential Districts (R, OR)
 3   A. Purpose.

 4        1. The residential (R-12, R-18, R-22, R-30 and R-43 R-24, R-36, R-50) districts are intended to provide for
 5        medium and higher density residential development based upon consistency with the comprehensive plan and
 6        compatibility with surrounding land uses. The following factors will be considered in the application of one (1)
 7        of these districts to a particular site:

 8            a. Properties designated urban medium density residential on the comprehensive plan should not exceed
 9            a density of R-22 be zoned for R-24 or R-36 districts. Urban high density residential areas are
10            appropriate for densities in the R-30 and R-43 districts the R-50 district.

11            b. Proximity to major streets and the available capacity of these streets, adequacy of public water and
12            sewer, vehicular and pedestrian traffic circulation in the area, proximity to commercial services and
13            proximity to public open space and recreation opportunities. Development within these districts will be
14            reviewed to ensure compatibility with adjacent uses including such considerations as privacy, noise,
15            lighting and design.

16        2. The office residential (OR-15, OR-18, OR-22, OR-30, OR-43 OR-24, OR-36, OR-50) districts are
17        intended to provide for residential and professional office development based upon consistency with the
18        comprehensive plan and compatibility with adjacent land uses. It is intended that office developments within
19        these districts will be of a higher standard in recognition of their residential setting. The following factors will
20        be considered in the application of one (1) of these districts to a particular site:

21            a. Properties designated urban medium density residential on the comprehensive plan should be
22            zoned for OR-24 or OR-36 districts. Urban high density residential areas are appropriate for the
23            OR-50 district.

24            a b.   Proximity to major streets and the available capacity of these streets;

25            b c.   Availability of public water and sewer;

26            c d.   Vehicular and pedestrian traffic circulation in the area;

27            d e.   Proximity to commercial services; and

28            e f.   Proximity to public open space and recreation opportunities.

29     Development within these districts will be reviewed to ensure compatibility with adjacent uses, including such
30   considerations as privacy, noise, lighting and design.

31   B.   Uses.

32      The uses set out in Table 40.220.020-1 are examples of uses allowable in residential and office residential zone
33   districts. The appropriate review authority is mandatory.

34        •   “P” Uses allowed subject to approval of applicable permits.

35        •   “R/A” Uses permitted upon review and approval as set forth in Section 40.520.020.




                                                                                                                        92
1       •   “C” Conditional uses which may be permitted subject to the approval of a conditional use permit as set
2           forth in Section 40.520.030.

3       •   “X” Uses specifically prohibited.

4   Where there are special use standards or restrictions for a listed use, the applicable code section(s) in Chapter
5   40.260, Special Uses and Standards, or other applicable chapter is noted in the “Special Standards” column.

8   C.   Development Standards.




                                                                                                                98
 1   1. With the exception of mobile manufactured home parks, new lots and structures and additions to
 2   structures subject to this section shall comply with the applicable standards for minimum and maximum
 3   density, lots, building height, setbacks and building separation in Tables 40.220.020-2 through and
 4   40.220.020-5 3. Mobile Manufactured home parks shall not be required to comply with the minimum density
 5   standards set out in this section, but are required to meet all other applicable standards.

 6   2. Multifamily, single-family attached (townhouses), and single-family detached dDevelopments are
 7   each subject to different standards, described in Tables 40.220.020-2 through 40.220.020-5 and 40.220.020-
 8   3, including replacement single-family detached dwellings and additions to existing single-family
 9   detached dwellings.

10   3. The development of single-family detached lots where permitted in these zones shall require
11   setbacks to be shown on the final plat. In addition, lots less than forty (40) feet wide are also subject to
12   the narrow lot standards in Section 40.260.155.

13   4. All townhome developments shall require submittal of a site plan that shows proposed building
14   envelopes and driveway locations. In addition, lots less than forty (40) feet wide are also subject to the
15   narrow lot standards in Section 40.260.155.

16   5. Replacement single-family detached structures and additions to existing single-family structures in
17   the R-30, OR-30, R-43 and OR-43 zones shall use the setbacks for multifamily dwellings in Table
18   40.220.020-3.

       Applies to interior lots only - widths of corner lots shall be dependent upon the applicable narrow lot
 8   standards in Section 40.260.155(C)(2).

12   6. Minimum and Maximum Densities within the R-12, R-18, R-22, R-30, R-43 R and All OR Districts. For
13   uses other than mobile manufactured home parks, the review authority shall find that the established
14   minimum densities will be achieved by all proposed land divisions or site plans for each individual phase and



                                                                                                                 102
 1   at total project buildout. The applicant shall demonstrate that all required densities will be achieved. The
 2   review authority shall establish appropriate conditions to ensure density is achieved. Demonstration that the
 3   established minimum densities will be achieved shall be provided by the applicant as follows:

 4       a. Where single-family or duplex developments at densities below the minimum density are
 5       proposed for a portion of the project. The minimum density to be transferred to a higher density
 6       multifamily development elsewhere on the site may be approved; provided, that:

 7          (1)     The application shall provide for a fully integrated design; or

 8          (2)     The development shall provide for phasing in which each phase meets the minimum density;
 9          or

10          (3) The development shall provide a site plan for preliminary approval of the remainder of the
11          site, which shall be binding on the land owner and all future land owners. The binding document
12          shall be a deed covenant, approved by the county. The covenant shall require that the approved
13          site plan for the multifamily development will be constructed at the densities approved. Where
14          any portion of the project exceeds either the density of the zoning district or twenty-one (21) units
15          per acre, a market analysis shall be provided demonstrating that such a project is economically
16          feasible in the county. Where a difference in density of over three (3) units per acre is proposed
17          between developments in the site plan, the applicant shall provide deed covenants which notify
18          the future owners of the lower density developments of the type and density of the adjoining
19          development.

20       b. For all other projects including multifamily and detached single-family structures the density of
21       the project for each individual phase and at total project buildout shall meet the minimum density.
22       In order to reduce impacts to and from adjacent properties, multifamily developments adjacent to
23       industrial uses should include the following design standards:

24          (1) Aspects of the development that will not be utilized for residential purposes should be
25          located adjoining to the industrially zoned property where possible. Examples of such uses
26          include parking, garages, access roads, required open space, carports and stormwater facilities.

27          (2) If residential buildings must be placed adjoining to the industrially zoned property, single-
28          story buildings should be used. If multiple-story structures are proposed building openings above
29          the first floor should face away from the adjoining property in order to limit views and exposure
30          to the adjacent use.

31       c a.     Minimum density will be based on the developable area of the lot that remains after subtracting:

32          (1) Land devoted to public or private roads or right-of-way or private street easements including
33          alleys, common parking areas and required sight distance triangles required for narrow lots
34          under Section 40.260.155, public parks and trails, required landscaping including required
35          landscaping within setbacks, and drainageways;

36          (2) Land designated by covenant or public dedication to be permanently maintained in an
37          undeveloped state because the land is identified as sensitive due to the presence of steep slopes,
38          unstable land, historical or archaeological sites, wetlands and buffers, or other permanent physical
39          development limitations as may be determined by the responsible official. All other lands shall be
40          considered in the calculation of minimum density or floor area ratio including required setbacks not
41          counted towards required landscaping private recreation or common areas or amenity areas
42          under Section 40.260.150.




                                                                                                              103
 1        d e. Maximum density shall be calculated based upon the gross area of the site lot, excluding public
 2        right-of-way or street easements including alleys.

 3   7. In order to reduce impacts to and from adjacent properties, multifamily developments adjacent to
 4   industrial uses should include the following design standards:

 5        a. Aspects of the development that will not be utilized for residential purposes should be located
 6        adjoining to the industrially zoned property where possible. Examples of such uses include parking,
 7        garages, access roads, required open space, carports and stormwater facilities.

 8        b. If residential buildings must be placed adjoining to the industrially zoned property, single-story
 9        buildings should be used. If multiple-story structures are proposed building openings above the first
10        floor should face away from the adjoining property in order to limit views and exposure to the
11        adjacent use.

12   7.   Signs. Signs shall be permitted according to the provisions of Chapter 40.310.

13   8. Off-Street Parking. Off-street parking shall be provided as required in Chapter 40.340 and Section
14   40.260.155 as applicable.

15   9. Landscaping. Within the R-12, R-18, R-22, R-30 and R-43 and all R and OR Office Residential
16   districts, a minimum of twenty percent (20%) fifteen percent (15%) of the site lot area shall be landscaped
17   to an a minimum L1 standard as described in Section 40.320.010. Additional setbacks and/or landscape
18   requirements may apply, particularly adjoining residential uses or zones pursuant to Section 40.520.040 and
19   Chapter 40.320. Setbacks that are landscaped to a minimum L1 standard may be counted towards
20   meeting the minimum site landscaping.

21   10. Recreation Space Amenity Areas. Recreation space Amenity areas shall be provided as required in
22   Section 40.260.150. Amenity areas shall not be counted towards the minimum site landscaping.

23   11. Safe pedestrian routes, including sidewalks and other planning features, shall be provided for students
24   who only walk to and from school.

25   12. Townhouses shall be assessed school and park impact fees at the multifamily rate. Traffic impact
26   fees for townhouses are assessed at their own rate.

                                                                                                             104
 1   EXHIBIT 13


 2   40.230.010 Commercial Districts (NC, CC, GC)
 3   A. Purpose.

 4          1. Neighborhood Commercial (NC) District. These commercial areas of limited size are intended to provide
 5          for the convenience shopping needs of the immediate neighborhood. This district is permitted under the
 6          neighborhood commercial and mixed use comprehensive plan designations.

 7          2. Community Commercial (CC) District. These commercial areas are intended to provide for the regular
 8          shopping and service needs for several adjacent neighborhoods. This district is only permitted in areas
 9          designated as community commercial or mixed use on the comprehensive plan.

10          3. General Commercial (GC) District. These commercial areas provide a full range of goods and services
11          necessary to serve large areas of the county and the traveling public. This district is limited to the general
12          commercial comprehensive plan designation.

13         (Amended: Ord. 2008-12-15; Ord. 2019-07-01; Ord. 2020-03-08; Ord. 2024-12-16)

14   B.     Definitions.

15        For the purposes of this section, the following definitions shall apply:

16          1.   Streets. In commercial zones, the definition of “street” shall be limited to public rights-of-way.

17         (Amended: Ord. 2019-07-01; Ord. 2020-03-08; Ord. 2024-12-16)

18   C.     Uses.

19     The uses set out in Table 40.230.010-1 are examples of uses allowable in the various zone districts. The
20   appropriate review authority is mandatory.

21         •     “P” – Uses allowed subject to approval of applicable permits.

22         •     “R/A” – Uses permitted upon review and approval as set forth in Section 40.520.020.

23         •     “C” – Conditional uses which may be permitted subject to the approval of a conditional use permit as set
24               forth in Section 40.520.030.

25         •     “X” – Uses specifically prohibited.

26   Where there are special use standards or restrictions for a listed use, the applicable code section(s) in Chapter
27   40.260, Special Uses and Standards, or other applicable chapter is noted in the “Special Standards” column.

14        shall be a minimum of eleven (11) feet (eight (8) feet of sidewalk/walkway with a minimum of three (3)

17        canopy. The required landscape area should function as a buffer between auto drives and the pedestrian
18        routes. Where the pedestrian circulation route crosses vehicular accessways the landscape area is not
19        required.

21        provided. Minimum requirements shall be trees, of a suitable species according to Section 40.320.010,

 1       provided every thirty (30) feet on center planted in a landscaped strip or tree wells along the length of the
 2       building.

 3       c. Landscape buffers required by Section 40.320.010 shall not apply between pad development sites and
 4       the remainder of the development site.

 5       d. Landscaping required between commercial developments may be altered where parking lots are
 6       adjoining as follows: a single, shared five (5) foot buffer instead of five (5) feet for each development;
 7       provided, that joint access is provided between parcels for auto and pedestrian access and trees are planted
 8       every twenty (20) feet on center along the length of the buffer.

 1   EXHIBIT 14


 2   40.230.020 Mixed Use District (MX)
 3   A. Purpose.

 4      The mixed use (MX) district requires mixed use developments to provide the community with a mix of mutually
 5   supporting retail, service, office and residential uses. It promotes cohesive site planning and design which integrates
 6   and interconnects two (2) or more land uses into a development that is mutually supportive. It provides incentives
 7   to develop a higher-density, active, urban environment than generally found in a suburban community, and which is
 8   further expected to:

 9          1.   Achieve goals and objectives of the community framework plan and the comprehensive plan;

10          2.   Enhance livability, environmental quality and economic vitality;

11          3. Accommodate and respect surrounding land uses by providing a gradual transition adjacent to lower
12          density neighborhoods that may encircle a potential mixed use site;

13          4.   Maximize efficient use of public facilities and services;

14          5.   Provide a variety of housing types and densities;

15          6.   Reduce the number of automobile trips and encourage alternative modes of transportation; and

16          7.   Create a safe, attractive and convenient environment for living, working, recreating and traveling.

17   B.     Applicability.

18          1. General. The provisions of this section shall be applied to parcels or groups of contiguous parcels
19          designated mixed use on the zoning map.

20          2. Interpretation. If the requirements of the mixed use district conflict with other regulations, the more
21          stringent of the two (2) shall apply as determined by the responsible official.

22   C.     Definitions.

23        For the purposes of this section, the following definitions shall apply:

24          1. “Mixed use development” shall mean a group of structures planned as a single entity and containing within
25          and/or among them a variety of complementary and/or mutually supporting uses (such as housing, offices,
26          retail, public service or entertainment).

27          2. “Developable area” means that portion of the site which is unencumbered by environmentally sensitive
28          lands to be protected and their respective buffers, designated parks and open space, public right-of-way, and
29          road easements.

30          3. “Building footprint” means the area contained within the exterior building walls of the ground floor of a
31          building plus the area of any portion of upper floors that extend outside of the footprint of the ground floor.

32          4. “Cottage housing” means a collection of small single-family detached dwelling units arranged
33          around a common open space.




                                                                                                                       122
 1       5 4. “Mixed use building” means any building that combines both permitted residential and nonresidential
 2       uses.

 3       6 5.    “Net ground floor area” means nonresidential floor area and excludes parking areas.

 4       7 6. “Senior housing” means dwelling units specifically for occupancy by persons fifty-five (55) years of age
 5       or older.

 6   D. Uses. The uses set out in Table 40.230.020-1 are permitted or conditional in the mixed use (MX) district.
 7   Residential uses are permitted so long as the minimum required densities of this section are met. The appropriate
 8   review authority is mandatory.

 9       •   “P” Uses allowed subject to approval of applicable permits.

10       •   “R/A” Uses permitted upon review and approval as set forth in Section 40.520.020.

11       •   “C” Conditional uses which may be permitted subject to the approval of a conditional use permit as set
12           forth in Section 40.520.030.

13       •   “X” Uses specifically prohibited.

17   E.   Mixed Use Standards.

18        1.     Residential.

19               a. Whether within a single building or on a development site, a minimum of twenty ten percent (20
20               10%) of the development shall be nonresidential and a minimum of twenty percent (20%) of the
21               development shall be residential. This percentage shall be calculated by determining the percent of
22               developable area devoted to each type of use. For mixed use buildings, the percentage shall be calculated
23               by one (1) of the following methods:

24                  (1)   The percentage of total building’s floor area devoted to residential to nonresidential uses.




                                                                                                                         130
 1              (2) The percentage of net ground floor area (for nonresidential uses) to gross ground floor area
 2              (residential and/or parking areas).

 3           b. Partial Exception. The responsible official may allow a reduction in the percentage of required
 4           nonresidential uses as required in Section 40.230.020(E)(1)(a), to a minimum of five percent (5%)
 5           provided all of the following criteria are met:

 6              (1) The development includes a vertical mix of uses in one (1) or more mixed use buildings. This
 7              shall include space for nonresidential uses on at least a portion of the ground floor and residential uses
 8              on one (1) or more upper floors;

 9              (2)   The development site is not larger than two (2) acres; and

10              (3) All ground floor residential units facing a public street shall maintain a minimum structural
11              ceiling height of thirteen (13) feet to provide the opportunity for future conversion to nonresidential
12              uses.

13           c. Full Exception. Multifamily affordable housing projects located in the Vancouver Urban Growth
14           Area will be exempt from Section 40.230.020(E)(1)(a) if the applicant provides evidence to the
15           responsible official prior to building permit certificate of occupancy that the project meets the definition
16           of “affordable housing” herein and provides of an executed long-term restrictive covenant for a period
17           of at least thirty (30) years that requires one hundred percent (100%) of the units at an average of
18           sixty percent (60%) of area median income (AMI) or less. Publicly subsidized affordable housing
19           that receives or otherwise qualifies for an exemption from real and personal property taxes under
20           RCW 84.36.560.23, co-living housing, transitional housing and permanent supportive housing
21           projects will also be exempt from Section 40.230.020(E)(1)(a).

22           d. A minimum density of twelve twenty-four (12 24) dwelling units per acre is required. This shall be
23           based on the developable area devoted to residential use. If the development site is greater than five (5)
24           twenty (20) acres and less than fifty (50) acres, the residential use shall contain a minimum three (3)
25           permitted housing types as set forth in the Mixed Use Design Standards in Appendix A of this title. If the
26           development site is fifty (50) acres or more, the residential use shall contain a minimum four (4)
27           permitted housing types as set forth in the Mixed Use Design Standards in Appendix A of this title.

28           e. The maximum density shall be forty-three fifty (43 50) dwelling units per acre. This shall be based
29           on the developable area devoted to residential use. Exception: There shall be no maximum density for
30           multifamily affordable housing projects, transitional housing, permanent supportive housing
31           projects, or for buildings featuring vertical mix of uses. To qualify, nonresidential uses (net ground floor
32           area) shall occupy at least fifty percent (50%) of the building’s gross ground floor area. Residential uses
33           shall occupy one (1) or more upper floors.

34           f. Conversion of Existing Structures. An existing residential structure may not be converted to a
35           commercial or office use unless consistent with this chapter.

36       2. Nonresidential. All uses on the ground floor must maintain a minimum structural ceiling height of thirteen
37       (13) feet.

38   F. Design Standards. All new development shall be designed in accordance with the Mixed Use Design Standards
39   as set forth in Appendix A of this title.

40   G. Incentives. A reduction of the TIF may be granted pursuant to this section with the implementation and
41   maintenance of the corresponding action in Table 40.230.020-2 on approval of the responsible official.

 1
 2   H.   Approval Process.

 3        1. Site Plan Review Required. Mixed use developments shall require submittal of a site plan, as required
 4        under Section 40.520.040, Site Plan Review, and Table 40.510.050-1, Application Submittal Requirements.
 5        The mixed use site plan shall be processed under the Type III review process, per Section 40.510.030.

 6            a. The applicant for a proposed mixed use development shall certify that two (2) or more of the
 7            following have been involved with the preparation of the preliminary mixed use development plan:

 8               (1)   An architect licensed in the state of Washington; and/or

 9               (2)   A landscape architect licensed in the state of Washington; and/or

10               (3) A registered civil engineer or a registered land surveyor licensed in the state of Washington;
11               and/or

12               (4)   A certified arborist, if a vegetation management plan is required.

13            b. One of the above professionals shall be designated by the applicant to be responsible for submitting
14            materials to and communicating with the responsible official with respect to the concept and details of the
15            development plan. This applicant designee shall act as a liaison between the responsible official, the
16            design team and the applicant. The selection of this liaison shall not prevent the applicant or any member
17            of the design team from conferring with the responsible official or presenting material to the responsible
18            official. The responsible official may require that the expertise of other professionals be used in the
19            planning and development of the mixed use development if it is determined that the site merits special
20            consideration due to particularly unusual or adverse features or conditions.

21            c. All plans and specifications required for the development shall be prepared and designed by engineers
22            and/or architects licensed in the state of Washington.

23        2. Neighborhood Meeting Required. A neighborhood meeting shall be held prior to submission of an
24        application for a mixed use development. The applicant shall hold a public meeting to offer owners of property
25        adjacent to the affected property an opportunity to participate in the development process. A pre-application




                                                                                                                  132
 1   conference is not a substitute for the required neighborhood meeting. The applicant shall follow the
 2   neighborhood meeting guidelines established by the county.

 3       a.    The neighborhood meeting shall be held within ninety (90) days prior to submitting the application.

 4       b. The applicant shall send a notice of the meeting at least fifteen (15) days prior to the neighborhood
 5       meeting to:

 6            (1) The official representative(s) of the county-recognized neighborhood association(s), if applicable,
 7            in whose boundaries the affected property is located, based on the list of official neighborhood
 8            associations kept by the responsible official; and

 9            (2) Residents and property owners of record of property within a radius of five hundred (500) feet of
10            the subject property. The records of the County Assessor shall be used for determining the property
11            owners of record. The mailing list used for notification shall be based on the most recent property tax
12            assessment rolls within thirty (30) days of mailing of the Clark County Assessor. At the request of the
13            applicant, and upon payment of an applicable fee, the county will provide the required mailing list.

14            (3)   The responsible official.

15       c. The notice must identify the date, time and place of the meeting and provide a brief description of the
16       proposed development.

17       d. A copy of the notice, proposed development plan as presented at the meeting, the mailing list, meeting
18       summary and the sign-in sheet from the meeting shall be submitted with the application.

19   3. Master Plan. All developments with proposed phasing of uses shall submit a master plan pursuant to
20   Section 40.520.070.

                                                                                                              133
 1   EXHIBIT 15


 2   40.250.120 Manufactured Home Park Overlay District (MHP)
 3   A. Purpose.

 4     The Manufactured Home Park Overlay District (MHP) is intended to preserve existing manufactured
 5   home parks, protect long-term affordability, and prevent the displacement of existing residents. The district
 6   promotes community stability by limiting redevelopment of manufactured home parks while allowing the
 7   continued repair, replacement, and expansion of manufactured housing in accordance with the standards of
 8   this title.

 9   B.   Applicability.

10      The MHP Overlay District shall be applied to existing and new manufactured home parks within urban
11   growth areas of Clark County. Once a property has been developed as a manufactured home park, a docket
12   is required to change the zoning to apply the MHP Overlay District.

13   C.   Uses.

14      Permitted uses in the MHP Overlay District are limited to the following, and shall supersede the use
15   standards for the underlying zoning district:

16        1.   Manufactured home parks, subject to Section 40.260.140.

17        2.   Transitional housing.

18        3.   Permanent supportive housing.

19        4.   Accessory uses and structures normal to a residential environment, subject to Section 40.260.010.

20        5.   Family day care providers, subject to Section 40.260.160.

21        6.   Adult family homes, subject to Section 40.260.190.

22        7.   Home business – Type I, subject to Section 40.260.100.

23        8.   Garage sales, subject to Section 40.260.090.

24        9. Temporary uses, subject to Section 40.260.220.

25        10. Utilities, other than wireless communications facilities, subject to Section 40.260.240.

26        11. Wireless communications facilities, subject to Section 40.260.250.

27        12. Electric vehicle infrastructure, subject to Section 40.260.075.

28   D.   Development Standards.

29        1. Manufactured home parks shall be subject to the development standards for the underlying zoning
30        district and Section 40.260.140 (Manufactured Home Parks – Standards and Requirements).

31        2. All other uses shall be subject to the development standards for the underlying zoning district and
32        any applicable special use standards.




                                                                                                            134
 1   E.   Use Exception.

 2     A property owner within the MHP Overlay District may seek a comprehensive plan map and zone change
 3   for the property pursuant to the procedures set forth in Section 40.560.020 (Changes to Zoning Districts and
 4   Code Amendments). The applicant must demonstrate that:

 5        1.   The existing MHP Overlay District does not allow for reasonable use of the property;

 6        2. The uses permitted in the MHP Overlay District are not economically viable due to the property’s
 7        location, site conditions, or surrounding development; or

 8        3. The applicant will redevelop the site and the replacement development will provide affordable
 9        housing to maintain long-term affordability of no more than sixty percent (60%) of area median income.
10        Residents displaced by the redevelopment shall be provided a right of first opportunity to occupy units
11        in the replacement development upon completion, subject to applicable income, household size and other
12        lawful program eligibility requirements.

13

 3   A. In the R1-5, R1-6, R1-7.5, R1-10, R1-20, R-12, R-18, R-22, R-30, R-43, OR-15, OR-18, OR-22, OR-30,

 1   EXHIBIT 17


 2   40.260.060 Co-living Housing
 3   A. Purpose. The purpose in providing for co-living housing is to authorize the development of rental homes
 4   affordable to people with moderate to low incomes that can be developed without any public funding and
 5   that are energy efficient due to their small size, and to be consistent with RCW 36.70A.535.

 6   B. Applicability. These standards apply to co-living housing developed in all zoning districts where
 7   permitted.

 8   C.   Development Standards.

 9        1. Co-living housing shall meet the development standards for the zoning district where it is located.
10        For the purposes of calculating dwelling unit density, a sleeping unit in co-living housing shall be
11        counted as one-quarter of a dwelling unit.

12        2. Co-living units may be provided in a mix of sizes and number of rooms. Room dimensional
13        standards shall minimally meet the Washington State building code, including dwelling unit size,
14        sleeping unit size, room area, and habitable space.

15        3. Sleeping units may include kitchenettes, with a sink and at least one 120v electrical outlet, but shall
16        not include kitchens, with a sink, gas or 220/240v electric stove and oven, and refrigerator.

17        4. Co-living housing with fifty-six (56) or more sleeping units shall be subject to Section 40.260.150
18        (Multifamily Residential Amenity Areas).

19        5.   Off-street parking shall be provided pursuant to Chapter 40.340.

20        6. For purposes of calculating sewer connection fees, a sleeping unit in a co-living housing is no more
21        than one-half of a dwelling unit, unless a finding, based on facts, is made that the connection fees should
22        exceed the one-half threshold.

23

8   commercial activities taking place within the building.

 1   EXHIBIT 19


 2   40.260.072 Compact Lot Developments
 3   A. Purpose.

 4        The purposes of the compact lot development provisions are:

 5         1. To encourage development of underutilized and challenged parcels within developed portions of the low-
 6         density residential districts by providing flexibility for smaller, more efficient housing.

 7         2. To diversify the county’s housing stock by providing a variety of housing types, sizes and price points that
 8         responds to changing household sizes and ages, such as retirees, small families, and single-parent households.

 9         3.   To encourage development in existing residential areas by allowing a density bonus and design flexibility.

10         4. To mitigate potential impacts of density and narrow lots through site design balancing community
11         connections and privacy.

12         5.   To integrate developments into existing neighborhoods.

13         (Added: Ord. 2024-03-02)

14   B.     Applicability.

15         1. Compact lot developments are permitted on parcels created prior to the adoption date of the ordinance
16         codified in this section that meet all of the following criteria:

17              a. The parcel is three (3) acres or smaller in area, excluding any critical areas, public rights-of-way,
18              private road easements, or street tracts; and

19              b. The proposed development can and will be served by urban services at the time of final plat or
20              development approval. For the purposes of this chapter, “urban services” shall mean public water and
21              sewer service as described in Chapter 40.370.

22         2. Compact lot developments may not be used in conjunction with a planned unit development per Section
23         40.520.080.

24         3. Compact lot developments that meet the applicability standards of Section 40.260.155(B) shall also be
25         subject to the narrow lot standards of Section 40.260.155 (Narrow Lots).

26         (Added: Ord. 2024-03-02)

27   C.     Review Required.

28     A site plan is required that meets the requirements of Section 40.260.155(C)(1). The site plan shall be submitted
29   with the preliminary land division application.

30         (Added: Ord. 2024-03-02)

31   D.     Development Standards.

32         1. Compact lot developments may include single-family detached dwellings at up to two hundred percent
33         (200%) of the maximum density of the underlying zone, including any accessory dwelling units.

 1   EXHIBIT 20


 2   40.260.076 Emergency Shelters, Transitional Housing, Emergency Housing, and Permanent Supportive
 3   Housing
 4   A. Purpose. The purpose in providing for emergency shelters, transitional housing, emergency housing,
 5   and permanent supportive housing is to facilitate the prompt delivery of safe, dignified, and stable housing
 6   and to support efforts to address homelessness and the housing crisis.

 7   B. Applicability. These standards apply to emergency shelters, transitional housing, emergency housing,
 8   and permanent supportive housing (STEP housing) developed in all zoning districts where permitted.

 9   C.   Development Standards for all STEP Housing.

10        1.   STEP housing shall meet the development standards for the zoning district where it is located.

11        2. Transitional housing and permanent supportive housing with eighteen (18) or more dwelling units
12        shall be subject to Section 40.260.150 (Multifamily Residential Amenity Areas).

13        3.   Off-street parking shall be provided pursuant to Chapter 40.340.

14        4. The County may enter into a contract with a sponsor proposing a STEP housing development to
15        establish additional and reasonable operational requirements pertaining to health and safety protocols.
16        Consideration for a contract entered into under this Section on the part of the County must include at
17        least one of the following:

18             a.   A donation of real property;

19             b.   A long-term lease of land at least fifty (50) years in length; or

20             c. A significant contribution from the general fund for capital or operating expenses to be
21             mutually determined by the parties to the contract.

22        In the event the County unilaterally withdraws or modifies the consideration identified in this Section,
23        in material breach of the contract, then the operational requirements specified in the contract no longer
24        apply to the STEP housing. A sponsor's refusal to accept contract terms relating to the consideration
25        may not be construed as failing to meet the requirements of other fund sources available other
26        provisions relating to the siting of STEP housing.

27   D.   Development Standards for Emergency Shelters and Emergency Housing.

28        1. Indoor emergency shelters and emergency housing are permitted. Temporary structures are
29        prohibited.

30        2. Certification Required. Sponsors or managing agencies of an indoor emergency shelter or indoor
31        emergency housing must certify in writing that they have, between the date the permit application was
32        approved and the date the certificate of occupancy was issued, completed and provided to the County
33        the following:

34             a. Provided the name, if any, of the facility and the name and headquarters address of the
35             managing entity;




                                                                                                             142
 1       b. Provided a description of all other programs operating in the building and copies of the
 2       applicable licenses and certifications held by the program;

 3       c. Notified residents within five hundred (500) feet of the indoor emergency shelter or indoor
 4       emergency housing;

 5       d.    Conducted more than one community meeting;

 6       e. Designated at least one point of contact authorized to make decisions and take corrective action
 7       in the case of emergencies;

 8       f.   Provided at least one telephone number to be used in emergencies; and

 9       g.    Provided documentation of operational policies or procedures for:

10            (1) Promoting and addressing health and safety, inside the building and in areas immediately
11            adjacent to the building;

12            (2)   Program exit or transfer;

13            (3) Handling complaints and grievances from occupants;

14            (4) Health, fire, safety and occupancy; and

15            (5) Staffing to provide services and resources to the population being served comparable to
16            standard or customary practices for the particular type of housing being provided. The staffing
17            policy or procedure may include but is not limited to, the number of staff or volunteers present
18            during daytime and nighttime operations, staff or volunteer training, and other processes to
19            provide services and resources to the population being served.

20   3. Upon receipt of a permit application for an indoor emergency shelter or indoor emergency housing
21   with a proposed location within five hundred (500) feet of walking distance from another indoor
22   emergency shelter or other indoor emergency housing or within five hundred (500) feet of walking
23   distance from the physical grounds of a public or private school that existed before an indoor emergency
24   shelter or indoor emergency housing is in operation or has submitted a permit application, the County
25   may enter into good-faith negotiations with the sponsor to establish additional and reasonable
26   requirements pertaining to health and safety protocols. If the parties are unable to come to an
27   agreement within one hundred eighty (180) days of the permit being submitted, the County may deny
28   the permit. If the County denies the permit, it will assist the sponsor in identifying suitable properties to
29   site the indoor emergency shelter or indoor emergency housing. This section does not apply to indoor
30   emergency shelters or indoor emergency housing designated for individuals under the age of eighteen
31   (18), families, or victims of domestic violence. The County may not limit the location of indoor
32   emergency shelters or indoor emergency housing in a manner where the capacity of indoor emergency
33   shelters and indoor emergency housing is insufficient to meet the projected needs identified under RCW
34   29.36.70A.070(2)(a)(ii).

 1   EXHIBIT 21


 2   40.260.140 Manufactured Home Parks – Standards and Requirements
 3   A. Use. Uses within a manufactured home park permitted outright include:

 4     A manufactured home park may be placed or located on any parcel of land in any multifamily (R-12, R-
 5   18, R-22, R-30, R-43, OR-15, OR-18, OR-22, OR-30 and OR-43) district upon approval of the responsible
 6   official, and provided such manufactured home park is for residential use only.

 7          1.   Manufactured homes.

 8          2. A Recreational vehicles and park models (camper, camp trailer, recreational coach, park models,
 9          and similar uses) may be placed in a manufactured home park provided the unit has an applicable
10          Washington State Department of Labor and Industries (L&I) tag for the use.

11          3. Normal accessories for manufactured homes, such as awnings, patios, carports, ramadas, cabanas, or
12          storage buildings, shall be allowed.

13          4. Accessory structures supporting the primary residential use, including community buildings,
14          recreational facilities, and commercial uses.

15         (Amended: Ord. 2018-10-02)

16   B.     Criteria for Locating a New Manufactured Home Park.

17        The following criteria must be taken into consideration in the establishment of new manufactured home parks:

18         1. Adequate buffering or screening may be required in order to make the manufactured home park
19         compatible with adjacent surrounding residential uses. Buffering and screening shall be required when
20         such parks abut commercial and industrial zones.

21         2. Manufactured home parks should not be located within areas less than five (5) acres nor more than
22         fifty (50) acres in area, unless it is demonstrated to the responsible official that lesser or greater
23         concentration of the use would be compatible with the surrounding property and its use, and that such
24         variation is in the interest of the public health, safety and general welfare.

25         3 1. The park must have its primary direct access to a county or public road, which shall have a minimum
26         right-of-way of sixty (60) feet, as shown on the master plan.

27         (Amended: Ord. 2018-10-02)

28   C.     Manufactured Home Space Requirements for New Manufactured Home Parks.

29         1. Coverage. A manufactured home and all accessory structures shall not occupy more than fifty percent
30         (50%) of the area of the manufactured home space.

31         2. Density. Manufactured homes shall not exceed the maximum density of the zoning district. Density shall
32         be calculated on the gross area of the park. No minimum density shall apply to manufactured home parks.

33         3. Setbacks. No manufactured home or accessory thereto shall be located any closer than twenty-five (25)
34         ten (10) feet from a park property line abutting on a public street or road, five (5) feet from all other park




                                                                                                                    144
 1        property lines and five (5) feet from any such areas as a park street, a common parking area, or a common
 2        walkway.

 3        4. Spacing. A manufactured home shall be separated from an adjacent manufactured home by a minimum of
 4        ten (10) feet.

 5        5. Overnight Spaces. Not more than five percent (5%) of the total manufactured home park area may
 6        be used to accommodate persons wishing to park their manufactured home or camping vehicles
 7        overnight.

 8        6 5. Parking. Two (2) Off-street parking spaces shall be provided for each manufactured home space
 9        consistent with Section 40.340.010, either on the manufactured home space or within one hundred (100) feet
10        thereof, in the manufactured home park. Each parking space shall not be less than nine (9) by twenty
11        (20) feet in size.

12        (Amended: Ord. 2018-10-02)

13   D.   Manufactured Home Park Requirements for New Manufactured Home Parks.

14        1.   Park Streets and Walkways.

15             a. Park Streets. A park street shall connect each manufactured home space to a public road. The park
16             street shall be a minimum of thirty (30) feet in width, with a paved surface width of at least twenty-four
17             (24) feet.

18             b. Walkways. Walkways of not less than forty-four (44) inches in width shall be provided from each
19             manufactured home space to any service building, recreation area, and parking area.

20             c.   Paving. Park streets shall be paved with crushed rock base asphalt or concrete surfacing.

21        2. Buffering or Screening. Buffering or screening, if required to make the manufactured home park
22        compatible with its adjacent surrounding uses, is required around the perimeter of the property to make
23        the manufactured home park compatible with its adjacent surrounding uses. Buffering or screening
24        shall be a sight-obscuring fence, wall, evergreen, or other suitable planting. Where walls or fences are required
25        along boundaries or public roads, the walls or fences shall be set back from the property lines to conform with
26        setbacks for structures in the basic zone. Evergreen planting shall not be less than five (5) feet in height, and
27        shall be maintained in a healthy living condition for the life of the manufactured home park. All walls and
28        fences shall be a minimum of five (5) feet in height and shall be approved by the responsible official.

29        3. Landscaping. There shall be landscaping provided within the front and side setback areas and all open
30        areas in the manufactured home park not otherwise used. Landscaping shall meet the requirements of
31        Section 40.320.010 for developments zoned multifamily.

32        4.   Signs. Signs shall comply with the provisions of Chapter 40.310, specifically Section 40.310.010(I).

33        5. Recreational Area. A recreational area shall be a contiguous, improved area, and be suitably maintained
34        for recreational purposes. The recreational area shall contain a minimum of two hundred (200) square
35        feet of space per manufactured home. It may contain a mix of landscaping, lawn area, pedestrian paths,
36        and/or paved courtyard area, and recreational amenities. Impervious elements shall not exceed fifty
37        percent (50%) of the total recreational area. Such land shall be determined on a gross area basis. The
38        amount of land to be established as recreational shall be determined by dividing the number of dwelling
39        units into the gross development area. The following minimum areas shall be required:




                                                                                                                    145
                Dwelling Units per Gross Acre                    Recreational Area Required
                                     5 to 6                                             0%
                                     7 to 9                                             3%
                                    10 to 11                                            5%
                                  12 and over                                           8%
 1
 2        6. Accessories. Structures located on a manufactured home space, in addition to the manufactured home,
 3        shall be limited to the normal accessories, as set forth under Section 40.260.140(A). No other structural
 4        additions shall be built onto or become part of any manufactured home, and no manufactured home shall
 5        support any building in any manner.

 6        7. Manufactured Home Pads. Pads, stands, strips or rails adequate for the support of the manufactured home
 7        shall be installed.

 8        8. Manufactured Home Skirting. All manufactured homes within the manufactured home park shall be
 9        skirted on their lower perimeter by fire-resistant siding, if occupied for a period of more than ninety (90) days.

10        (Amended: Ord. 2009-03-02; Ord. 2018-10-02)

11   E.   Repair and Replacement Within Existing Manufactured Home Parks.

12     The repair or replacement of existing manufactured homes within an existing manufactured home park,
13   including nonconforming parks, shall be allowed as provided in this section.

14        1. Repair of existing units. The repair or rehabilitation of an existing manufactured home shall be
15        permitted outright.

16        2. Replacement of existing units. The replacement of an existing manufactured home with a
17        replacement manufactured home is permitted outright.

18        3. Placement of new accessory structures and replacement manufactured homes in existing
19        manufactured housing parks shall be subject to the dimensional standards in effect when the parks were
20        approved, provided, that the current maximum density of the zoning district shall apply. Where internal
21        setbacks are not specified, the average of the prevailing setbacks on the pads to either side of the
22        proposed new or replacement structure shall apply.

23   F.   Expansion of Existing Manufactured Home Parks.

24     Existing manufactured home parks may be expanded through the addition of additional manufactured
25   homes or the expansion of their existing area.

26        1. The addition of a manufactured home to an existing manufactured home park development is
27        permitted outright.

28        2. The expansion of an existing manufactured home park is allowed subject to site plan approval by
29        the responsible official.

30        3. Additional manufactured homes and expansion areas shall comply with all applicable development
31        standards of this section as well as the underlying zoning district.

32   E G. Manufactured Home Park Approval Criteria – Responsible Official Approval.




                                                                                                                     146
 1   1. New manufactured home parks may be permitted in any multifamily district upon require site plan
 2   approval by the responsible official. The responsible official shall find that the internal design proposed shall
 3   separate traffic pattern from outdoor living or recreational areas, and will group the service facilities (such as
 4   the laundry and service buildings). Further, such approval shall be in the best interest of the public health,
 5   safety and general welfare. No manufactured home park shall be constructed without first obtaining site plan
 6   approval from the responsible official.

 7   F 2. Manufactured Home Park Site Plan Submittal Requirements. In addition to the submittal
 8   requirements for site plan review, an application for a new manufactured home park shall include a plot plan
 9   of the proposed park.

                                                                                                                147
 1   EXHIBIT 22


 2   40.260.150         Multifamily Residential Outdoor Recreation Amenity Area Standards
 3
 4   A.     Purpose.

 5     Availability of amenity areas is an important aspect of livability and benefits public health and well-being.
 6   Amenity area standards are intended to assure opportunities for both private relaxation and for community
 7   interaction among residents. These standards are intended to ensure that required amenity areas are
 8   functional in terms of location, area, and level of improvement.

 9   A B.        Applicability.

10          1. This section applies to multifamily, transitional housing and permanent supportive housing
11          developments containing twelve (12) eighteen (18) or more residential dwelling units.

12          2.     This section applies to co-living housing containing fifty-six (56) or more sleeping units.

13   C. Amount of Amenity Area Required. The required amount of amenity area for multifamily
14   developments is equal to five (5) percent of the developable area of the lot as calculated per Section
15   40.220.020.C.6.a.

16        1. Private outdoor amenity areas may be used to meet up to fifty percent (50%) of the total amenity
17        area requirement.

18        2. Parking areas, required setbacks, required site landscaping, and driveways do not qualify as
19        amenity areas.

20        3. Land designated by covenant or public dedication to be permanently maintained in an undeveloped
21        state because the land is identified as sensitive due to the presence of steep slopes, unstable land,
22        historical or archaeological sites, wetlands and buffers, or other permanent physical development
23        limitations as may be determined by the responsible official do not qualify as amenity areas.

24   B D.        Private Residential Outdoor Amenity Areas Standards.

25        1. Each ground-level residential living unit shall have an outdoor private area (patio, terrace porch,
26        yard) containing at least forty-eight (48) square feet and a width of at least four (4) feet. A balcony used
27        for an entrance or exit shall be considered an open space only if it is for the exclusive use of the dwelling
28        unit in question and it contains at least forty-eight (48) square feet and a width of at least four (4) feet.

29        1. Dwelling units may include private outdoor amenity areas including patios, terraces, decks,
30        balconies, porches and yards.

31        2.      Private outdoor amenity areas shall be directly accessible from the interior of the dwelling unit.

32        3. Private outdoor amenity areas shall contain at least forty-eight (48) square feet of area with a width
33        of at least four (4) feet.

34        2 4. Private outdoor amenity areas for multifamily residential units shall be screened from view from
35        common amenity areas, other residential dwelling units, abutting land uses, and public or private streets
36        through the use of landscaping, fencing, wall, change of grade or other similar design element. to the
37        extent practicable using the orientation and location of structures, windows, and private outdoor spaces,



                                                                                                                 148
 1      landscaping and screening, natural features such as topography and open space, and built features such
 2      as windowless walls; provided, an applicant is not required to reduce the otherwise permitted density of
 3      a proposed development or to increase the cost of a proposed development by more than five percent
 4      (5%) per unit to comply with these standards.

 5   C E. Shared Recreation Areas for Multifamily Residential Uses Common Outdoor Amenity Area
 6   Standards.

 7      1. Usable recreation space shall be provided in residential development for the shared or common use
 8      of all residents in the following amounts:

 9            a.   Studio size up to and including two-bedroom units, two hundred (200) square feet per unit; and

10            b.   Three (3) or more bedroom units, three hundred (300) square feet per unit.

11       1. Common outdoor amenity areas shall be a minimum of one thousand (1,000) square feet in area,
12       and amenity areas shall have a minimum horizontal dimension of twenty (20) feet. The required
13       amenity area may be provided as multiple individual areas, each meeting these dimensions.

14      2. The required recreation space may be all outdoor space or part outdoor space and part indoor space
15      and all public or common space or part public and part common space; provided all public and common
16      outdoor recreation spaces shall be readily observable from residential dwelling units and/or public or
17      private streets to allow for surveillance that contributes to greater public safety.

18      2. Common outdoor amenity areas shall be abutted by dwelling units or common areas on at least two
19      sides that include windows and/or entrances facing onto the amenity areas to enhance residents’ access
20      to such spaces and to allow for surveillance that contributes to greater public safety.

21      3. Common outdoor amenity areas shall be accessible to all dwelling units, and connected to the site via
22      pedestrian paths. Any pedestrian paths that cross parking areas shall be differentiated with distinct
23      paving materials or other similar techniques.

24      3. The boundaries of public areas, such as streets or public gathering places, semipublic areas, such as
25      transition areas between streets and dwelling units, and private outdoor areas shall be clearly defined so
26      that a person can readily determine where the public space ends and the private space begins, such as by
27      using one (1) or more of the following:

28            a.   A deck, patio, low wall, fence or other suitable structures;

29            b.   Landscaping, such as a hedge or draping vine on a trellis or arbor;

30            c.   A change in the texture of the path material;

31            d.   Signs; or

32            e.   Substantial natural features, such as a drainageway or tree grove.

33       4.   Common outdoor amenity areas shall be improved as follows:

34            a. At least fifty percent (50%) of a common outdoor amenity area shall be improved with elements
35            that enhance the usability and livability of the space for residents, including at least one feature
36            besides (1) or (2):

37                   (1)   Site furnishings (benches, tables, pedestrian-scale lighting);




                                                                                                          149
 1                  (2)   Picnic areas;

 2                  (3) Gazebo or other similar structures for weather protection;

 3                  (4)   Patios or courtyards;

 4                  (5) Rooftop decks if improved with furnishings including seating and planters, and
 5                  accessible to all residents;

 6                  (6) Gardens, including community garden spaces;

 7                  (7)   Play structures;

 8                  (8)   Art or water features;

 9                  (9)   Swimming pools;

10                  (10) Sports courts such as tennis, pickleball, basketball, or volleyball;

11                  (11) Fitness equipment;

12                  (12) Fenced pet area with furnishings including garbage cans, up to 25% of total required
13                  amenity area;

14           b. The remaining unimproved portions of a common outdoor amenity area shall be landscaped
15           with grass, ground cover, shrubs, and/or trees. Stormwater low impact development features that
16           are not fenced such as rain gardens and infiltration planters may be integrated in outdoor common
17           amenity areas provided they do not exceed twenty-five percent (25%) of the total required amenity
18           area.

19       5. Indoor amenity areas may be provided and counted towards the total required amenity area.
20       Indoor amenity areas may include indoor recreation spaces, game rooms, club houses, indoor swimming
21       pools or fitness facilities if improved and accessible to all residents.

22   F. Alternative Amenity Area Design. The responsible official may approve and condition an alternative
23   design for common amenity areas that depart from the standards of subsection E as part of the site plan
24   review application if the responsible official finds that the proposed design will equally or better meet the
25   intent of the standards in subsection E. The total amount of amenity area required by subsection C may not
26   be reduced under this subsection.

 1   EXHIBIT 23


 2   40.260.160 Nursery Schools, Preschools, Kindergartens, Commercial Child Day Care Centers, and Family
 3   Day Care Providers
 4   A. Nursery schools, preschools, kindergartens and commercial child day care centers shall comply with the
 5   following criteria:

 6        1. Minimum site size shall be ten thousand (10,000) square feet, except, when a preschool, kindergarten or
 7        commercial child day care center is designed as a part of an integrated industrial, commercial or multifamily
 8        development, in which case the minimum lot size may be reduced by the review authority, provided all other
 9        applicable code requirements are met.

10        2. Provide and maintain outdoor play areas with a minimum area of one hundred (100) square feet per
11        individual based upon total capacity.

12             a. The outdoor play area requirement shall not apply to strictly “drop-in facilities” where the individuals
13             cared for are not on the premises for more than three (3) hours in a twenty-four (24) hour period; provided,
14             that the requirements of the Washington Administrative Code are met.

15             b. Facilities with a capacity of forty (40) individuals or more, under the licensing authority of the state
16             Department of Social and Health Services (DSHS), and with an approved “shifting schedule” for the use
17             of outdoor play area by DSHS, may calculate the outdoor play area based on one hundred (100) square
18             feet per individual using the outdoor area at any one (1) time; however, a minimum of four thousand
19             (4,000) square feet of outdoor play area must be provided.

20             c. Facilities with a capacity of thirty-nine (39) or less, or which do not qualify with a “shifting schedule”
21             as stated above, may count up to fifty (50) square feet of dedicated indoor play area per individual of
22             capacity toward the outdoor play area requirements.

23        3.   The play area shall be abutting the indoor facility.

24        4. A sight-obscuring fence of at least four (4) feet, but not more than six (6) feet in height, shall be provided
25        around the outdoor play area.

26        5.   Adequate off-street parking and loading space shall be provided pursuant to Chapter 40.340.

27        (Amended: Ord. 2008-06-02)

28   B.   Family day care facilities providers shall comply with the following criteria:

29        1. When located in a resource, rural or residential zone (R1-5, R1-6, R1-7.5, R1-10, R1-20, R-12, R-18, R-
30        22, R-30, R-43, OR-15, OR-18, OR-22, OR-30, OR-43 R-24, R-36, R-50, OR-24, OR-36, OR-50, R-5, R-
31        10, R-20, FR-80, FR-40, AG-20, and AG-WL districts), no exterior structural or decorative alteration which
32        will alter the residential character of a residence is permitted.

33        2.   Adequate off-street parking and loading space shall be provided pursuant to Chapter 40.340.

34        3. Two (2) nonresident or non-family member employees are permitted if located within a resource, rural or
35        residential zone.

36        4. Signage shall be limited to one (1) sign, not to exceed two (2) square feet in area, for identification
37        purposes only.

                                                  152
 1   EXHIBIT 24


 2   40.260.260 Zero Lot Line Developments
 3   A. Purpose.

 4     Zero lot line provisions are intended to provide flexibility for the placement of structures in new residential land
 5   divisions in order to increase usable yard area. Zero lot line developments are different from townhouse
 6   developments in that structures are not attached at property lines.

 7        (Amended: Ord. 2024-03-02)

 8   B.   Applicability.

 9      These provisions can be used as an alternative to standard side or rear setback requirements in the R1-5, R1-6
10   and R1-7.5, R-12, OR-12, R-18, OR-18, R-22 and OR-22 zoning districts, including within a compact lot
11   development subject to Section 40.260.072, when the requirements of this section are applied in conjunction with a
12   land division application.

13        (Amended: Ord. 2024-03-02)

14   C.   Requirements.

15        1. Preliminary plats using zero lot line provisions may not be approved without approval of a submitted site
16        plan. Both the site plan and preliminary plat shall be consistent with the standards of this section and all other
17        applicable requirements.

18        2. Conditions may be imposed to ensure that development on the resultant lots is consistent with the
19        approved site plan.

20        3. Building permits for structures may only be approved when consistent with the approved site plan and land
21        division.

22        4. Developments meeting all requirements of this section are exempt from separate site plan review under
23        Section 40.520.040.

24        5. Zero setbacks may be applied to a side or rear property line; provided, that the setback on the lot abutting a
25        zero setback lot line shall be at least ten (10) feet in the applicable single-family residential districts, and eight
26        (8) feet in the applicable residential or office residential district, except that the setback shall be at least six (6)
27        feet in a compact lot development in any residential district.

28        6. Structures less than three (3) feet to a property line must meet fire resistive provisions of the residential
29        building code.

30        7. A note shall be placed on the final plat, and covenants running with the land shall be approved by the
31        Prosecuting Attorney and recorded with the County Auditor to guarantee that the required setbacks are kept
32        perpetually free of structures.

33        8. Easements for such purposes as maintenance, or for building projection encroachments beyond a zero
34        setback line, shall be shown on the plat.

35        (Amended: Ord. 2024-03-02)

36   D.   Figure 40.260.260-1.
