 3
 4   40.310.010   Sign Standards
 5   A.     Purpose.
 6          The purpose of this section is to add sign requirements common to the several zoning districts for the
 7          preservation of the character of the areas, structures, and uses; the needs of residential, commercial,
 8          industrial and agricultural potential; the need for healthful, safe, and convenient use of all lands, and the
 9          conservation and promulgation of values and resources. These requirements include, but are not limited
10          to, standards relating to the number, size, placement and physical characteristics of signs. In addition, the
11          purpose of this section is to provide an effective administrative process for the review and enforcement of
12          these standards to protect and improve the aesthetic quality of the community.
13   B.     Scope.
14          The signage covered under this section includes, but is not limited to: all commercial signs, and wall
15          graphics; professional and business signs; home business signs; banners, balloons, flags and other
16          temporary signage. It is not intended to regulate traffic signs or other governmental street signs, doorway
17          identification nameplates, holiday decorations, informational signs pursuant to Chapter 13.20, temporary
18          interior window signage or memorial signs. Also, it is not intended to regulate signage which is not visible
19          from adjacent properties or from public rights-of-way. Further, it is not intended to regulate the display of
20          the national or state flag.
21          Three other sections of the Clark County Code contain additional requirements for sign codes, namely:
22          (1) Section 40.260.115(E)(5), which pertains to marijuana facilities, (2) Appendix A (F), which pertains to
23          sign standards for mixed use development, and (3) Appendix F, Chapter 8.4, which pertains to sign
24          standards for development in the Highway 99 Overlay District.
25   C.     Definitions.
26          The following definitions and terms shall be used in the interpretation of this section:
27
            Banner                 “Banner” means an on-site sign such as those used to announce an
                                   open house, a grand opening, or to make a special announcement.
                                   Normally, it is constructed of cloth, canvas, or similar material and
                                   is without a rigid frame. It will be considered either as a fascia or
                                   freestanding sign, depending on the method of attachments, and
                                   will have to comply with the normal zone requirements.


            Billboard              “Billboard” shall mean the same as “off-premises sign.” See
                                   definition of “off-premises sign.”
            Business complex       “Business complex” means two (2) or more commercial
                                   businesses on a lot or abutting lots with common access and
                                   parking.

            Business complex       “Business complex sign” means a sign which is designed to
            sign                   identify a business complex where no single business
                                   identification and/or advertisement occupies more than fifty
                                   percent (50%) of the sign area.




                                                                                                                  155
Directional sign      “Directional sign” means any sign which is designed and erected
                      solely for the purpose of traffic or pedestrian direction, and which
                      is placed on the property to which the public is directed. See also
                      “off-premises directional sign.”

Doorway               “Doorway identification sign” means a non-illuminated sign which
identification sign   is limited to the name, address, and number of the building,
                      institution, or person and to the activity carried on in the building
                      or institution, or the occupancy of the person; provided, that the
                      lettering, excluding numbers, of each sign shall not exceed two (2)
                      square feet.

Electronic            “Electronic message center” means a sign on which the copy
message center        changes automatically on a lampbank or through mechanical
                      means, such as electrical or electronic time and temperature units.


Fascia sign           “Fascia sign” means a flat sign which projects one and one-half
                      (1½) feet or less horizontally from the vertical face of the wall of a
                      building, or vertical face of a canopy awning or parapet upon
                      which it is affixed, painted, or attached, running parallel for its
                      whole length to the face or wall of the building and which does not
                      extend beyond the horizontal width of such wall, awning or
                      parapet.

Freestanding sign     “Freestanding sign” means a sign not attached to or forming a part
                      of a building.
Height of sign        “Height of sign” means the vertical distance measured from grade
                      at the point of support, or average grade for multiple supports and
                      monument signs, to the highest point on the sign and sign
                      structure.

Industrial            “Industrial complex” means two (2) or more industrial businesses
complex               on a lot or abutting lots with common access and parking.


Industrial            “Industrial complex sign” means a sign which is designed to
complex sign          identify an industrial complex where no single business
                      identification and advertisement occupies more than fifty percent
                      (50%) of the sign.

Monument sign         “Monument sign” means a sign and supporting structure which has
                      similar top and bottom dimensions and is constructed as a solid
                      structure or one which gives the appearance of a continuous, non-
                      hollow, unbroken, unfenestrated mass. Further, similar top and
                      bottom dimensions shall mean dimensions which are within ten
                      percent (10%) of each other.




                                                                                               156
Nonconforming sign “Nonconforming sign” means a sign which was erected legally but
                   which does not comply with currently applicable sign restrictions
                   and regulations; provided, a sign which does not conform to the
                   currently applicable sign code requirements, but for which a
                   variance or conditional use permit was issued, shall not be
                   considered a nonconforming sign.


Off-premises sign    “Off-premises sign” means a sign that advertises products, services
                     or facilities or directs persons to a premises different from where
                     the sign is installed.

Off-premises         “Off-premises directional sign” means a sign which directs persons
directional sign     to a premises different from where the sign is located. These signs
                     typically include an address or street name or direction such as
                     “two blocks north on the right.”

On-premises sign     “On-premises sign” means a sign which carries only
                     advertisements strictly related to a lawful use of the premises on
                     which it is located, including signs or sign devices indicating the
                     business transacted, services rendered, goods sold or produced on
                     the premises, name of the business, and name of the person, firm or
                     corporation occupying the premises. “On-premises sign” also
                     means a sign which contains religious, political, social and other
                     noncommercial messages.


Portable sign        “Portable sign” means any sign designed to move or be moved by
                     trailer or vehicle to advertise at different locations. Further, these
                     signs include any sign which is not firmly fastened to a building or
                     the ground.

Premises             “Premises” for the purposes of this section, means one (1) of the
                     following:
                      a. A legal lot; or
                      b. A combination of contiguous legal lots under one (1)
                      ownership; or
                      c. A group of legal lots with common access, parking and
                      signage.
                     If more than one (1) definition of “premises” can be applied to a
                     group of lots, the choice of which definition applies shall be the
                     owner(s) of the lots or the applicant for a sign permit representing
                     the owner(s); provided, only one (1) definition may be applied at
                     one (1) time to a group of lots; and provided further, all signs
                     within the premises or subsequent revision to the premises, shall
                     comply with the provisions of the sign code.




                                                                                              157
Projecting sign   “Projecting sign” means a sign which projects more than one and
                  one-half (1½) feet horizontally from the vertical face of a building,
                  awning, canopy or parapet.

Rooftop sign      “Rooftop sign” means a sign erected upon the roof of a building or
                  canopy, or a sign attached to a building which projects vertically
                  above the roof, eave, awning or parapet; however, this does not
                  include signs attached to the vertical face of a parapet, awning or
                  canopy; provided, the sign does not project above the vertical face
                  of the parapet, awning or canopy to which it is attached.


Rotating sign     “Rotating sign” means a sign in which the sign itself or any portion
                  of the sign moves in a revolving or similar manner.
Sign              “Sign” means a display or device affixed to the ground, attached
                  to a building, or other structure using graphics, symbols, and/or
                  written copy designed specifically for the display of a commercial
                  or other advertisement to the public.

Sign area         “Sign area” means:
                   a. The area of a freestanding sign or structure not using an
                   integral part of the building for its background means the largest
                   cross-sectional area of the sign measured to a line encompassing
                   all portions of the sign structure, including tubing used in
                   lighting such sign or structure, but excluding posts without
                   attached lighting. Further, the base on which a monument-type
                   sign is set may be excluded; provided, there is no attached
                   lighting.
                   b. The area of a double-faced sign (i.e., a sign painted on two
                  (2) sides, or signs which are erected in a “V” configuration with an
                  angle between the two (2) faces not exceeding thirty
                  (30) degrees), shall be the largest area on one (1) side of the sign.
                  Further, these types of signs shall be considered one (1) sign for the
                  purpose of determining the number of signs allowed.
                  c. The area of any sign or structure using an integral part of the
                  building or awning as a background means the area within the
                  shortest line drawn to include all letters, design and tubing which
                  are a part of the sign or structure; provided, that for illuminated
                  awnings the area shall be limited to the area within the shortest line
                  drawn to include all copy and graphics, excluding illuminated
                  areas outside of these lines.




Street frontage   “Street frontage” means the linear frontage of a parcel of property
                  abutting a single public street.




                                                                                           158
          Temporary sign         “Temporary sign” means any sign which is not permanently
                                 installed or affixed to any sign structure or building, and not
                                 displayed for longer than thirty (30) days. In case of construction
                                 project signs, they may be maintained for the duration of the
                                 construction. Temporary signs shall also include balloons attached
                                 to buildings or property.


 1   D.   Signs Prohibited.
 2        Erection or maintenance of signs having any of the following characteristics is prohibited in Clark
 3        County:
 4        1. Signs which bear or contain statements, words or pictures of an obscene nature;
 5        2. Signs advertising activities that are illegal under state or federal laws or regulations in effect at the
 6           location of such signs or at the locations of such activities;
 7        3. A sign which does not bear the names of the owner or person responsible for the maintenance of the
 8           advertising sign;
 9        4. Signs artificially illuminated which are of such intensity or placed in such manner as to interfere
10           with, or impair the vision of the driver of a motor vehicle, or otherwise interfere with any driver’s
11           operation of a motor vehicle;
12        5. Signs which attempt or appear to attempt to direct the movement of traffic by interfering with,
13           imitating or resembling any official traffic sign, signal or device;
14        6. Signs which prevent the driver of a vehicle from having a clear and unobstructed view of official
15           signs and approaching or merging traffic as defined in Section 40.350.030(B)(8);
16        7. Signs which exceed a height of thirty-five (35) feet, except as authorized under Section
17           40.310.010(G). Signs authorized by the district standards under Section 40.310.010(F)(3) and
18           Tables 40.310.010-2 through 40.310.010-6 shall comply with the height restrictions contained
19           therein;
20        8. Signs located or projecting within the county right-of-way unless a written street use permit has
21           been obtained;
22        9. Rooftop and rotating signs;
23        10. Portable signs, temporary signs, flags and banners unless a temporary sign permit has been
24            approved by the responsible official;
25        11. Signs containing strobe lights which are visible from beyond the property line;
26        12. Any sign not specifically permitted by this section, excluding those signs identified in the scope
27            of this chapter;
28        13. Signs which contain flashing lights which exceed more than ten percent (10%) of the area of the
29            sign;
30        14. Off-premises signs (billboards), except for off-premises directional signs as allowed under Table
31            40.310.010-1 of this section.
32   E.   Sign Permits Required.
33        Sign permits pursuant to Section 40.520.050 are required for all signs which are authorized under

10   G.   Limited Access Highway – Restricted Advertising.
11        The provisions of this subsection shall be applicable to all land abutting or adjoining Interstate 5 (I-5) and
12        Interstate 205 (I-205) and within three hundred (300) feet of the edge of the right-of-way, and with-in
13        commercial or employment zoning districts.
14        1. Restrictions.
15            a. No sign or sign structure, or portion thereof, shall exceed a height of fifty
16               (50) feet;
17            b. The erection or remodeling of signs directed at limited access highways shall be approved by the
18               responsible official, only after consideration has been given to the location, size, and orientation of
19               such signs, as provided for in this subsection;
20            c. The sign shall be designed to be viewed by a person of normal visual acuity traveling on I-5 or I-
21               205;



                                                                                                                 167
 1            d. Signs shall only contain advertising display specifically and directly related to products produced,
 2               sold, used, or otherwise handled, or services rendered on the real property;
 3            e. Not more than one (1) limited access highway advertising sign shall be located on the premises.
 4               The total number of signs allowed on the premises shall not exceed the district standards of
 5               40.310.010(F) and Tables 40.310.010-2 through 40.310.010-6;
 6            f.   The area of the sign shall comply with the standards of the district in which it is located.
 7        2. Freestanding signs oriented toward Interstate 5 (I-5) or Interstate 205 (I-205) that are not oriented
 8           toward the abutting roadway are considered limited access highway signs, and are subject to this
 9           section. (See Figure 40.310.010-1.)
10        3. Conformance to State Law. Nothing in this subsection shall be construed to permit the erection
11           of signs which are prohibited by state law or any amendments thereto.
12
13                                  Figure 40.310.010-1 – Highway-Oriented Signs




14
15
16   H.   Nonconforming Signs.
17        Nonconforming signs shall be subject to the conditions set forth below:
18        1. A nonconforming sign or sign structure shall not be altered or enlarged in any manner unless
19           such alteration or enlargement would bring the sign into conformity with the signs permitted in
20           the zoning district in which it is located; provided, the restriction against alteration does not apply
21           to copy or panel changes where the sign area and shape is maintained.
22        2. Any nonconforming sign or sign structure may be maintained with ordinary care.
23        3. All nonconforming temporary signs, portable signs, flags, pendants and banners shall be
24           removed.
25




                                                                                                                       168
 1   I.   Mobile Manufactured Home Park Signs.

 2        Mobile Manufactured home park signs shall be permitted as follows:

 3        1. Identification signs advertising mobile manufactured home parks; provided, that such signs shall have
 4            a maximum area of thirty-two (32) square feet and no more than one (1) sign allowed per street
 5            frontage;

 6        2. Only nonilluminated and indirectly illuminated signs shall be permitted in R-12, R-18, R-22, R-30, R-
 7            43, OR-15, OR-18, OR-22, OR-30, OR-43 R-24, R-36, R-50, OR-24, OR-36, OR-50, and PUD
 8            zones. No flashing or intermittent illumination shall be permitted;

 9        3. No sign or sign structure shall exceed fifteen (15) feet in height;

10        4. Signs over five (5) feet in height shall conform to the minimum setback designated for structures in that
11            zone;

12        5. Incidental signs for the information and convenience of tenants and the public relative to parking, traffic
13            movement, office, restrooms, etc., are allowed; provided, such signs do not exceed four (4) square feet
14            in area; and provided further, name plates do not exceed eight (8) by twelve (12) inches (8" x 12") in
15            area;

16        6. One (1) sign advertising the sale of each mobile manufactured home located on a mobile
17            manufactured home lot.
18   J.   Signs Facing Residential Districts.
19        A sign advertising a business which is not conducted on the premises, or a commodity or service which is
20        not the preliminary product of sale or services on the premises, shall not face or be oriented toward any
21        adjoining or abutting residential district within two hundred (200) feet of the premises on which the sign is
22        to be placed.
23   K.   On-Site Interference.
24        The location and structural design of freestanding signs shall not interfere with the safe and efficient use
25        of off-street parking and loading areas, including aisle ways and access driveways.
26   L.   Lighted Signs as Nuisance.
27        Illuminated signs shall be placed so as not to be a nuisance to any residents or future residents of adjacent
28        residentially zoned property within two hundred (200) feet of the sign. A nuisance shall be defined as
29        flashing lights or lights of such intensity which may interfere with the residents’ peaceful occupancy of
30        their home. As part of a sign permit or site plan review process, the planning manager may require signs to
31        be screened, shielded, relocated or the lighting adjusted or other measures to mitigate a potential
32        interference with abutting residentially zoned property.
33   M.   Enforcement.
34        Upon presentation of proper credentials, the responsible official or a duly authorized representative may
35        enter at reasonable times a building or structure, or upon any premises in Clark County, to perform any
36        duty imposed by this section. The responsible official may inspect or reinspect all signs at his/her discretion.
37   N.   Removal of Signs in Violation of This Section.
38        If the responsible official finds that any advertising sign is erected or maintained in violation of the prior
39        sign regulations, or is erected or maintained in violation of the provisions of this section, the responsible
40        official may institute appropriate action or proceedings to prevent, restrain, correct or abate the violation,
41        including fines and enforcement pursuant to Title 32 of this code.




                                                                                                                   169
 1   O.         Maintenance and Appearance of Signs.
 2              All advertising signs, together with all of their supports, braces, guys, and anchors, shall be kept in good
 3              repair and maintained in a safe condition. All advertising signs and the sites upon which they are located
 4              shall be maintained in a neat, clear, and attractive condition, and advertising signs shall be kept free from
 5              excessive rust, corrosion, peeling paint or other surface deterioration. The display surface of all outdoor
 6              advertising structures shall be kept neatly painted or posted.

 7   P.         Abandoned Signs.
 8              Except as provided in this section, any person who owns or leases a sign shall remove such sign when
 9              either the function has discontinued or business it advertises has discontinued on the premises on
10              which the sign is located; or when the sign is no longer properly repaired or maintained as required
11              by this section.

 1   EXHIBIT 26
 2
 3
 4          40.320.010 Landscaping and Screening on Private Property

 5         A.     Applicability.
 6                The following standards apply to landscaping and screening on private property. Landscaping and
 7                screening within public rights-of-way shall comply with the applicable provisions in Section
 8                40.320.020.

 9         B.     Landscaping and Screening Design Standards.

10                1.   L1, General Landscaping.

11                     a.   Intent. The L1 standard is for open areas. It is intended to be used where distance is the
12                          principal means of separating uses or development, and landscaping enhances the area
13                          between them. The L1 standard consists principally of groundcover plants, trees, and
14                          shrubs.

15                     b.   Required Materials. There are two (2) ways to provide trees and shrubs to comply with an
16                          L1 standard. Shrubs and trees may be grouped. Groundcover plants, grass lawn or
17                          approved flowers must fully cover the landscaped area not in shrubs and trees. See Figure
18                          40.320.010-1 for conventional and LID cross-sections that comply with the L1 standard.

19                          (1) Where the area to be landscaped is less than ten (10) feet deep, one
20                              (1) tree shall be provided per thirty (30) linear feet of landscaped area.
21                          (2) Where the area is ten (10) feet deep or greater, one (1) tree shall be provided per eight
22                               hundred (800) square feet and either two (2) high shrubs or three (3) low shrubs shall
23                               be provided per four hundred (400) square feet of landscaped area.

24                     c.   Within the commercial districts where a building is to be placed at the buffer line for a
25                          front setback, permeable pavement may be used in place of the required groundcover for
26                          the length of the building for the front setback only; provided, the required trees are still
27                          supplied, the paved area is connected to the public sidewalk, and pedestrian amenities are
28                          provided such as benches or pedestrian plazas. The building need not be placed at the
29                          required buffer line to utilize this section if the area between the buffer line and the building
30                          is devoted entirely to pedestrian only areas.

31                2.   L2, Low Screen.

32                     a.   Intent. The L2 standard uses a combination of distance and low-level screening to separate
33                          uses or development. The standard is applied where a low level of screening sufficiently
34                          reduces the impact of a use or development, or where visibility between areas is more
35                          important than a greater visual screen.
36                     b.   Required Materials. The L2 standard requires enough low shrubs to form a
37                           continuous screen three (3) feet high and ninety-five percent (95%) opaque year-
38                           round. In addition, one (1) tree is required per thirty (30) lineal feet of landscaped
39                           area or as appropriate to provide a tree canopy over the landscaped area.
40                           Groundcover plants must fully cover the remainder of the landscaped area with the
41                           exception of energy dissipation points at the locations of stormwater inlets. LID
42                           bioretention facility plantings may be used in combination with perimeter shrubs,



                                                                                                                      171
 1              provided a continuous screen three (3) feet high and ninety-five percent (95%)
 2              opaque year-round can be achieved within two (2) years of planting. A three (3)
 3              foot high masonry wall or fence at an F2 standard or a berm may be substituted for
 4              shrubs, but the trees and groundcover plants are still required. When applied along
 5              street lot lines, the screen or wall is to be placed along the interior side of the
 6              landscaped area. See Figure 40.320.010-2.

 7   3.   L3, High Screen.

 8        a.   Intent. The L3 standard provides physical and visual separation between uses or
 9             development principally using screening. It is used where such separation is
10             warranted by a proposed development, notwithstanding loss of direct views.

11        b.   Required Materials. The L3 standard requires enough high shrubs to form a screen
12             six (6) feet high and ninety-five percent (95%) opaque year-round. In addition, one
13             (1) tree is required per thirty (30) lineal feet of landscaped area or as appropriate to
14             provide a tree canopy over the landscaped area. Groundcover plants must fully cover
15             the remainder of the landscaped area with the exception of energy dissipation points
16             at the locations of stormwater inlets. LID bioretention facility plantings may be used
17             in combination with perimeter shrubs, provided a continuous screen six (6) feet high
18             and ninety-five percent (95%) opaque year-round can be achieved within two (2)
19             years of planting. A six (6) foot high wall or fence that complies with the F2 standard
20             (Figure 40.320.010-7) with or without a berm may be substituted for shrubs, but the
21             trees and groundcover plants are still required. When applied along street lot lines,
22             the screen or wall is to be placed along the interior side of the landscaped area. See
23             Figure 40.320.010-3.

24   4.   L4, High Wall.

25        a.   Intent. The L4 standard is used where extensive screening of visual and noise
26             impacts is needed to protect abutting sensitive uses and/or there is little space for
27             separation between uses.
28             b.       Required Materials. The L4 standard requires a six (6) foot high wall that complies
29             with the site obscuring F2 standard for walls (Figure 40.320.010-7). When abutting another
30             property, the wall shall abut the property line. When abutting a street or road right-of-way,
31             the wall shall be on the interior side of the landscaped area. One (1) tree is required per
32             thirty (30) lineal feet of wall or as appropriate to provide a tree canopy over the landscaped
33             area with the exception of energy dissipation points at the locations of stormwater inlets.
34             LID bioretention facility plantings may be used, and are encouraged, to satisfy plant
35             requirements. In addition, four (4) foot high shrubs are required per thirty (30) lineal
36             feet of wall. Ground-cover plants must fully cover the remainder of the landscaped area.
37             See Figure 40.320.010-4.

38   5.   L5, High Berm.

39        a.   Intent. The L5 standard can be used instead of the L4 standard where extensive screening
40             is warranted and more space is available for separation between uses.

41        b.   Required Materials. The L5 standard requires a berm four (4) to six (6) feet high. If the
42             berm is less than six (6) feet high, low shrubs that comply with the L2 standard must be
43             planted on top of the berm so that the overall screen height is six (6) feet. In addition, one
44             (1) tree is required per thirty



                                                                                                          172
 1    (30) lineal feet of berm or as appropriate to provide a tree canopy over the landscaped area.
 2    Groundcover plants must fully cover the remainder of the landscaped area. See Figure 40.320.010-5.

 3        6.   F1, Partially Sight-Obscuring Fence.

 4             a.   Intent. The F1 fence standard provides partial visual separation. The standard is applied
 5                  where a proposed use or development has little impact, or where visibility between areas
 6                  is more important than a total visual screen and the installation of fencing will not interfere
 7                  with the implementation of LID stormwater management on the site.

 8             b.   Required Materials. A fence or wall that complies with the F1 standard shall be six (6) feet
 9                  high and at least fifty percent (50%) sight-obscuring. Fences may be made of wood, metal,
10                  chain link with slats, bricks, masonry or other permanent materials. See Figure 40.320.010-
11                  6.

12        7.   F2, Fully Sight-Obscuring Fence.

13             a.   Intent. The F2 fence standard provides visual separation where complete screening is
14                  needed to protect abutting uses, and landscaping alone cannot provide that separation.

15             b.   Required Materials. A fence or wall that complies with the F2 standard shall be six (6) feet
16                  high and one hundred percent (100%) sight-obscuring. Fences may be made of wood,
17                  metal, bricks, masonry or other permanent materials. This shall not include chain link
18                  fences with slats or similar construction. See Figure 40.320.010-7.

19   C.   Landscaping and Screening Approval Standards – General.

20        1.   A landscape plan shall contain landscaping and screening consistent with the
21             applicable design standards, based on Table 40.320.010-1 and other applicable provisions of this
22             section.

23        2.   The applicant may provide landscaping and screening that exceeds the standards in this
24             section; provided:

25             a.   A fence or wall (or a combination of a berm and fence or wall) may not exceed a
26                  height of six (6) feet above the finished grade at the base of the fence or wall (or at
27                  the base of a berm, if combined with one) unless the review authority finds additional
28                  height is necessary to mitigate potential adverse effects of the proposed use or other
29                  uses in the vicinity; and

30             b.   Landscaping and screening shall not obstruct sight distance at intersections as
31                  provided in Section 40.350.030 of the UDC.

32        3.   The responsible official may approve use of existing vegetation to fulfill landscaping and
33             screening requirements of this section if that existing landscaping provides at least an
34             equivalent level of screening as the standard required for the development in question.

35        4.   As a condition of approval of a conditional use or the expansion or alteration of an existing
36             conditional use or planned unit development, the county may require an applicant to
37             provide landscaping and screening that differs from the standards in Table 40.320.010-1
38             and Section 40.320.010(C)(2) where necessary to comply with the other applicable
39             approval standards for the use or development.




                                                                                                               173
 1        5.   Landscaped areas required for stormwater management purposes may be used to satisfy
 2             the landscaping area requirements of this section even though those areas may be
 3             inundated by surface water. All stormwater facility designs shall meet the standards as
 4             defined in Chapter 40.386.

 5        6.   Required landscaping and screening shall be located on the perimeter of a lot or parcel.
 6             Required landscaping and screening shall not be located on a public right-of-way or
 7             private street easement, unless authorized under Section 40.320.020.

 8        7.   The responsible official may approve and condition an alternative landscape design and
 9             buffer standard that is compatible with existing, abutting landscaping that still meets the
10             intent of the required buffer type, such as shared buffers between users.

11        8.   Required landscaping buffers shall not apply between buildings in common wall
12             construction situations.

13        9.   The exception to setback allowances under Section 40.200.070 shall not apply to
14             landscape buffers except as follows:
15             a.   Eaves that are sixteen (16) feet or more above grade may project twenty percent
16                  (20%) of the width of the buffer, up to a maximum of two (2)feet.

17             b.   Eaves seven (7) feet or more above grade may project ten percent (10%) of the width of
18                  the buffer, up to a maximum of two (2) feet.
19




20
21   D.   Landscape and Screening Standards for Storage and Equipment Areas.

22        1.   Storage and equipment areas shall be screened from property used or zoned for residential
23             purposes or a public road right-of-way to at least an F2 or L3 standard if within one hundred
24             (100) feet of the property or right-of-way and to at least an F1 standard if equal to or more than
25             one hundred (100) feet from the property or right-of-way. Storage areas include storage of solid
26             waste and recyclables from the site and, where permitted, storage of goods, materials or
27             equipment.

28        2.   Rooftop and ground-level exterior equipment shall be screened from abutting property used or
29             zoned for residential purposes or from an abutting public road right-of-way to at least an F2 or
30             L3 standard if visible at grade from the property or right-of-way.

31   E.   Landscaping and Screening Standards for Parking, Loading, and Drive-Through Areas.



                                                                                                             174
 1        1.   A minimum five (5) foot wide strip landscaped to at least an L2 standard shall be provided where
 2             vehicle parking, loading, or drive-through facilities abut a public road right-of-way.

 3        2.   Where a vehicle parking or loading area abuts a property with zoning or land uses other than the
 4             proposed land use, the area shall be landscaped and screened as provided in Table 40.320.010-
 5             1 abutting the other property.
 6        3.   Parking areas that contain at least seven (7) spaces shall contain landscape islands equally
 7             distributed at a ratio of one (1) island for every seven (7) parking spaces. A landscape island
 8             shall contain at least twenty-five (25) square feet, shall be at least four (4) feet wide, and
 9             shall prevent vehicles from damaging trees, such as by using a wheel stop or curb. Islands
10             may include storm-water facility design components, such as bioretention features.

11        4.   At least one (1) tree shall be planted in each landscape island. Trees in landscape islands
12             shall reach a mature height of thirty (30) feet or more, cast moderate to dense shade in the
13             summer, live at least sixty (60) years, require little maintenance (such as by being insect-
14             , disease- and drought-resistant and not producing fruit), and be suited for use in the
15             proposed location (such as by being tolerant of pollution and direct and reflected heat).

16   F.   Establishing Setback Standards for Retaining Walls and Fences.

17        1.   This section regulates the height of retaining walls and fences along the perimeter of sites.
18             Building codes specify the circumstances under which retaining walls and fences require
19             building and grading permits. Changes to stormwater runoff resulting from construction
20             of retaining walls are subject to Chapter 40.386.

21        2.   Construction of private retaining walls or fences within public rights-of-way is prohibited.
22             Exceptions to this prohibition require approval of the Public Works Director.

23        3.   The construction of retaining walls four (4) feet or less in height and fences seven (7) feet
24             or less in height may be constructed within easements. This subsection does not exempt
25             retaining walls or fences from otherwise applicable easement provisions.

26        4.   The construction of retaining walls in excess of four (4) feet in height and fences in excess
27             of seven (7) feet in height shall meet the setback requirements of the underlying zone,
28             except as authorized below. The height of a fence on top of retaining walls shall be
29             measured to the grade at the bottom of the wall. Each of the following is an exception to
30             the height and setback requirements of Section 40.320.010(F)(4):

31             a.   Retaining walls taller than four (4) feet may be placed within setbacks by using a
32                  series of retaining walls four (4) feet high or less that are separated a minimum of eight
33                  (8) feet, provided the area between walls is maintained in ground cover or shrubs.
34                  The total height of a series of walls within the building setback shall not exceed eight
35                  (8) feet. Landscaping shall be maintained consistent with Section 40.320.010(G)(6).
36                  Fences are allowed on top of such walls consistent with Section 40.320.010(F)(4)(h)
37                  and Figure 40.320.010-8;

38             b.   The exposed faces of retaining walls over four (4) feet in height must be directed
39                  toward the interior of the lot;

40             c.   The retaining walls and/or fences are constructed as part of the site improvements
41                  prior to a final plat, and located between lots within the development. Retaining
42                  walls on the perimeter of the plat may not use this exception, except as allowed under




                                                                                                                 175
 1                   subsection (F)(4)(d) of this section;
 2             d.    Retaining walls and/or fences abutting a road right-of-way or road easement; subject to the
 3                   following:

 4                   (1) The wall or fence does not block required sight distance;
 5                   (2) Walls and/or fences over twelve (12) feet in height will be reviewed for potential
 6                        shading and visual impacts beyond the right-of-way or easement. The Community
 7                        Development Director may impose conditions on the design and setbacks of such
 8                        walls if needed to mitigate impacts;

 9             e.    The retaining wall and/or fence is constructed between lots under the same ownership;

10             f.    Permission to exceed the height limits within the setback is granted in writing from the
11                   abutting property owner;

12             g.    The retaining wall and/or fence is abutting commercial or industrial zoned property or
13                   legally permitted nonresidential uses;

14             h.    Non-sight-obscuring fences such as chain link or wrought iron seven (7) feet high or less
15                   and sight-obscuring fences forty-two (42) inches high on top of retaining walls no greater
16                   than four (4) feet tall are allowed within setbacks per Figure 40.320.010-8.

17        5.   Building codes for retaining walls may require setbacks that are greater than those required by
18             Section 40.320.010(F)(4).

19        6.   These height and setback limitations do not apply to fences required by state law to enclose
20             public utilities, or to chain link fences enclosing school grounds or public recreation areas.




21
22
23   G.   Timing, Selection, Installation, and Maintenance Standards.

24        1.    Timing. That applicant shall install landscaping and screening required by this section
25              consistent with the approved site plan or an approved modification thereto before the
26              county issues an occupancy permit or final inspection for the development in question;



                                                                                                          176
 1        provided, the responsible official may defer installation of plant materials for up to six
 2        (6) months after the county issues an occupancy permit or final inspection for the
 3        development in question if the responsible official finds doing so increases the likely
 4        survival of plants.

 5   2.   Shrubs and Groundcover Selection. All required groundcover plants and shrubs must be
 6        of sufficient size and number to meet the required standards within three (3) years of
 7        planting. Mulch (as a groundcover) must be confined to areas underneath plants and is not
 8        a substitute for living groundcover plants, lawn or approved flowers.

 9         a.    Shrubs shall be supplied in a minimum of three (3) gallon containers or equivalent
10               burlap balls, with a minimum spread of eighteen (18) inches to meet the L2 buffer
11               requirement, and minimum of five (5) gallon containers or equivalent burlap balls
12               with a minimum spread of thirty (30) inches to meet the L3 buffer requirements.
13               Reduction in the minimum size may be permitted if certified by a registered
14               landscape architect that the reduction shall not diminish the intended effect or the
15               likelihood the plants will survive.

16         b.    Groundcover plants shall be placed not more than thirty (30) inches on center and
17               thirty (30) inches between rows. Rows of plants shall be staggered for a more
18               effective covering. Groundcover shall be supplied in a minimum four (4) inch size
19               container or a two and one-quarter (2¼)
20               inch container or equivalent if planted eighteen (18) inches on center. Reduction in the
21               minimum size may be permitted if certified by a registered landscape architect that the
22               reduction shall not diminish the intended effect or the likelihood the plants will survive.
23               A lawn or flower bed of flowers approved by the responsible official may be substituted
24               for groundcover plants.

25   3.   Tree Selection. Trees may be deciduous or evergreen. The required tree height shall be measured
26        from the ground level at final planting to the top of the tree.

27        a.    Required trees for parking and loading areas shall be a minimum caliper of two (2) inches
28              and a minimum height of ten (10) feet at the time of planting.

29        b.    Required deciduous trees (other than street trees) shall be fully branched, have a minimum
30              caliper of one and one-half (1½) inches, and a minimum height of eight (8) feet at the time
31              of planting.

32        c.    Required evergreen trees (other than street trees) shall be fully branched and a minimum
33              of six (6) feet high at the time of planting.

34        d.    If the responsible official decides reducing the minimum size of trees will not detract from
35              the desired effect of the trees, the minimum size of trees (other than street trees) may be
36              reduced if the applicant submits a written statement by a landscape architect registered in
37              Washington or expert in the growing of the tree(s) in question certifying that the reduction
38              in size at planting will not decrease the likelihood the trees will survive.

39        e.    See Section 40.320.010(E)(4) regarding trees in landscape islands in parking lots.

40        f.    See Section 40.320.020 regarding street trees and vegetation in the right-of-way. Such
41              required trees and vegetation may be determined by the responsible official to meet aspects
42              of the landscape buffer requirements of this section.




                                                                                                        177
 1        4.    Selection Generally. Landscape materials should be selected and sited to produce a hardy and
 2              drought-resistant landscape area. Selection should include consideration of soil type and depth,
 3              the amount of maintenance required, spacing, exposure to sun and wind, the slope and contours
 4              of the site, compatibility with existing native vegetation preserved on the site, water
 5              conservation where needed, and the impact of landscaping on visibility of the site for purposes
 6              of public safety and surveillance. Landscaping materials shall be selected in accordance with a
 7              list of plant materials found in the Standard Details Manual.

 8        5.    Installation Standards. The applicant shall show and comply with the following:

 9              a.   Plant materials will be installed to current nursery industry standards.
10              b.   Plant materials shall be properly supported to ensure survival. Support devices such
11                   as guy wires or stakes shall not interfere with vehicular or pedestrian movement.

12              c.   Existing trees and plant materials to be retained shall be protected during
13                   construction, such as by use of chain link or other sturdy fence placed at the dripline
14                   of trees to be retained. Grading, topsoil storage, construction material storage,
15                   vehicles and equipment shall not be allowed within the dripline of trees to be
16                   retained.

17        6.    Maintenance. Maintenance of landscaped areas is the ongoing responsibility of the
18              property owner. Required landscaping must be continuously maintained in a healthy
19              manner. Plants that die must be replaced with in-kind materials unless otherwise
20              authorized by the responsible official. Vegetation shall be controlled by pruning,
21              trimming or otherwise so that it will not interfere with the maintenance or repair of any
22              public utility, restrict pedestrian or vehicular access, or obstruct sight distance at
23              intersections as provided in Section 40.320.020.

24   H.   Plant List.
25        The county shall maintain a plant list to assist in administration of this chapter (see the
26        Standard Details Manual).

27   I.   Verification of the Installation of Required Landscape.
28        Prior to the issuance of an approval of occupancy for a site plan, the applicant shall provide
29        verification in accordance with Section 40.320.030(B) that the required landscape has been
30        installed in accordance with the approved landscape plan(s).

 2   EXHIBIT 27
 3
 4   40.340.010 Parking and Loading Standards

 5   A.   General.

 6        1.   Applicability. Except as otherwise provided by the UDC, required off-street parking and loading spaces
 7             shall be improved and maintained as set forth in this section for all uses in all zoning districts.

 8        2.   Timing. Parking and loading spaces required for a given use or development shall be provided
 9             consistent with the approved site plan before the county issues an occupancy permit or final inspection
10             for the use or development in question.

11        3.   Availability.

12             a.    Parking spaces required for a given use or development shall be available for the parking of
13                   operable passenger automobiles of residents, customers, patrons and employees only, and shall
14                   not be used for the storage of vehicles or materials, or for the parking of trucks used in conducting
15                   the business or use.

16             b.    Loading spaces required for a given use or development shall be available for loading and
17                   unloading of trucks and similar vehicles.

18             c.    Required off-street parking spaces may not be used for loading or unloading unless the responsible
19                   official finds that loading and unloading in those spaces will occur during hours of the day when
20                   the spaces are not needed for parking.

21        4.   Location of Parking and Loading Facilities.

22             a.    Off-street parking spaces for dwellings shall be located on the same lot as the dwelling.

23             b.    Off-street parking spaces for other uses shall be located on the same lot as the use or on another
24                   lot not more than three hundred (300) feet from the building or use they are intended to serve,
25                   measured in a straight line from the building; provided, where required parking for a use or
26                   development will be located on a lot other than the lot on which the use it serves is located, then,
27                   before the county issues a building permit for the use or development, the applicant shall submit
28                   an agreement executed by the owner of the lot where the off-site parking is proposed authorizing
29                   use of the lot for the proposed parking spaces for the use in question.

30             c.    Loading spaces for a given use shall be situated on the same lot as the use it serves, except as
31                   provided for joint use of facilities.

32             d.    Parking and loading spaces shall not be located in a required front setback, except in the case of
33                   single-family or duplex dwellings. Access drives and maneuvering areas for parking and loading
34                   spaces shall not be located in a required front setback, except to the minimum extent




                                                                                                                   181
 1                   practicable for access to the site.
 2         e.        Parking spaces for residential development may be arranged in tandem, with two or more vehicles,
 3                   one in front of or behind the others with a single means of ingress and egress, at the rate of one space
 4                   for every twenty (20) linear feet with any necessary provisions for turning radius.

 5   5.    Joint Use of Facilities. The responsible official may authorize use of given off-street parking
 6         and/or loading spaces by two (2) or more uses if:

 7          a.        The responsible official finds the applicant has shown that the uses that share the parking
 8                    and/or loading do not require that parking and/or loading at the same time; and

 9          b.        Where shared parking or loading for a use or development will be located on a lot other
10                    than the lot on which the use it serves is located, then, before the county issues a building
11                    permit for the uses or developments on one (1) lot that will be served by the shared parking
12                    and/or loading on another lot, the applicant shall submit an agreement executed by the
13                    owner of the off-site lot where parking and/or loading is proposed authorizing use of the
14                    lot for the proposed parking and/or loading spaces for the use or development in question
15                    subject to terms and conditions that assure parking and/or loading will be available for
16                    each use it serves as provided in Section 40.340.010(A)(5)(a).

17    6.    Change or Expansion of a Use. A site plan that changes the use of an existing structure or lot
18          shall provide off-street parking and loading for the new use as required by this section. A site
19          plan that enlarges an existing structure or use shall provide for additional parking and loading
20          based only on the parking and loading requirements of the expansion, except as otherwise
21          provided in Chapter 40.530.

22    7.    Lighting. Light fixtures in parking or loading areas shall be consistent with RCW 47.36.180 on
23          public roadways and not cast significant light or glare off site on adjacent properties.

24    8.    Surfacing. All parking and loading spaces and related access drives, maneuvering, and vehicle
25          storage areas shall be paved to standards, including the use of permeable pavements and grass
26          block pavers, as approved by the responsible official except as follows:

27              a.     Driveways leading to parking and maneuvering areas for unoccupied utility and wireless
28                     communication facilities need not be paved, except as required by Section 40.350.030(B)
29                     (7)(c) (this still requires the first twenty (20) to twenty-five (25) feet of driveway to be
30                     paved so gravel does not enter the paved road);

31              b.     Three (3) or fewer parking spaces serving unoccupied utility and wireless communication
32                     facilities need not be paved;

33              c.     Transitional uses such as coffee and food stands approved under Section 40.260.055;

34              d.     Driveways used only for fire access purposes;
35              e.     Parking areas for uses that receive access from unpaved roads;

36              f.     Reuse of existing legally placed parking areas in the rural area of the county; provided, that
37                     proposed new parking and required ADA parking spaces and ADA accessways are paved;

38              g.     Agricultural stands and markets as allowed under Section 40.260.025;

39              h.     Rural kennels as allowed under Section 40.260.110;




                                                                                                                      182
 1             i.    Wineries as allowed under Section 40.260.245; and

 2             j.    Use of existing legally placed parking areas for residential development, up to a maximum of six
 3                   (6) parking spaces; and
 4
 5             jk. Other uses as approved by the responsible official.

 6        9.   Drainage. Stormwater drainage facilities for parking and loading spaces and related access drives and
 7             maneuvering areas shall comply with Chapter 40.386.

 8        10. Wheel Stops and Curbs.
 9             a.    Parking and/or loading spaces on the perimeter of a parking lot or abutting interior landscaped
10                   areas or sidewalks shall include a wheel stop or curb at least four (4) inches high located three
11                   (3) feet back from the front of the parking and/or loading space. Include breaks in curbs, as
12                   necessary, to allow flow of stormwater to LID stormwater facilities.

13             b.    The front three (3) feet of a parking space may be improved with a low-growing vegetated LID
14                   feature, instead of asphalt or concrete pavement. However, this area shall not be counted toward
15                   landscape or open space area requirements unless it is part of a LID storm-water feature.

16             c.    The perimeter of a parking or loading area and access and maneuvering drives associated with
17                   them shall be improved with a curb, rail or equivalent so that vehicles do not extend over a
18                   property line, sidewalk or public or private street. Breaks must be provided along the perimeter
19                   feature to allow stormwater movement to LID stormwater features.

20        11. Maintenance of Parking and Loading Areas. Required parking and loading spaces and associated
21              access and maneuvering drives shall be maintained in good repair at all times.

22   B.    Calculation of Parking Requirements.
23         A site plan for a given use or uses shall show that at least the number of parking spaces required by
24         Table 40.340.010-4 will be provided consistent with this section unless otherwise consistent with the
25         UDC.

26        1.    Where Table 40.340.010-4 requires a certain number of parking spaces based on the area of a
27              building, the area shall be the gross floor area of the structure, as defined in Section 40.100.070
28              (“floor area, gross”) excluding the area of a building that encloses parking or loading spaces.
29        2.    Where more than one (1) use occupies a given structure or parcel of land or where a given
30              business includes a combination of uses, the minimum required number of parking spaces shall
31              be the sum of the requirements for each use, except to the extent the uses comply with the
32              requirements of this section for shared parking.

33        3.    Where a building may be used for more than one (1) purpose and the applicant does not limit
34              the permitted uses in the building, parking spaces shall be provided based on the possible use(s)
35              that require the most parking spaces.

36        4.    Where Table 40.340.010-4 does not list the parking requirements for a proposed use, the
37              responsible official shall determine the minimum parking requirements for the use, based on
38              requirements in Table 40.340.010-4 for other similar uses, if any, or on substantial evidence
39              of parking needs for similar uses in other, similar locations.

40        5.    Up to thirty percent (30%) of required parking spaces and all parking spaces in excess of
41              minimum requirements may comply with the standards for compact cars in Table 40.340.010-



                                                                                                                    183
 1              5.

 2        6.    All parking areas shall comply with applicable local, state and federal standards regarding
 3              parking for disabled persons.

4         7.    The review authority may reduce the required number of parking spaces to less than that
5               required in Table 40.340.010-4 as part of site plan review application or other application if the
6               review authority finds that a lesser number of off-street parking spaces will be enough to fulfill
7               all parking needs of the use or development, based on substantial evidence in the application,
8               such as an adequate survey of parking demand at similar uses under similar conditions. The
9               number of parking spaces for disabled persons may not be reduced under this subsection.

10        8.    The review authority may allow parking credits established through a neighborhood parking
11              plan adopted pursuant to Section 40.340.030 to meet the number of spaces required by Table
12              40.340.010-4. The number of credits for an individual parcel shall be as established in the
13              approved neighborhood parking plan.

14   C.   Parking Design Standards. Off-street parking spaces shall comply with the standards for stalls and
15        aisles, as set forth in Table 40.340.010-5 and Figure 40.340.010-1.

16   D.   Loading Space Number and Design Standards.

17        1.    Commercial, industrial and public utility buildings that have a gross floor area of five thousand
18              (5,000) square feet or more shall provide truck loading or unloading spaces in accordance with
19              Table 40.340.010-1:
20
               Table 40.340.010-1. Required Truck Loading or Unloading Spaces for
                                          Buildings 5,000 Square Feet or Greater
               Square Feet of Floor Area                     Number of Berths Required
               Less than 5,000                                      0
               5,000 to 25,000                                      1
               25,000 to 50,000                                     2
               For each additional 50,000, or each fraction         1 additional
               thereof

21        2.    Restaurants, office buildings, hotels, motels, hospitals and institutions, schools and colleges, public
22              buildings, recreation or entertainment facilities, and any similar use that has a gross floor area of
23              thirty thousand (30,000) square feet or more, shall provide off-street truck loading or unloading
24              spaces in accordance with Table 40.340.010-2:
25
                Table 40.340.010-2. Required Truck Loading or Unloading Spaces for
                               Buildings 30,000 Square Feet or Greater
               Square Feet of Floor Area                   Number of Berths Required
               Less than 30,000                            0
               30,000 to 100,000                           1
               100,000 and over                            2




                                                                                                                     184
 1     3.       Loading Berth Design Standards.

 2              a.     All required loading berths shall meet the minimum dimensional standards shown in Table
 3                     40.340.010-3. Where the vehicles generally used for loading and unloading exceed these
 4                     dimensions, the required length of these berths shall be increased.

 5              b.     In addition to the length of the loading berth, additional maneuvering space may be required by
 6                     the director for any uses with over ten thousand (10,000) square feet of gross floor area with
 7                     loading berth access from a collector arterial or local access street, especially if located across
 8                     the street from another high-demand use. When required, the additional maneuvering space
 9                     shall be designed and arranged to allow the most efficient use of all required loading berths by
10                     motor vehicles of the types typically employed by the activities served. The minimum
11                     maneuvering width between the outermost point of the loading berth and the limit of the
12                     maneuvering area shall be fifty (50) feet.

13              c.     Loading spaces shall be located so that trucks shall not obstruct pedestrian or vehicle traffic
14                     movement or project into any public right-of-way. All loading space areas shall be separated
15                     from parking areas and shall be designated as truck loading spaces.

16         4.    Loading berths on the perimeter of the site shall be screened to a minimum L4
17               landscape standards. If the adjoining property is within an industrial or commercial district
18               and contains similar uses that are compatible with loading facilities then this standards may
19               not be required. Any loading space located within one hundred (100) feet of areas zoned for
20               residential use shall be screened and operated as necessary to reduce noise and visual
21               impacts. Noise mitigation measures may include architectural or structural barriers, beams,
22               walls, or restrictions on the hours of operation.

23         5.    The review authority may reduce the number or size of required loading spaces to less than that
24               required as part of site plan review or other application if the review authority finds that a
25               lesser number or size of loading spaces will be adequate to serve the expected needs of the
26               development.
