19   c.    Yield-Controlled Intersections. For roads with a posted speed of twenty-five (25) mph or
20         less, traffic entering an uncontrolled public road from a yield-controlled public road shall
21         have minimum intersection sight distance of two hundred fifty (250) feet. The intersection



                                                                                                  214
 1              sight distance
 2              shall be measured at one hundred thirty (130) feet back on the yield-controlled
 3              approach from the line that is four (4) feet from the uncontrolled roadway center,
 4              in drivers’ direction, for both approaches.

 5         d.   Uncontrolled Intersections. Uncontrolled intersections for access roads in urban and
 6              rural areas with a posted speed limit of twenty-five (25) mph or less shall have an
 7              unobstructed intersection sight distance triangle per Section 40.350.030(B)(8)(b) of
 8              one hundred (100) feet on both approaches. This requirement may be reduced to
 9              eighty (80) feet for intersections abutting corner lots in an urban residential
10              subdivision. The intersection sight distance shall be measured along the lines four
11              (4) feet from the roadway center, in drivers’ direction, for both approaches.

12         e.   New Urban and Rural Residential Driveways. New urban and rural residential
13              driveways accessing roads with a speed limit of over twenty-five (25) mph are
14              subject to Table 40.350.030-8.

15   9.   Street Extensions.

16        a.    General Requirements. Where a public or private road has been constructed, created
17              or stubbed in such a manner as to be able to be extended or widened in accordance
18              with the Clark County Arterial Atlas, other requirements of this section, or prior
19              approved development, the following shall apply:

20              (1) Connection with Adjacent Areas. All residences, buildings or structures shall
21                   be constructed in such a position on the property that they will not interfere with
22                   the extension or widening of the roadway to adjacent areas and shall be so
23                   situated that such extension will make orderly and planned development for
24                   additional road installations to meet the reasonable minimum requirements of
25                   good and safe traffic circulation, consistent with applicable zoning setbacks.

26              (2) Right-of-Way for Street Extensions. Right-of-way or private easements
27                   necessary to such extension or widening and falling within parcels being
28                   developed shall be granted or created as a condition of development approval.

29        b.    Urban Developments.

30              (1) Provisions for Future Extensions. Any street within the urban area for which
31                   an extension in the future is planned shall be extended to the edge of the
32                   property being developed through the plat, short plat or site plan approval
33                   process, unless otherwise approved by the review authority. The street stub
34                   shall be a full street section, including sidewalks.

35              (2) Use of Temporary Turnaround. If a road serving more than eighteen (18)
36                   dwelling units or more than one hundred fifty (150) feet in length temporarily
37                   terminates at a property boundary, a temporary turnaround cul-de-sac bulb
38                   consistent with this standard shall be constructed near the plat boundary. The bulb
39                   shall be paved and shall be eighty (80) feet in diameter, which may include the width
40                   of the roadway with side-walks, where required, terminating at the point where the
41                   bulb radius begins. Removal of the temporary turnaround and extension of the
42                   sidewalk shall be the responsibility of the developer who extends the road (see the
43                   Standard Details Manual). The easement for a temporary turnaround may be




                                                                                                             215
 1                   extinguished without county approval after the temporary turnaround is determined
 2                   to be no longer necessary by the county.

 3             (3) Barricades. for placement of temporary and permanent barricades, see Section
 4                   40.350.030(C)(4)(f).

 5       c.    Rural Developments. For any road in the rural area for which an extension is planned, the
 6             right-of-way falling within parcels being developed shall be dedicated where the existing
 7             platting pattern, the development under review and the potential for development of
 8             adjacent lots demonstrate a need for the dedication.

 9   10. Private Roads.
10        a.   Purpose. The purpose of private road standards is to provide an option to retain rural
11             character, reduce costs to serve large rural lots, and allow more control, security, and sense
12             of identity when public roads are not needed for public circulation.
13             For private road maintenance agreement, private road inspection, and developer
14             maintenance obligation for private roads, see Section 40.350.030(C)(4)(g).

15        b.   Approval Criteria and Requirements.

16             (1) Approval Criteria – General. Private roads are not allowed in either the urban or rural
17                   area:

18                   (a) When they conflict with the Arterial Atlas, as amended; or
19                   (b) When they are needed for public circulation; or
20                   (c) When they connect two (2) public roads (except for commercial or industrial
21                           uses in urban areas); or

22                   (d) When they are to serve more than fifty (50) potential residential lots in rural areas
23                           or one hundred (100) lots in urban areas, created after April 12, 1994, except
24                           within a planned unit development; provided, that where expansion of a public
25                           road is not presently feasible, the limitations of this subsection shall not apply
26                           if the affected internal and frontage roads are
27                            improved to public standards (if otherwise required) and dedicated to
28                            the county, with the acceptance of such dedication(s) being deferred
29                            until extension of a public road allows connection.

30             (2) Additional Requirements for Urban Private Roads. Private roads within
31                   developments may be allowed, provided they meet the following additional
32                   criteria:

33                  (a) Structural sections shall be the same as for public roads of equivalent
34                        classification;

35                  (b) A pedestrian access plan shall be approved;
36                  (c) Internal traffic calming measures or devices such as speed humps or traffic
37                        circles may be required.

38        c.   Notice. The following statement is required on the face of any site plan, or binding
39             site plan, or within the Developer Covenants to Clark County for any subdivision or



                                                                                                         216
 1              short plat containing a private road:
 2              Clark County has no responsibility to improve or maintain the private roads
 3              contained within or private roads providing access to the property described in
 4              this development. Any private access street shall remain a private street unless
 5              it is upgraded to public street standards at the expense of the developer or abutting
 6              lot owners to include hard surface paving and is accepted by the county for public
 7              ownership and maintenance.

 8        d.    Private bridges are addressed in Section 40.350.040.

 9   11. Joint Driveways. A maximum of four (4) legal lots may use a joint driveway to access a
10        public or private road.

11   12. Cul-de-Sacs and Turnarounds.
12        a.    Cul-de-Sacs.

13              (1) Short Cul-de-Sacs. The minimum right-of-way diameter across the bulb section
14                   is seventy (70) feet, with a minimum pavement diameter across the bulb section
15                   of sixty (60) feet. Rolled curb and gutter and thickened sidewalk shall be
16                   constructed in accordance with the Standard Details Manual.

17              (2) Whenever a residential urban cul-de-sac street is more than one hundred fifty
18                   (150) feet long, a bulb or hammerhead shall be constructed as follows:

19                    (a) Standard Cul-de-Sac Turnarounds. The minimum right-of-way diameter
20                         across the bulb section is one hundred (100) feet, with a minimum
21                         pavement diameter across the bulb of ninety (90)
22                         feet. Alternatively, right-of-way may be reduced to eighty (80) feet, with a
23                         paved diameter across the bulb of seventy (70) feet, provided rolled curb and
24                         gutter with thickened sidewalk is constructed in accordance with the Standard
25                         Details Manual.

26                    (b) Temporary Cul-de-Sacs. Temporary cul-de-sacs shall provide at minimum
27                         paved diameter of eighty (80) feet for the bulb. Those portions of the bulb area
28                         lying outside the straight-street right-of-way shall be provided as a temporary
29                         easement pending extension of the street.

30                   (c) Urban Cul-de-Sac or Eyebrow Island. Optional feature for any cul-de-sac when
31                         the bulb’s paved diameter is ninety (90) feet or less; mandatory when the bulb’s
32                         paved diameter exceeds ninety (90) feet. If provided, islands shall have full-
33                         depth vertical curbs. Minimum island diameter shall be twenty (20) feet and
34                         there shall be at least twenty-two (22) feet of paved traveled way in a shoulder
35                         type section; thirty (30) feet of paved traveled way in a curb type section around
36                         the circumference. Islands shall be grassed or landscaped. Islands shall be
37                         maintained by the adjoining lot owners. Islands are required on eyebrows with
38                         a radius greater than thirty (30) feet. The minimum island diameter shall be ten
39                         (10) feet.

40              (3) In the urban and urban holding areas of the county, a permanent cul-de-sac shall not
41                   be longer than six hundred (600) feet measured from curb line of intersecting street to
42                   the center of the bulb section. Proposed modifications to this rule will be considered
43                   by the review authority, based on pertinent traffic planning factors such as



                                                                                                        217
1         topography, sensitive areas and existing development.

 2   (4) In the rural area of the county, there is no limitation to the length of a dead-end road
 3        or cul-de-sac; provided, however, that approved turnarounds on roads greater than
 4        one-half (½) mile long are provided every one thousand three hundred twenty (1,320)
 5        feet or as close to that distance as practical considering topography, natural features
 6        and existing manmade structures. Approved turnarounds may include cul-de-sacs,
 7        which may include the width of the roadway, intersecting public or private roads,
 8        hammerheads, or driveways meeting the dimensional requirements of a hammerhead.
 9        Within the wildland urban interface/intermix, approved turnarounds shall be
10        provided every one thousand (1,000) feet. Dimensional requirements for all
11        transportation-related features in this subsection are as drawn in the Standard Details
12        Manual. Notwithstanding the foregoing, the development approval authority may
13        restrict the length of a dead-end road or cul-de-sac where it is clearly shown that either:




                                                                                              218
 1                   (a) There is a practical alternative design that results in significantly superior
 2                         actual or potential road connectivity or emergency services accessibility;
 3                         or

 4                   (b) The presence of unstable slopes, flood risk, or other road blockage hazard
 5                         presents a significant potential for isolating a substantial area from
 6                         emergency services.

 7              (5) The review authority may require an off-street accessway or an emergency
 8                   vehicle access to connect a cul-de-sac at its terminus with other streets, parks,
 9                   schools, bus stops, or other pedestrian traffic generators, if the need exists for
10                   pedestrian and bike circulation.

11         b.    Turnarounds.

12              (1) When four (4) or more lots are served on a private road greater than one
13                    hundred fifty (150) feet in length, an approved turnaround shall be provided
14                    at the end of the private road. Easements may be required to be expanded to
15                    accommodate turnaround requirements.

16              (2) Approved turnarounds may include cul-de-sacs with an approved diameter in
17                    accordance with the Standard Details Manual (which may include the width of
18                    the roadway, or hammerheads, intersecting public or private roads or any
19                    alternative design approved through the road modification process.
20                    Dimensional requirements for all transportation related features in this
21                    subsection are as drawn in the Standard Details Manual.

22              (3) For those areas identified as wildland urban interface/intermix, refer to Chapter
23                    15.13 as amended.

24   13. Urban Neighborhood Traffic Management.
25         a.    Purposes. Urban neighborhood traffic management is intended to manage traffic
26               speeds within residential neighborhoods and to discourage external traffic cutting
27               through residential neighborhoods.

28         b.    Applicability. The provisions of this section shall apply only to access roads within
29               a development in the R1-5, R1-6, R1-7.5, R1-10, R1-20, R-12, R-18, R-22, R-30,
30               R-43, OR-15, OR-18, OR-22, OR-30, OR-43 R-24, R-36, R-50, OR-24, OR-36,
31               OR-50, CR-1, CR-2, or MX zoning districts which meet one (1) of the following
32               conditions:

33              (1) Projected average daily trip of greater than six hundred (600) and less than
34                    two thousand (2,000) motor vehicles and a sight distance in excess of six
35                    hundred (600) feet; or

36              (2) Determined by the County Engineer, not his or her designee, that traffic
37                    calming measures and/or traffic calming devices are warranted.
38                     The review authority may waive the requirements of this section for Type I and
39                     Type II applications where the conditions listed above will not occur.

40         c.    Standards and Requirements. If the condition in Section 40.350.030(B)(13)(b) occurs,
41               traffic calming measures and/or traffic calming devices shall be required:



                                                                                                          219
 1                   (1) Traffic Calming Measures. Traffic calming measures, such as “T” intersections,
 2                           street trees, curvilinear streets, or entry treatments, shall be incorporated into the
 3                           overall development design to manage traffic speeds.

 4                   (2) Traffic Calming Devices. Traffic calming devices, such as speed bumps/humps and
 5                           the devices shown in the Standard Details Manual, or as approved by the review
 6                           authority, shall be installed.

 7        14. Urban Transit Circulation Standards. New residential, commercial and industrial developments
 8             shall be reviewed with the participation of C-TRAN invited during the development review
 9             process under Subtitle 40.5 to ensure appropriate design and integration of transit facilities into
10             the development.

11        15. Right-of-Way Standards. Dedication of arterial right-of-way shall occur within developments in
12             accordance with the transportation element of the comprehensive plan.

13             a.    Public Roads. The minimum right-of-way and roadway widths for all public urban and
14                   rural roads shall be as shown on the design criteria detailed in Section 40.350.030(B)(3).
15                   Right-of-way widths, roadway widths and structural sections shall be consistent with
16                   projected traffic volumes planned for twenty (20) years from the time of design. Additional
17                   right-of-way or easement may be required where necessary to accommodate slopes, sight
18                   distance or other features necessary for maintenance or to enhance safety.

19             b.    Urban Planned Unit and Multifamily Developments. When constructed, public roads shall
20                   be constructed according to the design criteria of Section 40.350.030(B)(3); provided,
21                   parking may be deleted when it is shown that four (4) non-tandem off-street parking spaces
22                   per unit are provided and distinct signs and markings show that no parking is permitted;
23                   and sidewalks along internal circulation roadways may be deleted by the review authority
24                   when it is shown that public walkways adequate for pedestrian circulation and meeting or
25                   exceeding the requirements of Section 40.350.015 are provided.

26             c.    Commercial, Office Campus and Industrial Areas. When constructed, public roads shall
27                   be constructed according to the design criteria of Section 40.350.030(B)(3). The structure
28                   of the road shall be designed according to minor arterial standards.

29   C.   Specifications for Design and Construction.

30                      1.     Transportation Standard Specifications.

31              a.    Transportation Standards. The standards for Clark County roads and bridges, and
32                    all other construction within publicly owned rights-of-way, shall consist of:

33                   (1) The current published edition of the Standard Specifications for Road, Bridge
34                        and Municipal Construction as published by the Washington Department of
35                        Transportation (WSDOT) and the American Public Works Association
36                        (APWA), referred to as standard specifications;

37                   (2) The current Standard Plans for Road and Bridge Construction as published by
38                        WSDOT and APWA (referred to as standard plans); and

39                   (3) The Standard Details Manual as defined in Section 40.100.070, and issued by
40                        the County Engineer, containing typical drawings to implement transportation,
41                        erosion control, drainage, and other engineering standards adopted in the Clark



                                                                                                            220
 1                  County Code.

 2        b.    Supplemental Standards. To implement the above standards, the following
 3              publications and their subsequent revisions are adopted and shall apply:

 4             (1) The WSDOT Design Manual;
 5             (2) The WSDOT Construction Manual;
 6             (3) The WSDOT Hydraulics Manual;
7              (4) A Policy on Geometric Design of Highways and Streets prepared by the
8                   American Association of State Highway and Transportation Officials
9                   (AASHTO);

10             (5) The Washington State adopted Manual on Uniform Traffic Control Devices
11                  (MUTCD) prepared by the U.S. Department of Transportation, Federal
12                  Highway Administration;

13             (6) Chapter 40.386, Stormwater and Erosion Control;
14             (7) Chapter 51-304 WAC, state of Washington adoption of the Americans with
15                  Disabilities Act into the International Building Code; and

16             (8) The AASHTO LRFD Bridge Design Specifications, U.S. Customary Units,
17                  including its commentary (refer to Section 40.350.040, Private Bridges, for
18                  exceptions to this manual).
19        c.    Conflict of Standards. In the event of conflict with any of the specifications, the County
20              Engineer shall specify which of the supplemental specifications will apply.

21   2.   Construction Plan Requirements for Transportation and Utility Improvements. The applicant
22        shall submit to the responsible official plans and specifications for street and utility construction
23        for the proposed development. The plans and specifications shall include a vicinity map, a plan
24        and profile, special provisions, and reference to the standard specifications, including the typical
25        sections.

26        a.    The Plan. The plan shall include the road alignment at a scale of not less than one (1) inch
27              to fifty (50) feet (where less detail is required, a scale of one (1) inch to one hundred (100)
28              feet may be approved by the responsible official) showing centerline stationing on all
29              intersecting streets, with bearings on centerlines; curve data on all horizontal curves;
30              right-of-way; relevant topography; existing and proposed utility location; street names in
31              the development and adjoining the development; typical roadway section showing
32              placement of utilities; existing and proposed drainage and water quality appurtenances;
33              sidewalk ramp locations; floodplain and wetland boundaries; signalization,
34              channelization, striping and signing; sufficient topographic data adjacent to the site to
35              identify cut and fill limits from the proposed improvements and such further data as may
36              be required by the responsible official.

37        b.    The Profile. The profile shall show the relevant original ground lines using the same
38              stationing as in the plan, control elevations, grade line showing the proposed grades,
39              vertical curves, all bench marks, the vertical datum, and such further information as may
40              be reasonably required by the responsible official. For new streets, the relevant original
41              ground lines will show the ground line at centerline at a minimum and also at the edges



                                                                                                        221
 1               of the right-of-way if grade differences are significant (or alternatively, surveyed contour
 2               lines on the plan view). For an existing street, the applicant shall also show the ground line
 3               at the edge of pavement or face of curb, whichever is applicable. The profile lines for
 4               roads extending to the perimeter of any development shall be extended a minimum of
 5               three hundred (300) feet beyond the perimeter to include any change in contours which
 6               would affect the profile of the extension of the proposed road. If vertical control is
 7               available within one-half (½) mile of the project, the applicant shall use the National
 8               Geodetic Vertical Datum of 1929 (1947 adjustment) as a vertical datum. If county
 9               horizontal control, based on the North American Datum of 1983 (1991 adjustment) is
10               available within one-half (½) mile of the project, the applicant shall make a tie to the grid
11               bearing and coordinate of the datum.

12         c.    Required Project Construction Notes. Any required construction notes shall be shown or
13               referenced on the plans.
14         d.    Format. The cover sheet of all plans shall include a statement identifying which
15               standard specifications will apply to the project. Plan and profile may be shown on
16               the same sheet with profiles shown on the bottom half of the sheet. Sheets shall
17               measure twenty-two (22) to twenty-four (24) inches in height by thirty-four (34) to
18               thirty-six (36) inches in length with a borderline of one and one-half (1½) inches on
19               the left side of the length of the sheet and one-half (½) inch on remaining sides.
20               When more than two (2) plan sheets are used, an overall development layout shall
21               be submitted showing the relationship of roads and utilities.

22              (1) A north arrow shall be shown on each plan view sheet of the plans and adjacent
23                    to any other drawing which is not oriented the same as other drawings on the
24                    sheet.

25              (2) Character size shall not be smaller than eight one-hundredths (0.08) inch high.
26              (3) All detail drawings shall be included in the drawings.
27              (4) A title block shall appear on each sheet of the plan set and shall be placed in
28                    the lower right-hand corner of the sheet, across the bottom edge of the sheet or
29                    across the right-hand edge of the sheet. The title block shall include the name
30                    of the project, the engineering firm, the sheet title and the owner, if not shown
31                    on the first sheet.

32   3.   Transportation Design Specifications. The design criteria set out in Tables 40.350.030-2
33        and 40.350.030-3 are adopted as a portion of the Clark County Standard Specifications.
34        Such criteria are applicable to roads located within and adjacent to a development. These
35        criteria are intended for normal conditions. The responsible official may require higher
36        standards for unusual site conditions.

37        a.    Typical Roadway Section. The typical roadway section shall be as shown on the
38              county standard plans. The roadway section used shall be detailed on the
39              construction plans submitted for each new roadway or improvement to an existing
40              roadway. Any deviation from the standard plans for typical cross-section will require
41              a modification pursuant to Section 40.550.010(A).

42        b.    Required Details. The typical section shall show the width of right-of-way, width of
43              roadway, type and compacted depth of surfacing and paving materials, and such
44              other dimensions as may be necessary or required. The location and width of



                                                                                                          222
 1        sidewalks, walkways, curbs or curb and gutter shall also be shown, where applicable.

 2   c.   Surfacing – Depth Determination. The depth of surfacing and paving materials shall
 3        be determined from the standard plans. The depth to be used shall be determined
 4        from the functional use classification of the road to be improved and the AASHTO
 5        soil classification as shown in the Soil Survey of Clark County, Washington,
 6        published by the U.S. Department of Agriculture, Natural Resources Conservation
 7        Service. In the case where the applicant wishes to submit an alternative surfacing
 8        and paving depth design, the applicant shall provide a design to support the proposed
 9        depths. The County Engineer shall establish and maintain criteria and methodologies
10        for performing alternative surfacing and pavement depth design, including verified
11        soil engineering characteristics and traffic loadings for approving such alternative
12        designs.

13   d.   Alternate Surface Treatment. Alternate surface treatments, other than permeable
14        pavement, may only be used upon approval of the County Engineer. The applicant shall
15        supply the County Engineer with specifications for materials and application rates as part
16        of the approval. Permeable pavement is allowed under Section 40.350.030(B)(3)(b)(1)(a),
17        subject to the specifications in the current edition of the LID Technical Guidance Manual.

18   e.   Steeper Grades. Roads constructed in grades steeper than fifteen percent (15%) shall be
19        constructed with Portland cement concrete, with grooved surfacing. Rural roads are
20        exempted from this requirement.

21   f.   Grades and Drainage. Access road drainage facilities shall be designed to meet the
22        requirements of the county stormwater drainage and erosion control ordinances, and be
23        sufficient to prevent water damage or impairment from normal rain flow or surface water.
24        Erosion control shall be provided during construction to maintain the roadways such that
25        mud and debris are minimized consistent with such ordinances.
26        Drainage details and street profile grades shall be shown on the construction plans. In the
27        urban and urban reserve areas, the centerline profile street grades shall not be less than
28        one percent (1%) unless an integral curb and gutter section is used, in which case the
29        minimum grade shall not be less than three-tenths percent (0.3%). If any grading or filling
30        of lots or other areas outside the streets is to be done which exceeds those amounts
31        specified in Chapter 14.07, an overall grading plan shall be submitted as a part of the
32        plans. In rural areas, driveway culverts must be a minimum of twelve (12) inches.
33        Culverts larger than the minimum will be sized by identifying the size of culverts
34        upstream. These standards may be superseded where culverts are placed within waters
35        subject to regulation under Chapter 77.55 or 77.57 RCW, or Chapter 220-660 WAC.

36   g.   Passing Sight Distance. County roadways with centerline striping shall have minimum
37        passing sight distance, as measured from a height of three and one-half (3½) feet to an
38        object of three and one-half (3½) feet, in accordance with Table 40.350.030-9. The passing
39        sight distance shall be based on the most current version of the American Association of
40        State Highway and Transportation Officials (AASHTO) “A Policy on
41        Geometric Design of Highwards and Streets.”

42   h.   School Zone Traffic Control. School zone traffic control shall be updated when
43        impacted by a project in accordance with the “Clark County School Zone Traffic
44        Control Policy.”

45   i.   Pedestrian Crossing Treatment. Appropriate pedestrian crossing treatments shall be



                                                                                                  223
 1              evaluated and provided in accordance with the “Clark County Pedestrian Crossing
 2              Treatment Policy.”

 3        j.    Traffic Control Devices.

 4              (1) Reimbursable. The developer shall reimburse the county for the installation
 5                    and/or modification of all necessary traffic control devices including but not
 6                    limited to street name signs, warning and regulatory signs, pavement markings
 7                    and traffic signals within county right-of-way. The cost of all the traffic
 8                    control devices will be determined on a time and materials basis.

 9              (2) Road Name Signs (Private Road to Private Road). Private road name signs shall
10                    be provided, installed, and maintained by the developer.

11              (3) Road Name Signs (Private Road to Public Road). Private road name signs shall
12                    be provided, installed, and maintained in county right-of-way by the county.

13              (4) Exceptions. Except for traffic signal related items, all other traffic control
14                    devices related to private roads shall be provided, installed and maintained by
15                    the developer outside county right-of-way. In some unusual circumstances,
16                    traffic control devices for private roads, such as stop control, may be installed
17                    and maintained by the developer within county right-of-way under a licensing
18                    agreement.

19   4.   Transportation Construction Specification.

20         a.    General. No construction shall begin until plans have been approved by the county,
21               except that rough grading operations may proceed before the plans are approved
22               under the following conditions:

23              (1) The grading plan is submitted separately along with an application for a
24                    grading permit, if required;

25              (2) The grading plan is in conformance with the approved preliminary plat or
26                    other development approval;

27              (3) The grading plan will not be in conflict with the street and drainage plans; and
28              (4) Any required grading permit is issued. No utility installation is allowed under
29                    grading permits. The responsible official shall be notified not less than
30                    forty-eight (48) hours prior to the start of any phase of construction.

31         b.    Subgrade. The subgrade must be inspected and approved by the responsible official prior
32               to application of the crushed surfacing material.

33         c.    Crushed Surfacing Materials. The standard specifications shall apply to all materials and
34               workmanship. Compaction of subgrade and surfacing materials shall be in accordance
35               with the WSDOT Standard Specifications. The subgrade and crushed surfacing materials
36               shall be compacted to ninety-five percent (95%) of the maximum density for the material.
37               The base course shall be approved prior to application of top course, and top course shall
38               be approved prior to placement of pavement. Approval shall be by the responsible official.

39         d.    Paving. The standard specifications shall apply to all materials and workmanship. The
40               department shall be notified not less than forty-eight




                                                                                                          224
 1         (48) hours in advance of the application of any type of paving and, in accordance with
 2         the standard specifications, the responsible official may stop or delay paving operations
 3         when the weather or other conditions indicate that suitable results may not be obtained.

 4   e.    Trench Backfill.

 5        (1) Trench Backfill for Construction. All trench backfill within the county right-of-way
 6              and the road improvement area shall be imported gravel backfill meeting the material
 7              specification of the WSDOT Standard Specifications, Section 9-03.19. Native soils
 8              may be utilized upon the responsible official’s approval if testing shows the material
 9              is classified as A-1 or A-3 by AASHTO. Trench backfill shall be compacted within
10              the roadway prism to ninety-five percent (95%) of maximum density as determined
11              by AASHTO T-99. Areas within the right-of-way and outside the roadway prism
12              may be compacted to ninety percent (90%) of AASHTO T-99. The trench backfill
13              shall be placed in conformance with the Standard Specification Section 7-08.3(3).

14        (2) Trench Backfill for Utility. Application of this specification is required on principal
15              and minor arterials, urban collectors, rural major and minor collectors, and any
16              roadway that has been reconstructed or overlaid within two (2) years.
17              Utility trenches in existing roadways and which run transverse to the direction of
18              vehicle travel shall be constructed in accordance with the requirements of the
19              utility cut permit, issued from Clark County’s operations division. In addition to
20              the requirements listed in Section 40.350.030(C)(4)(e)(1), tranverse utility cuts will
21              be required to have the top three (3) feet of trench backfill constructed with
22              controlled density fill meeting the requirements of the Standard Specification
23              Section 2-09.3(1)E. Refer to the Standard Details Manual for examples.

24   f.    Temporary and Permanent Barricades. Temporary and permanent barricades shall
25         conform to the standards described in the current adopted version of the Manual on
26         Uniform Traffic Control Devices (MUTCD). For street extensions, including
27         subtitle connection with adjacent areas, right-of-way for street extension, provision
28         for future extension, and use of temporary turnaround, see Section
29         40.350.030(B)(9).

30        (1) Type I or Type II barricades may be used when traffic is maintained through
31             the temporary traffic control zone.

32        (2) Type III barricades may be used when roadways and/or proposed future
33             roadways are closed to traffic. Type III barricades may extend completely across
34             roadway (as a fence) or from curb to curb. Where provision must be made for
35             access of equipment and authorized vehicles, the Type III barricades may be
36             provided with movable sections that can be closed when work is not in
37             progress, or with indirect openings that will discourage public entry. When job
38             site access is provided through the Type I barricades, the developer/ contractor
39             shall assure proper closure at the end of each working day.

40        (3) In the general case, Type III permanent barricades shall be installed to close
41             arterials or other through streets to traffic. They shall also be used to close off
42             lanes where tapers and/or delineations are not sufficient.

43        (4) Type III barricades or Type 4 (end-of-roadway) object markers shall be used at
44             the end of a local access street terminating abruptly without cul-de-sac bulb or




                                                                                                     225
 1              on temporarily stubbed off streets. Such Type III barricades can be
 2              supplemented with a Type 4 object marker.

 3        (5) Dead-end streets which may be extended in the future shall have a Type III
 4              barricade and a sign placed giving notice that the road will be extended in the
 5              future and an informational telephone number.

 6   g. Private Road Maintenance Agreement. The county will not maintain roadways, signs
 7        or drainage improvements on private roads. All private roads shall be maintained by
 8        the owners of the property served by them and kept in good repair at all times. A
 9        private maintenance covenant recorded with the County Auditor will be required for
10        any private road serving more than three (3) lots. The covenant will set out the terms
11        and conditions of responsibility for maintenance, maintenance methods, standards,
12        distribution of expenses, remedies for noncompliance with the terms of the
13        agreement, right of use easements, and other considerations. The covenant shall be
14        submitted to the responsible official for approval prior to recording. The covenants,
15        which may be in the form set forth in the Standard Details Manual, shall include the
16        following terms:

17        (1) The covenant shall establish minimum annual assessments in an amount adequate to
18              defray costs of ordinary maintenance and procedures for approval of additional
19              needed assessments.

20        (2) The covenant shall include a periodic maintenance schedule.
21        (3) The covenants for maintenance shall be enforceable by any property owner served by
22              the road.

23        (4) The means shall be established for assessing maintenance and repair costs equitably
24              to property owners served by the private road.

25        (5) The covenants shall run with the land.
26        (6) “Maintenance” shall include, but not be limited to, road surfacing, shoulders, gates,
27              signs, storm drainage facilities and vegetation control.

28        (7) Private Road Inspection. Private roads will be subject to the same inspection schedule
29              as public roads.

30        (8) Developer Maintenance Obligation. The developer of a residential plat or short plat
31              shall be responsible to ensure the maintenance of the private road for a period of two
32              (2) years from the date of recording of the plat or short plat. Thereafter, the
33              developer’s maintenance responsibility will depend upon the number of lots under
34              the developer’s continuing ownership, as stated in the recorded maintenance
35              agreement.

36   h.     Construction of Sidewalks.

37         (1) Construction Standards. Materials and construction of sidewalks and accessways
38               shall conform to the standard plans for the type of adjacent road. Sidewalks shall be
39               surfaced with Portland cement concrete. Accessways may be surfaced with Portland
40               cement concrete or two (2) inches of asphaltic concrete pavement on four
41               (4) inches of compacted crushed surfacing. Alternatively, permeable pavement is




                                                                                                   226
 1             allowed, subject to the requirements in Section 40.350.030(B)(3)(b)(1)(a).

 2        (2) Construction Timing. Unless earlier installation is required by the responsible
 3             official, required sidewalks and accessways shall be installed in conjunction with
 4             either the construction of an adjacent road or the construction of a building structure.
 5             That portion of any required sidewalks or accessways adjacent to any lot shall be
 6             constructed prior to issuance of a certificate of occupancy for a building constructed
 7             on such lot.
 8        (3) Construction Bond. If the responsible official determines that construction is
 9             not feasible when a building is ready for occupancy, a construction bond or
10             other security acceptable to the responsible official shall be posted
11             guaranteeing the construction of required sidewalks and accessways. The
12             amount of such construction bond, or other security, shall be set by the
13             responsible official based upon the estimated cost of installation at the time
14             when the sidewalk or accessway is to be constructed.

15        (4) Procedure. Except as modified herein, the reviews, approval, inspection and
16             acceptance procedures established elsewhere in this section shall similarly
17             apply to side-walks and accessways.
18             Note: For sidewalk requirements, see Section 40.350.015(B). For sidewalk
19             detailed specifications, see the Standard Details Manual.

20   i.   Construction Guarantee. In lieu of the completion of any required public
21        improvements prior to approval of a final plat, short plat or the issuance of building
22        permits, the responsible official may accept a bond, in amount and with satisfactory
23        surety and conditions, or other secure method as the responsible official may
24        require, providing for and securing to Clark County the actual construction and
25        installation of such improvements within a period specified by the responsible
26        official and specified in the bond or other agreement, and to be enforced by the
27        Community Development Director by appropriate legal and equitable remedies.
28        The amount of bond or escrow shall be one hundred percent (100%) of the
29        estimated cost as determined by the responsible official for the county to construct
30        the improvement.

31   j.   Record Drawing. The applicant shall submit a reproducible set of plans for all
32        public improvements showing all construction changes, such as location of
33        culverts, alignment and grade changes, added and deleted items, location of
34        utilities, water valves, stormwater facilities, sewer connections, etc. The record
35        drawings shall be prepared and stamped by a licensed engineer or surveyor, and
36        submitted prior to acceptance of any improvements for provisional maintenance by
37        the county.

38   k.   Acceptance by County. Roads, drainage, landscaping, irrigation, and any other
39        required right-of-way construction may be accepted for provisional maintenance by
40        the county upon receipt of a workmanship and materials bond (or other secure
41        method) in the amount of ten percent (10%) of the construction cost and the
42        recommendation of the responsible official. Final acceptance will not be made for
43        two (2) years from the date of provisional acceptance and the developer must repair
44        any failure within the two (2) year period. The applicant may request inspection of
45        the constructed facilities for release of the said workmanship and materials bond
46        (or other secure method) at the end of the two (2) year provisional maintenance




                                                                                                   227
 1                   period.
 2                           Construction Revisions and Modifications to Construction Specifications.
 3                   Revisions made during construction drawing review or during actual construction which
 4                   do not conflict with conditions of development approval or the road standards may be
 5                   authorized by the responsible official. Written consent between the responsible official
 6                   and the developer is required. The developer will be responsible for informing the
 7                   construction contractor of all approved changes. In unique circumstances the responsible
 8                   official will consider requests for variation from the above listed construction
 9                   specifications. It shall be the developer’s responsibility to furnish supporting
10                   documentation as required by the responsible official to substantiate the requested
11                   variation.

 3   40.520.020 Uses Subject to Review and Approval (R/A)
 4   A. Purpose.

 5     Upon review of the responsible official, uses designated as permitted subject to review and approval (R/A) may
 6   be allowed in the various districts; provided, that the responsible official is of the opinion that such uses would be
 7   compatible with neighboring land uses.

 8        (Amended: Ord. 2018-10-02; Ord. 2024-03-02)

 9   B.   Review Procedures.

10      Uses subject to review and approval (R/A) shall be reviewed through a Type II process; provided, that the
11   responsible official, at his or her discretion, may refer any proposal to the hearing examiner for review and
12   approval, or denial. Any uses approved under the provisions of this chapter, by either the responsible official or the
13   hearing examiner in public hearing, shall be compatible with adopted county land use policies and goals.

14        (Amended: Ord. 2018-10-02; Ord. 2024-03-02)

15   C.   Approval Criteria – General.

16      Except for the uses listed in Section 40.520.020(D), in approving a use, the responsible official shall first make a
17   finding that all of the following conditions exist:

18        1.   The site of the proposed use is adequate in size and shape to accommodate the proposed use;

19        2. All setbacks, spaces, walls and fences, parking, loading, landscaping, and other features required by this
20        title are provided;

21        3.   The proposed use is compatible with neighborhood land use;

22        4. The site for the proposed use relates to streets and highways adequate in width and pavement type to carry
23        the quantity and kind of traffic generated by the proposed use;

24        5. The proposed use will have no substantial adverse effect on abutting property or the permitted use thereof;
25        and

26        6. In the case of residential uses, the housing density of the development is consistent with the existing
27        zoning densities, or the general plan, and that all other aspects of the development are consistent with the
28        public health, safety, and general welfare for the development and for adjacent properties.

29        (Amended: Ord. 2018-10-02; Ord. 2024-03-02)

30   D.   Approval Criteria – Special Uses.

31      When the following uses are allowed subject to review and approval (R/A) the responsible official shall review
32   them subject to the applicable standards and criteria in Chapter 40.260:

33        1.   Bed and breakfast establishments (Section 40.260.050);

34        2.   Home businesses – Type II (Section 40.260.100);

35        3.   Kennels (Section 40.260.110);

 4   40.520.080 Planned Unit Development

 5   A. Purpose.
 6       The intent of planned unit developments (PUD) is to allow flexibility in design and creative site planning,
 7       and in some cases density, while providing for the orderly development of the county that meets the
 8       comprehensive plan. Planned unit developments should allow for a mix of housing types, lot sizes, and uses.
 9       The legislature through the Growth Management Act (RCW 36.70A.090 and RCW 36.70A.020(4)) and the
10       county in its comprehensive plan (Chapter 2.7.1) encourages a creative approach to affordable and
11       diversified housing. Council finds and concludes that planned unit developments are an important
12       development alternative to meet the needs of home buyers by providing a variety of lot sizes and housing
13       styles. Furthermore, the PUD code reduces the restrictive impact of critical area ordinances. Therefore,
14       Council concludes that a PUD implements GMA and the comprehensive plan and that applicants need not
15       demonstrate a change of circumstances.

16   B. Applicability.
17       1.     Planned unit developments are permitted pursuant to the provisions of this section within the
18              following districts: R1-5, R1-6, R1-7.5, R1-10, R1-20, R-12, R-18, R-22, R-30, R-43, OR-15,
19              OR-18, OR-22, OR-30, OR-43, R-24, R-36, R-50, OR-24, OR-36, OR-50,CR-1, CR-2, NC, CC,
20              CL, GC and MX districts.

21       2.     Uses Permitted.
22              Any use consistent with the zone districts designated for the parcel(s) within the proposed planned
23              unit development boundary may be permitted in planned unit developments. The location of the uses
24              in planned unit developments may vary from underlying zoning; provided, that the total allowed uses
25              (e.g., number of residential uses or area assigned to commercial use) was limited by the maximum
26              allowed on each respectively zoned parcel. Applications for PUDs shall be reviewed using a Type II-
27              A process unless submitted with a subdivision, then it shall be reviewed using a Type III process.

28   C. Design Flexibility.
29       1.     Design Flexibility. Zoning standards that may be varied without the need of a variance include, but
30              are not limited to, lot standards, setbacks, landscaping, and parking. Design flexibility for
31              transportation, stormwater, critical area, and other nonland-use-related standards may be reviewed
32              separately through other review processes such as a road modification or stormwater variance.

33       2.     Site Size. A PUD shall be located on a minimum site size of three (3) acres unless the responsible
34              official finds that the site of the proposed use is adequate in size and shape to accommodate the
35              proposed use and all setbacks, parking, loading, landscape/screening, and other features as required
36              by this title.
37        3.      Building Height. Maximum building height shall be that of the underlying zone; provided, that
38                the maximum height may be exceeded if the minimum setback of the higher portion of the
39                building is increased at a one-to-one (1:1) ratio (excess height in feet: extra setback in feet).

40        4.      Open Space. All PUDs shall provide a minimum of twelve percent (12%) open space based on
41                the net site area for active or passive recreational purposes:

42                a.   Active recreational areas include features such as jogging trails, child play equipment,
43                     open fields for pick-up games, game courts, swimming pools, club houses, picnic areas



                                                                                                                      231
 1                     and pavilions.

 2               b.    Passive recreational areas include features such as natural protected areas and open space
 3                     with features like access trails, benches, interpretive signs and view corridors.

 4               c.    Stormwater facilities may be counted as open space only if they are not fenced, and
 5                     include features such as natural areas, water gardens and habitat, and are incorporated
 6                     into the overall open space design.

 7               d.    The open space shall be consistent with the character of the PUD, considering its size,
 8                     density, expected population, topography, and the number and type of dwellings.

 9        5.     All open space shall be conveyed to and permanently maintained by a home owners
10               association unless a public agency agrees to maintain the open space and any structures or
11               improvements located on it. Prior to final plat/site plan approval, the association of owners
12               will be created under the laws of the state and shall adopt and propose articles of incorporation
13               or association and bylaws, and covenants, conditions and restrictions limiting the uses of the
14               open space shall be adopted and approved by the Prosecuting Attorney.

15        6.     All PUDs shall provide street trees on public and private roads as well as street lighting, except
16               street lighting will not be required on functional classifications of arterial, collector, and scenic
17               route roadways. One (1) street tree shall be provided on an average of twenty-four (24) linear
18               feet, species chosen from the Clark County Standard Details Manual or specified by a certified
19               landscape architect. Street trees on private roads shall be conveyed and permanently
20               maintained by the home owners association.

21   D. Approval Criteria.
22        Prior to preliminary approval of the proposed PUD, the Hearing Examiner or the responsible official must find
23        that the following three (3) conditions exist:

24        1.     Alternate designs proposed will provide a plan equal or superior to the standard being varied.

25        2.     Through lot size, setbacks, building orientation, and screening, the proposed PUD shall
26               provide a gradual transition adjacent to lower density neighborhoods or nonresidential uses.

27         3.     The applicant proposes design features that may include, but are not limited to, designs centered on
28                protected natural areas, front porches/recessed garages, pedestrian-friendly orientation,
29                benches/gazebos, water features, recreational areas, stormwater systems designed as features, and
30                affordable housing.

31   E.   Residential Density Bonus.
32        A density bonus may be granted for those PUDs that provide design concepts that will enhance the
33        livability of the proposed development and surrounding area. Each of the following design concepts may
34        result in a three percent (3%) density increase, with a maximum density increase of ten percent (10%):

35        1.      Variety of housing types and densities.

36        2.      A mix of uses.

37        3.      Design that reduces automobile trips and encourages alternative modes of transportation.

38        4.      Alley vehicle access only for at least fifty percent (50%) of the units.

39        5.      PUDs that contain more than fifteen percent (15%) open space based on the net site area.



                                                                                                                         232
1        6.      Low impact developments (LID).

2   F.   Open Space for LIDs.
3        LIDs that provide lot sizes equal to or greater than that required by the zoning district shall be exempt from
4        the open space requirements in subsection (C)(4) of this section.
5        (Amended Ord. 2006-05-01; Ord. 2010-08-06; Ord. 2019-05-07; Ord. 2024-06-04;Ord. 2024-06-04)

2   40.610.020 Definitions
3   For the purposes of this chapter, the following definitions apply:

         Applicable development        “Applicable development” means any subdivision or short platting of land,
                                       the construction or reconstruction of residential, commercial, industrial,
                                       public or any other building or building space, or the change in use of a
                                       building or building space, or the change in use of a building or land if
                                       approval therefor is required pursuant to Chapter 14.05 (International
                                       Building Code), Chapter 14.32A (Mobile Home Permits), Chapter 40.540
                                       (Boundary Line Adjustments and Land Division Ordinance) or Chapter
                                       Section 40.520.040 (Site Plan Review) and such land subdivision,
                                       construction or change in use would result in an increase in average daily
                                       traffic or residential dwelling units.
                                       (Amended: Ord. 2006-09-13)
         Building permit               “Building permit” means the permit required for new construction and
                                       additions pursuant to Chapter 14.05 or Chapter 14.32A of the Clark County
                                       Code. The term building permit, as used in this chapter, shall not be
                                       deemed to include:
                                       1. Permits required for the remodeling, rehabilitation, or other
                                       improvements to an existing structure or rebuilding a damaged or
                                       destroyed structure; provided, that there is no increase in the applicable
                                       unit of measure (for nonresidential construction) or number of dwelling
                                       units (for residential construction) resulting therefrom;

                                       2. Permits required for temporary dwellings approved pursuant to Section
                                       40.260.210;

                                       3. Permits required for placement of a mobile home within an approved
                                       mobile home park.

                                       (Amended: Ord. 2006-09-13)
         Capital facilities plan       “Capital facilities plan” means the capital facilities plan element of the
                                       Clark County comprehensive plan as amended. For the purposes of the
                                       traffic impact fee program, the capital facilities plan also means Appendix
                                       A: Transportation Capital Facility Plan. includes the traffic impact fee
                                       program technical document. In the event of an inconsistency, the traffic
                                       impact fee technical impact document shall govern.
         Development approval          “Development approval authority” means the county official or tribunal
         authority                     having statutory or code authority to approve a development.
         Impact fee                    “Impact fee” means the fee levied pursuant to this chapter as a condition of
                                       issuance of a building permit or development approval.
         Low-income housing            “Low-income housing” means a single-family or multifamily rental
                                       housing development, the construction of which is either undertaken by a
                                       housing authority operating pursuant to Chapter 35.82 RCW or financially
                                       assisted pursuant to a federal, state or local governmental low-income




                                                                                                                 234
                                 housing program; provided, that the term shall apply only to the number of
                                 units within such housing development as are required to be rented to low-
                                 income tenants.
     Overlay area                “Overlay area” means a special geographic area designated in a capital
                                 facilities plan to be served by a system improvement, which area is not
                                 generally contiguous with an established service area. An overlay area may
                                 be local (covering only a portion of a single service area), regional
                                 (covering portions or all of several service areas, or countywide (covering
                                 both incorporated and unincorporated areas).
     Project improvements        “Project improvements” means site improvements and facilities that are
                                 planned and designed to provide service for a particular development
                                 project and that are necessary for the use and convenience of the occupants
                                 or users of the project, and are not system improvements. No improvement
                                 or facility included in the capital facilities plan shall be considered a
                                 project improvement.
     Public facilities           “Public facilities” means the following capital facilities owned or operated
                                 by government entities:
                                     1. Public streets and roads;

                                     2.   Parks, open space and recreation facilities;

                                     3. Schools; and

                                     4. Fire District facilities.

     Schools                     “Schools” means and includes any primary or secondary public school
                                 operated by a school district whose boundaries include unincorporated
                                 areas of Clark County.
     Service area                “Service area” means a geographic area described in the county capital
                                 facilities plan in which a defined set of public facilities provides service to
                                 development within the area; provided, that the service area for schools
                                 shall be the applicable school district. Service areas may be separately
                                 described for each type of public facility.
     System improvements         “System improvements” means public facilities that are included in the
                                 capital facilities plan and are designed to provide service to service areas
                                 within the community at large, in contrast to project improvements.

 2   40.620.010 Traffic Impact Fee Formula
 3   The impact fee component for roads shall be calculated using the following formula:

 4   TIF = (size of development by unit of measure/unit of measure) x (daily trips per unit of measure) x (1 - pass-by
 5   factor) x (BEF) x (0.85) x (fee per daily trip by district)

 6   A. “Unit of measure” means the associated trip generation rates and other factors by land use type that have been
 7   provided in the capital facilities plan Traffic Impact Fee Technical Document and supplemented by the current
 8   Institute of Transportation Engineers Trip Generation Manual along with the Institute of Transportation Engineers
 9   Trip Generation Handbook. The rates shown in the Traffic Impact Fee Technical Document are most commonly
10   used and are based on the Institute of Transportation Engineers Trip Generation Manual and Trip Generation
11   Handbook.

12   B. “Pass-by factor” means trips that are made as intermediate stops on the way from an origin to a primary trip
13   destination without a route diversion. The pass-by factor can be obtained in the Institute of Transportation
14   Engineers Trip Generation Manual.

15   C. “BEF” means business enhancement factor; a multiplier of 0.70 used to reduce TIF payment for retail and
16   service-related businesses only. Retail and service related businesses have shorter average trip lengths, reducing
17   impacts on the existing transportation infrastructure.

18   D. Adjustment of 0.85 is applied for the portion of anticipated additional tax revenues resulting from a
19   development which is pro-ratable to system improvements contained in the capital facilities plan.

20       (Amended: Ord. 2016-06-12; Ord. 2023-01-08)

 2   40.630.010 Impact Fee Revision
 3   A. Impact fee rates shall be adjusted periodically to reflect changes in costs of land acquisition and construction,
 4   facility plan projects and anticipated growth.

 5   B. Except as provided in subsection C of this section, Such any adjustments to an impact fee rate shall only
 6   become effective upon adoption by Council of a modification to the capital facilities plan;.

 7   C. provided, that the A capital facilities plan may contain a provision authorizing for an automatic revision
 8   adjustment of an impact fee rate, no more often than annually once per calendar year, to reflect the change in a
 9   generally recognized and applicable inflation/deflation index. These automatic adjustments will be based on the
10   applicable index’s January issue and take affect March 1. Adjustments made pursuant to such automatic provisions
11   shall not require additional Council adoption.

12   D. Impact fee rates shall not be increased for more than three consecutive years without requiring Council review
13   and approval.
14       (Amended: Ord. 2009-11-06; Ord. 2016-06-12; Ord. 2019-05-07)

 2   40.630.040       Collection of Impact Fee
 3   A. The impact fee imposed under this chapter shall be due and payable at the time of issuance of a building
 4   permit (or site plan approval when no building permit is required) for the development.*

 5   B. For the transportation impact fee, the developer has the option to pay in a lump sum, without interest, or by
 6   installment with reasonable interest over a period of five years. The county will require security for the obligation
 7   to pay the transportation impact fee, in the form of a recorded agreement and lien, a deed of trust, a letter of credit,
 8   or other instrument determined satisfactory by the development approval authority.

 9   C. Impact fees shall continue to be collected for facilities or system improvements until their construction is
10   substantially complete, and the estimated private share has been completely paid and fulfilled.

11       (Amended: Res. 2009-06-07; Ord. 2012-05-25)

12   *   Code reviser’s note: Sections 2 and 3 of Resolution 2009-06-07 provide:
13     “Section 2. Developers of plats meeting the conditions in Section 1 of Resolution No. 2009-04-11, adopted on
14   April 28th, 2009, developing in every school district except the Vancouver School District, may pay the school
15   impact fees in accordance with Section 3 of this Resolution, instead of paying the entire recalculated school impact
16   fees prior to the issuance of building permits as is otherwise required under CCC 40.630.040. Developers in the
17   Vancouver School District shall pay school impact fees prior to the issuance of a building permit.
18      Section 3. School impact fees may be paid in two installments, provided the developer signs and records a school
19   impact fee assessment in a form that is acceptable to the county and school district attorneys prior to making the
20   first installment payment. The first installment payment shall be paid prior to the issuance of a building permit and
21   shall consist of no less than the school impact fee amount that was calculated at the time of preliminary plat, short
22   plat or site plan approval. The second installment (or all installments if the parcel is sold prior to building permit
23   issuance) shall be paid prior to transferring title to the property (at closing), as reflected in the recorded school
24   impact fee assessment. The person signing the school impact fee assessment is ultimately responsible for ensuring
25   the total impact fee is paid.”

 1   EXHIBIT 36


 2   40.630.060        Impact Fee Credits
 3   A. The developer shall be entitled to a credit against the applicable impact fee component for dedication of land
 4   for, and reasonable documented construction acceptable to the county engineer associated with the improvement to,
 5   or new construction of, any system improvements provided by the developer (or the developer’s predecessor in
 6   interest), to qualifying facilities that are identified in the capital facilities plan in place at the time impact fees are
 7   calculated or recalculated as set forth in Section 40.610.040 and Section 40.630.030. Such dedication or
 8   construction must be required by the county as a condition of approval for the immediate development proposal and
 9   the system improvement must be provided in its entirety, as described in the capital facilities plan. Credits shall be
10   issued at the time impact fees are imposed as set forth in Section 40.610.040.

11   B. For the traffic component of the impact fee, credits shall be based upon estimated costs set forth in the capital
12   facilities plan. Credit shall be calculated by multiplying the proportion of the total system improvement being
13   provided by the developer times the estimated cost of such system improvement in the capital facilities plan;
14   provided, that the County Engineer may adjust the credit to account for extraordinary cut, fill or structural costs
15   which are reflected in the plan estimate.

16   C. Additionally, the developer may be provided a credit against the impact fee in an amount up to ten percent
17   (10%) of the traffic component thereof for the value of mass transit facilities that are approved by the county and
18   made a condition of approval for the development.

19   D. Where impact fees are owing prior to completion of a system improvement undertaken by the developer, the
20   impact fee shall be reduced by eighty-five percent (85%) of the allowable credit up to the amount of the impact fee
21   for the subject development when the system improvements have been assured by a bond or other guarantee to be
22   completed no later than the date of occupancy for commercial/industrial/multifamily structures or the final building
23   inspection for single-family and other uses. Upon completion of the required system improvement, the remainder of
24   the credits will be issued.

25   E. Credits recognized by the county may be utilized in lieu of cash payment of impact fees for the subject
26   development and/or any other development within the same service area; provided, that PIF credits shall first be
27   applied to offset impact fees for the subject development.

28   F. In the Highway 99 Overlay district, the developer may be eligible for credits or trip reductions as set forth in
29   the transportation capital facilities plan Traffic Impact Fee Technical Document.

                                              y Space should be oriented to receive sunlight, facing east, west, or
                                                (preferably) south, when possible.
                                              y Required setbacks, landscaping, driveways, parking, or other vehicular
                                                use areas shall not be counted toward the common space requirement.

                                                                                 B. Vehicular Access and Parking


B.4 Parking
Intent
 To provide flexibility in how developments accommodate parking.
 To maintain active pedestrian environments along streets by placing parking
  lots primarily in back of buildings.
 To ensure safety of users of parking areas, increase convenience to
  businesses, and reduce the impact of parking lots wherever possible.
 To physically and visually integrate parking garages with other uses.
 To reduce the overall impact of parking garages when they are located in
  proximity to the designated pedestrian environment.

Standards
See Section A.2 (Building Location and Orientation) for standards involving
parking lot location.
B.4.1 Parking spaces shall provided consistent with 40.340.010(A)(5).
      Exceptions:

       a. Multi-family dwelling – studio unit: 1 space/dwelling unit.

       b. Assisted care facility, senior: 1 space/each 3 units.

       c. Senior housing: 1 space/dwelling unit

B.4.2 Tandem parking (one car behind the other) may be used for all housing
      types, provided the spaces are identified for the exclusive use of a
      designated dwelling unit.
B.4.3 On-street parking spaces adjacent to uses shall count towards off-street
      parking requirements.

          they are integrated into the architecture of the building. For example,
          balconies could be tied to changes in the roofline. Individual
          balconies shall be at least 35 square feet in size with no dimension
          less than 4 feet to qualify for this option. Simple balconies that are
          attached onto a square building and not integrated into the
          architecture of the building are not acceptable.

Section G:                                      G.1 Subdivision Design and Site Layout
Single-Family/                                  Intent
Duplex                                           To ensure that single-family developments are compact, pedestrian friendly,
                                                  and contribute to the character the surrounding neighborhood.
Developments                                     Create variety and interest in the appearance of residential streets.
                                                 To integrate open space with single-family developments.
                                                 Protect significant features of the natural environment.
Note: Single-Family uses are no longer
permitted in the Mixed-Use zone. This section
applies only to duplexes.                       Standards
                                                G.1.1 Development of “Neighborhoods.” New detached single-family/duplex
                                                      subdivisions shall be designed to integrate with the larger mixed-use
                                                      development and with surrounding properties and neighborhoods.
                                                      Subdivisions shall be designed so that individual, separately developed
                                                      projects work together to create distinct neighborhoods, instead of
                                                      disjointed or isolated enclaves. To accomplish this, such developments
                                                      shall comply with the following standards:
                                                         y A.1.4 regarding open space.
                                                         y B.1.3 regarding cross circulation.
                                                         y B.2.2 regarding street design.
                                                         y G.2 regarding lot and building design.
